# The claims this document makes, one per line: id, file, status, sentence.
# id is C and four digits, assigned in order of writing and never reused or renumbered.
# file is relative to end-goal/. status is built, unbuilt Mn (a roadmap.md milestone heading), or stated.
# sentence is one sentence, or one table row, of the file's Markdown source, verbatim; whitespace is normalized when matched.
# factory/cmd/tracecheck reads this file. It holds no reasons: a contested claim is argued in the commit.
C0001	components.md	unbuilt M14	[_Tight integration_](what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) states the rule this file completes: every record in the graph has one writer, the single component that writes it.
C0002	components.md	unbuilt M14	A component does not exist until it has a row here, and a call edge does not exist until the row of the component making the call names it.

C0017	components.md	unbuilt M14	| the pass over the [constraints](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) in force and the pass over the [advisory](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) feed | two of the [detectors](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) _Intake_ names, beside the health monitor reading a service, a [consumer](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md), and an objective, the deployer recording a criterion's crossing, and the pass over the deprecation list | intake, once per condition that matches |

C0019	components.md	unbuilt M14	| [the drift detector](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) | one process the owner installs beside the factory, comparing what runs against what the deploy record says and the log's chain against the head it recorded in a store of its own | no component, which is why the notifier reads that store for itself; one address the owner wrote into its store, for [the delivery](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) it makes when the notifier or the whole factory has stopped |

C0021	components.md	unbuilt M14	| the resolver [seam 3](deferred.md) names | what turns a credential's name into its value, for the models and the deploy targets alike | nothing |
C0022	components.md	unbuilt M14	| [the install's first-start step](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) | what runs at the factory's first start on a fresh install, again at an upgrade's first start whenever what shipped changed, and at the factory's first start after its records are restored from a backup; it writes the [fleet proposal](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) covering every role, at install and at an upgrade's first start that added a stage | the artifact store, to enter a shipped [role prompt, skill, or selection rule version](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) that changed, and to write a [consumer contract derived again](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md) for each release in force on a toolchain whose [extractor](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) changed; intake, to write a changed shipped constraint's [withdrawal-and-arrival pair](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md), a shipped design system excepted, which moves only when an owner picks the new version; the log, for the [install event](deferred.md) it writes at every upgrade and every restore, and for a shipped [score version](how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) or [policy version](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) it appends at install and at an upgrade's first start |
C0023	components.md	unbuilt M14	| [Work, Ops, Factory and People](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) | the four screens, one client the factory builds and versions with itself | intake, from Work for an answer and an intent ended and from Ops for [a revert](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md); dispatch, from Work for the priority and an item ended, and from Ops to end a revert item that [a mark](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) made unnecessary; the notifier, from Ops where a human fires a page; the deployer, from Ops to perform or end a [mitigation](deferred.md) and to delete a [schema-change snapshot](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md); the gate component, from Factory at [a safeguard's withdrawal](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md), at [a halt's withdrawal](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md), at [a shortening of decision-log retention](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md), and at [a legal hold's ending](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md); the log, from Factory at each write an owner makes and from People at each write to the [People declaration](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) other than the mapping from a per-person key to a name; the report store, from Factory at a [redaction](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) and from People at a deletion of that mapping, for the [erasure list](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md)'s row |
C0024	components.md	unbuilt M14	**A component calls another and never waits on a call that waits on it.**
C0025	components.md	unbuilt M14	None of them closes a loop in what waits on what.
C0026	components.md	built	What returns to dispatch is a transition written onto the item, what returns to intake is a new intent, and what an [agent](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) authoring a stage gives the artifact store is a version.
C0027	components.md	unbuilt M14	Every loop the table above holds is of that kind, a record one component writes and another reads on a pass of its own.
C0028	components.md	unbuilt M14	A call edge that closes a loop of calls instead is a defect, because a component waiting on a component waiting on it is a stop no gate, [hold](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), or [window](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) can end, and this table is what makes such an edge visible before it is built.
C0029	components.md	unbuilt M14	An agent is not a component and has no row.
C0030	components.md	unbuilt M14	It is a model in a role the factory runs, put on an item by dispatch, and it calls as the stage it was dispatched to: the artifact store for the version it authors, dispatch for the transition it reports.
C0031	components.md	unbuilt M14	[The grouper](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) is an agent of that kind before an intent exists, and calls intake.
C0032	deferred.md	stated	One thing is sequenced away rather than answered: security.
C0033	deferred.md	stated	It is a decision about order, and what it has to satisfy is that waiting creates no requirement the factory cannot meet when it arrives — met by leaving fields populated in the meantime.
C0034	deferred.md	stated	Software the factory did not build was sequenced away here once and is not any longer: [_A service that already exists_](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) adopts one through [the pipeline](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), because a customer with software already running is the ordinary first customer rather than a later one.
C0035	deferred.md	stated	The factory should be free and easy to play with at the start and tighten as the human world demands it.
C0036	deferred.md	stated	It sequences the five seams below; it does not cover the product's own release channel, which is not deferred and is stated next.
C0037	deferred.md	built	**An actor on every record** — every [gate decision](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md), edit, approval, [undo of a shipped change](what-humans-do.md) (10).
C0038	deferred.md	built	No authentication, no enforcement, just the field, always populated.
C0039	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	From the first record the field holds an opaque per-person key and never a name, and the same is true of every other field that names a human on the log seam 2 keeps, a [page event](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md)'s recipient included.
C0040	deferred.md	built	Beside the actor, a second field populated from the first record says how the key was obtained: claimed, while nothing verifies a caller, and verified, once seam 5 does.
C0041	deferred.md	built	[_People_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) maps a key to a name outside the chain, so an erasure there deletes the mapping and leaves the chain, its links, and its counts standing.
C0042	deferred.md	built	**One append-only decision log, each record chaining its predecessor.**
C0043	deferred.md	built	It is the audit trail and the [risk score](how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/README.md)'s training data at once, and must not become two systems.
C0044	deferred.md	built	Append-only is a promise its writer makes about itself; the chain is what makes it evidence instead — one field, populated from the first record, because a chain laid over history written without one starts at the retrofit.
C0045	deferred.md	built	A second field cannot wait for the same reason: a payload format version on every row, because rows are never migrated.
C0046	deferred.md	built	A later factory version reading an early row is told which shape that row holds rather than inferring it from which fields are present.
C0047	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The drift detector of [_Drift detection_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) records the head in its own store each pass and verifies the chain still holds the head it recorded, extended and nothing else.
C0048	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A chain rewritten and recomputed then verifies clean inside the store and disagrees with the record outside it.
C0049	deferred.md	stated	That is evidence against the factory, not against the owner, who hosts the detector and its store alike — against the party holding every store, a self-hosted product anchors nothing, and what the log offers an auditor is the owner's records, not proof against the owner.
C0050	deferred.md	built	The [policy](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/README.md) and [score version](how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) a decision was made under are named on the row appended when the [gate](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) fires, for the reason the actor is there: what a decision was made under is not recoverable from a history recording only the decision.
C0051	deferred.md	built	A decision is two rows, the second closing the first, because a row is chained when appended and a verdict written onto a chained row afterwards is a rewrite — so those two versions sit on the open event alone, and the close event carries the verdict and its actor.
C0052	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The open event names the artifact version under decision by identifier and by the content digest [the artifact store](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) keeps on it, as [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) states, so the chain covers the text decided over and not the name alone.
C0053	deferred.md	built	One chain means one writer: every component that decides anything calls the log rather than writing into it, because five writers would be five implementations of the chain and five heads.
C0054	deferred.md	built	The log holds eight shapes, not one.
C0055	deferred.md	built	A shape is what a row's payload format version declares, and [_Records and their writers_](records.md) lists every row the log writes.
C0056	deferred.md	built	Every format version the writer accepts declares which of these shapes it serialises, and the writer refuses a row declaring no shape, so every row that list gives the log is one of these shapes or is not written.
C0057	deferred.md	built	The first is a decision.
C0058	deferred.md	built	The second is a [_page event_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md).
C0059	deferred.md	built	The third is a wait the factory could not compute when a gate fired, of which an unreachable credential and [the backlog cap's stop](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) are two; [_What a gate may change_](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) gives the pair of rows every wait takes.
C0060	deferred.md	built	The fourth is a [rework request](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md), the row written when an author sends its [item](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) back with no gate fired.
C0061	deferred.md	built	The fifth is [the merge queue's rejection](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), one row naming which of the queue's readings it was and the moved release where there is one; nothing ends it, so it is not a wait, and no gate fired, so it is not a decision.
C0062	deferred.md	built	The sixth is a truncation, appended when the log enforces its retention setting.
C0063	deferred.md	built	It names the actor who authored the value, the value it enforced, the boundary it cut to, and the policy version and score version in force at the cut, because a log shortened for storage and a log cut to remove evidence otherwise read the same.
C0064	deferred.md	built	The seventh and eighth are the two rows a decision is read against.
C0065	deferred.md	built	The seventh is the [policy version](what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md), appended at each owner write with [_Factory_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) as caller, and with [_People_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) as caller at a write to the People declaration other than the key-to-name mapping.
C0066	deferred.md	built	The eighth is the score version, appended by the score as its values move.
C0067	deferred.md	built	A decision names the policy version and score version it was decided under, and a truncation names the pair in force at the cut; the page event, the wait, the rework request, and the queue's rejection name neither, not being decisions.
C0068	deferred.md	built	The cut removes a version a decision still names, and the truncation row naming what was in force at the cut keeps that decision readable.
C0069	deferred.md	built	The score is told to ignore the page event, the wait, the truncation, and the queue's rejection the way it already ignores a [hold](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), there being no factor vector on any of them to learn against.
C0070	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	It reads a rework request only as an outcome on the author, the one [_Going back up_](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md) says the [per-author prior](how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) takes.
C0071	deferred.md	built	Within the decision it reads, it joins the two rows, since the [factor vector](how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) it learns against is on one and the outcome it learns from is on the other.
C0072	deferred.md	built	How long the log is kept is a setting an owner authors, stated with the rest of what they author in [_Gate policy_](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md).
C0073	deferred.md	built	Enforcing it removes the oldest records and never the head, so the detector's check reads through a truncation undisturbed.
C0074	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The exception is a cut that reached past the head the detector recorded, which happens when retention is shorter than the gap since the detector's last pass; that cut reads as a rewrite until a human weighs the truncation row.
C0075	deferred.md	built	That one field is a hash and not an identifier of the predecessor, because every property above turns on the chain covering content and not order alone.
C0076	deferred.md	built	Its value is a hash over the row's own payload and over the same field on the row appended before it, and the head is that field on the newest row.
C0077	deferred.md	built	The payload format version already on every row fixes both the canonical serialisation of the payload and the hash algorithm, so a row re-serialised by a later factory version hashes to what the earlier version wrote, and an upgrade reads as an extension rather than a rewrite.
C0078	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The drift detector recomputes forward from the head it recorded and compares, which is what makes an edit in place of any row's payload a mismatch instead of a chain that still verifies.
C0079	deferred.md	built	A ninth shape joins the eight: an **install event**, written by the log with [the install's first-start step](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) as caller, at every upgrade and at every start after the factory's records are restored from a backup.
C0080	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	It names the event, an upgrade or a restore, the actor, and which version of the factory shipped it.
C0081	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	At an upgrade it also names the changes the first-start step applied to the factory's own store and, where one of those changes was a removal, the snapshot taken before it, as [_One process_](one-process.md) states.
C0082	deferred.md	built	A tenth shape joins the nine: a **read event**, appended by the log when the log itself is read and when stored report text a [redaction](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) could reach is read — a report, its statement, or an artifact version quoting it.
C0083	deferred.md	built	It names the principal and what was asked for, never a screen opened.
C0084	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	[Artifacts](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) and [specs](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) contain names, never values — they get copied, diffed, and given to agents.
C0085	deferred.md	built	So does every credential the factory records: the one an agent reaches its own model through, [_The fleet_](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md) using this seam before any artifact does, and the one on an [_environment record_](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) that seam 4 resolves at every deploy.
C0086	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The credentials a service itself reaches the outside world with are a third kind: held by name in its configuration and resolved through the same resolver at deploy.
C0087	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A service's repository credential is a fourth kind, stated below.
C0088	deferred.md	built	One resolver answers all four, and it can be a file on disk today.
C0089	deferred.md	built	The repository pair is the one place reach is separated now, by the host and not by the resolver, at the cost of two credentials to provision per repository.
C0090	deferred.md	built	The repository credential is held by name on the [service record](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) and resolved by the same resolver.
C0091	deferred.md	built	It is two credentials where the repository host can tell master from a branch.
C0092	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	One is resolved to the implementation stage's run and to the build runner's clone, and it pushes a candidate branch and nothing to master; the other is resolved to the merge queue alone, and it fast-forwards master.
C0093	deferred.md	built	Which of the two shapes the host gave is what the owner records with [provisioned](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md).
C0094	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	On a host that cannot restrict by branch the exclusivity stays the convention [_What the factory does not build_](what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) states, detected after the fact by [the queue's reading](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md).
C0095	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	What a [stage](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) hands an agent is filtered against what this resolver returns for the item's reach before it is handed over, the rule [_One pipeline_](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) states.
C0096	deferred.md	built	However it is implemented, the [deployer](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), the component that performs every deploy, reaches a [deploy target](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) through a small set of named operations, and no agent reaches one at all.
C0097	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Deploying is not a [stage](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) an agent is dispatched to, so the actor on a [deploy record](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is the deployer and never an agent.
C0098	deferred.md	built	That seam is where policy attaches later; without it, production access is spread through the codebase.
C0099	deferred.md	built	What it attaches to on the other side is the deployer's own scope and the credential on the [_environment record_](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) it resolves, declared and enforced by nothing until this seam is built.
C0100	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	That no agent reaches a deploy target rests on [dispatch](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md): no [role](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) is a deploy, and dispatch matches an item's stage against the roles the factory defines, so no agent is ever put on one.
C0101	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Beside that ground now sits the operation list [_One pipeline_](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) puts on every role, the operations an agent in it may perform, which an owner may narrow on a fleet entry and never widen.
C0102	deferred.md	stated	The list is declared now the way this seam is and enforced with it: no sandbox, no egress rule, and no credential issued per [candidate](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md).
C0103	deferred.md	built	A **mitigation** is a named class at this seam with two operations and not three: shifting traffic off a target, and changing the instance count of a release the factory deployed.
C0104	deferred.md	stated	Restoring a release outside [_the analysis window_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) is not added — a [revert](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) once one exists is the answer there.
C0105	deferred.md	built	Ending every instance of a service on a target is a third operation, and [_Retirement_](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md) is what calls for it outside a rollout.
C0106	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	None of the three makes a build.
C0107	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	All act on releases the pipeline already verified, which is why one path holds: the second path the design refuses is a change reaching production without a gate, and a mitigation changes where a release runs and how many instances of it do, never what runs.
C0108	deferred.md	built	The deployer performs one on a human's instruction from [Ops](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) and writes a **mitigation record**, naming the actor under seam 1, the operation, the target, and the [deploy record](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) it modifies, which [the drift detector](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) reads as intended state.
C0109	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A mitigation stands until a human ends it at Ops, is shown there while it stands, and how long each has stood is a count on [Factory](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C0110	deferred.md	built	Seam 1 puts an actor on a record; this puts one on the call that reaches a seam, carried from the entrance to whatever the call reaches.
C0111	deferred.md	built	A human at a [screen](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) calls as the [_People_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) row they say they are; an agent calls as its [fleet entry](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md) plus the [dispatch](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) that put it on the [item](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), so the [_scope_](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) it was dispatched under travels with every call it makes; a component calls as itself.
C0112	deferred.md	built	The resolver of seam 3 and the operations of seam 4 take the principal on every call and record it beside what was asked for, deciding nothing on it — populated, self-asserted, enforced by nothing, the treatment seam 1 gets.
C0113	deferred.md	built	The resolver is addressed by credential name, so when an agent at the [Spec](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) stage in one [project](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) names another project's production deploy credential, the resolver answers it, there being no caller for a policy to read.
C0114	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The actor seam 1 records is whatever the caller supplied, so the caller a policy would need is missing there too, one record later.
C0115	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	With the principal on the call, verification attaches once, where the call enters the factory: a human authenticated at a screen, an agent's dispatch checked against its scope.
C0116	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Every call and record downstream inherits it, so seam 1's field becomes verifiable without a second retrofit.
C0117	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	[_Reports_](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) ships the way in inside every deployed service, and the way in calls as the deploy that placed it.
C0118	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	That one principal is checked from the first record rather than recorded now and enforced later.
C0119	deferred.md	built	At every deploy the deployer mints a token for the way in, hands it to the service in its configuration beside the service's own credentials, and writes a digest of it on the [deploy record](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md), never the token itself.
C0120	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The way in sends the token on every submission; the [report store](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) digests it, finds the deploy record carrying that digest, and takes the service and the environment from that record and from nothing the submission says.
C0121	deferred.md	built	Enforcement is a one-way field on the [factory-wide settings record](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md): off at install, and turned on once by the owner at [_Factory_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), chained as a policy version the way every owner write already is.
C0122	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	While it is off, [_People_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) and [_Factory_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) show every actor as claimed.
C0123	deferred.md	stated	A rule requiring the field on before any production environment record is created is refused: a one-person install runs to production on a claimed name by design, and the claimed-or-verified field on every actor already makes the trail say so.
C0124	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A [constraint](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) of the document kind, the kind a human enforces at a gate rather than a diff or a run, may require the field on; an item within its reach then waits at [dispatch](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), as an item already waits when no fleet entry covers its stage, and clears when the field turns on.
C0125	deferred.md	stated	[One pipeline](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) is not a sixth seam — it is the architecture the five attach to, chosen for coherence rather than safety: a single path is a single place to put policy.
C0126	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Nor is the bound [_Hazard severity_](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md) puts on an `irreversible` area's operation: it is enforced in the software the factory writes, at the point of the action, and recorded by the count that software emits, so what a release may do to the world is bounded where it is done and nothing has to attach there later.
C0127	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Nor is the process the build runs in: [_Records, and one long-lived branch_](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) gives that process nothing of the factory's from the first build, and a boundary enforced now is not a field waiting for enforcement.
C0128	deferred.md	built	What makes that work is that nothing routes around it, including incidents — the emergency action [_One pipeline_](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) allows is approve now, not skip, so a rushed yes is a yes with an actor on it and not a gap in the record.
C0129	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	What replaces the second path is mostly downstream of the deploy and reads what the factory wrote: the log for detection, the actor for attribution, and a human undoing a change after it shipped (10) — inside the [_analysis window_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) — for the undo.
C0130	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	[_Drift detection_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) is the one thing that disagrees from outside, over two facts — what is actually running against the record naming it, and the log's chain against the head it recorded — the whole of what a separate second path is worth.
C0131	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	[**The builder**](what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md) publishes a digest and a signature over each release of the product.
C0132	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Install and upgrade verify a release against both before the factory's first start on it; a verification that fails stops the start.
C0133	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Two keys verify it, and the product ships both.
C0134	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A **root key** the builder keeps offline signs the key that signs releases and signs nothing else but a revocation; the **release key** signs each release.
C0135	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	An install verifies the release against the release key and the release key against the root, and a release key the root has revoked verifies nothing from then on, so a release signed after a key compromise is refused wherever the revocation has reached.
C0136	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The revocation travels on the channel with every release after it and is read at every start, and it names the key and the last release the builder vouches for under it, so what verified before it stays verified and what came after does not.
C0137	deferred.md	stated	One link is left that nothing verifies, moved one key up: the first install trusts the root it was handed, and a compromised root is recoverable by no release, since every release would be verified against it.
C0138	deferred.md	stated	A key per kind of shipped content, one for prompts and one for design systems, is refused: every kind reaches every agent, so separate keys multiply what the builder keeps and narrow nothing a compromise reaches, and the [shipped-bundle identity](deferred.md#the-products-release-channel) below is what puts a substitution on the record.
C0139	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A first start refuses a release whose version is not later than the one the newest [install event](deferred.md#security-comes-last) names, so a signed release replayed onto an install that has passed it verifies and does not start.
C0140	deferred.md	stated	A release carries no expiry.
C0141	deferred.md	stated	An install may run indefinitely without the channel, because the regulated customer the [no-tenancy decision](what-the-factory-does/README.md) was made for is the one most likely to run that way, and a release that stopped past a date would stop that install first.
C0142	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The install reads the channel each pass for the newest release it offers and for a release the builder marked superseded for a defect, a mark the root signs the way it signs a revocation, and [Factory](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) shows the running release's age beside the newest offered and how long the channel has been unreachable where it is.
C0143	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A running release marked defective is a row on [the home view](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md), standing until an upgrade, the treatment an [advisory](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) against the product already gets.
C0144	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The channel keeps every release reachable for as long as an install can be upgraded from it.
C0145	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	[_One process_](one-process.md) supports no skipped version, so an install reaches the current release through every release between, one at a time, and a release it cannot fetch is one it can never pass.
C0146	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	Releases therefore leave the channel from the oldest forward and never from the middle: no release leaves while a release before it is still on the channel, and a release found defective is superseded by the one after it and never withdrawn.
C0147	deferred.md	stated	That is a requirement on the builder, the way a build's artifact staying fetchable is one [_Rollback_](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) puts on the host.
C0148	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A release carries more than code: the [role prompts](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md), [skills](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md), [evaluation sets](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md), [starting priors](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md), [the list of allowed predicate kinds](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md), the fixed defaults of the fields the [service record](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) authors outright, and [design systems](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) it ships with.
C0149	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A **shipped-bundle identity** is one digest over that whole set, naming which release it shipped in.
C0150	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	It is written on every [ungated entry](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) [the artifact store](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) writes at install and at an upgrade's first start, on every score version and policy version written at either event, and on the install event above.
C0151	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A release also carries the product's own [resolved set](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) and [notice file](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), and the coverage the set was resolved under, written by the builder from the build that produced the release in the shape a [build](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) record gives a service's.
C0152	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The shipped-bundle identity covers both.
C0153	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	The product is the one software in an install that no [service](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) record names, so the [advisory](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) detector matches the running release's set against the advisory feeds beside every service's current release, on the same pass and keyed by source the same way.
C0154	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A match raises no [intent](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), since no item can be cut for software the factory does not build.
C0155	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	It is a row on [the home view](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) naming the advisory and the release, standing until an upgrade's install event names a release whose set no longer matches or the feed withdraws it; past the [remediation period](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) it pages as any advisory does.
C0156	deferred.md	unbuilt M2	A release whose set could not be derived, or whose coverage leaves out something a watched feed speaks to, lists the product on [Factory](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) as unassessed beside the services.
C0157	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	An **item** is the unit the factory moves: one thing that can ship by itself, one [timeline](11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), one [release](06-releases/02-the-release-record.md).
C0158	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	An **agent** is a worker the factory runs — a model in a role, with a scope, working from a [_role prompt_](10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) the factory wrote.
C0159	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Two agents on the same model share one [per-author prior](04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md): the [score](04-risk-score/README.md) is kept per model, not per role or role prompt — which [_Paid for is not authored by_](10-fleet/04-paid-for-is-not-authored-by.md) settles, against the argument that would split it.
C0160	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	A **role** is what an agent is put on, a **scope** is where it may be put.
C0161	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	A role names one stage, a step of the path, and the artifact that stage writes about the item.
C0162	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	No role reaches a [deploy target](05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md): a deploy is performed by the [deployer](08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), the component that reaches [environments](05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), and is not a stage an agent is dispatched to.
C0163	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	An owner composing a [fleet entry](10-fleet/README.md) chooses among roles and never invents one.
C0164	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	It is not a duty: a human holds one of [the numbered twelve](../what-humans-do.md), an agent holds a role, and nothing about a role is scored, because the prior is the model's.
C0165	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	A scope is drawn on the same lines a [safeguard](09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) is — a [project](11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), an [area](../what-humans-do.md).
C0166	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	It limits which items an entry may be put on.
C0167	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Two roles are put on an intent rather than an item, because [the interview](02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) and [decomposition](02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) happen before there is an item to match against, and what scopes them is the [project the intent's request came in under](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) — an intent naming no [service](02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) and no area until decomposition writes them.
C0168	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	A third, [the grouper](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), is put on neither: it runs before an intent exists, scoped by the project whose reports it reads.
C0169	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	A fourth, the role that argues [a fleet proposal](10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md), is put on neither, and no scope narrows it: it argues over the whole fleet.
C0170	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	**Dispatch is a match, not a judgment.**
C0171	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	That is why [_Dispatch_](02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) needs no [gate](03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md): nothing there is decided that a later gate does not see, since everything the match produces is reviewed at one.
C0172	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	What runs after the match is a judgment: context assembly, named below, choosing what the agent reads.
C0173	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	Context assembly needs no gate of its own for a different reason: the rule it applies is decided at a gate, and the manifest it writes is read at the next.
C0174	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Dispatch is also what writes the field it matches on — [_What an item names_](02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) puts the item's stage and the attempts each stage has taken there, every stage reporting a transition to it.
C0175	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	What each stage spent is never a field of the item: it is on the [agent run record](10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) the run wrote, and that record names the item and the stage it served.
C0176	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Writing a transition somebody else reported is not a judgment either, and the component that reads the field on every move is the one that keeps it.
C0177	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Every call the agent makes carries the scope, in the principal that [seam 5](../deferred.md) puts on a call, so what stops it later has the call to read.
C0178	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	A role also carries the operations an agent in it may perform: the factory defines the list per role, and an owner never invents one.
C0179	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	A [fleet entry](10-fleet/README.md) reaches the list by naming the role, adding no field of its own beyond the role it already names, and an owner may narrow the list on an entry and never widen it.
C0180	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The list is declared now and enforced only when seam 5 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../deferred.md) is, like the other seams: until then nothing stops an agent performing an operation off its list, since there is no sandbox, no egress rule, and no credential issued per [candidate](06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md).
C0181	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	**What an agent reads is content, never instruction.**
C0182	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	An agent is told one thing and reads everything else.
C0183	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	What it is told is its [_role prompt_](10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md), and the [_skills_](10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) that match the item — records the factory writes, versioned, decided at a gate, and authored from the factory's own decisions rather than from anything that arrived from outside.
C0184	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Everything a stage hands it is material to work from instead — the [intent](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md)'s statement, the [reports](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) grouped into it, an owner's answer to an [interview](02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) question, the repository being changed, what a run printed.
C0185	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Where the item was sent back to this stage, the [reject](03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) or [rework request](03-gates/06-going-back-up.md) that sent it is material too, with its reason and the version it was decided over.
C0186	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	A reject's reason is a human's sentence or a check's name and reaches the agent as material like the rest, so an attempt re-authors against what was found wrong rather than blind, and the [attempt limit](03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) is spent on defects rather than on guesses.
C0187	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Text arriving through any of them that tells the agent to skip a stage, author something else, or reach past its scope is content that says so, and the agent reports it and continues with the stage it was dispatched onto.
C0188	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The line between the two is where the text came from and not what it says: a sentence in a role prompt is instruction because a gate decided it, and the same sentence in a report is content because nobody did.
C0189	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	Untrusted text reaches an agent by five paths, and what carries it differs by path.
C0190	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	The [report store](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) carries a stranger's sentence whole, authenticated by nothing, so the [grouper](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) and every stage below it read that sentence, and a report-grouped intent resolves the [source value](04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) toward the [Spec](03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) gate rather than being weighed.
C0191	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	The [advisory feed](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) carries a machine-readable match alone: package, range, identifier; its prose reaches the human at [_Work_](11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) and nobody else, so an advisory-raised intent is weighed rather than resolved.
C0192	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	An [adopted repository](02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) resolves the source value the same way, once, for the adoption intent alone; after that item merges, the repository is on [master](05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), admitted content like anything else the factory wrote.
C0193	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	Resolved dependency contents are not evidence of an intent at all: the [digests](05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) and the [security predicates](07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) are their check.
C0194	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	What a run prints is the item's own code's output, and it stays weighed like any other artifact: that is the residue this inventory leaves.
C0195	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	This is a property required of an agent rather than a check on the text — a check would have to decide what a sentence is trying to do, which is the thing the factory is asking a model to do in the first place.
C0196	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	That is a human's job, at the gate [_Reports_](02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) keeps at [Spec](03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) for every intent grouped from reports and the gate [_A service that already exists_](02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) keeps there for an adoption intent.
C0197	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	Stated prospectively: the material a stage hands an agent, checkout and run output alike, is filtered against the values [seam 3](../deferred.md)'s resolver returned for the item's reach (the credentials named by its service's configuration and by its [environment record](05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md)) before it is handed over; and a resolver-resolved value never enters agent-visible material by any other path.
C0198	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	**What did not fit is recorded.**
C0199	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Reading everything else is bounded by what the model an [entry runs on](10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) can read at once, which is a field of the entry and one the owner writes.
C0200	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	**Context assembly** is that component.
C0201	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	At every dispatch it selects, from the material the stage hands the agent, what the entry's model reads at once, under the [selection rule](10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) version in force, the newest one its gate approved, and it writes an **input manifest** before the agent starts.
C0202	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The selection rule is the third record of what an agent is told, versioned and decided at a gate as a role prompt is.
C0203	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The manifest names by reference and never by value everything that reached the agent: the intent, each report, each constraint, each artifact version, the commit and the paths of the repository read, the run outputs, and the reject or rework request the stage re-authors against.
C0204	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	It names the selection rule version applied, and what was excluded with the reason: the rule, or a class of material the [fleet entry](10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) does not name, which context assembly withholds before the rule selects.
C0205	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The artifact version names the manifest it was authored from.
C0206	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	The [score](04-risk-score/README.md) reads the manifest's exclusions the way it reads [a factor it cannot compute](04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md): resolved and not valued, a human deciding whatever the formula returns.
C0207	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	The decision's [factor vector](04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) names the manifest beside the role prompt and skill versions it already names.
C0208	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	An assembly that silently dropped material is caught by the manifest disagreeing with the item: the sources an item links are on the item, and a manifest omitting one of them, or naming one the item does not link, is a mismatch the gate reads.
C0209	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	**Authorship is per stage.**
C0210	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Every item goes through the pipeline.
C0211	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	A human-authored change, an AI-authored change, and one the two write together take the same stages, the same gates, and the same score.
C0212	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Authorship is an attribute of each stage, not a mode on the item: an item can have an AI [spec](03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md), a co-authored [plan](03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/03-implementation-plan.md), and a human implementation, and it is still one timeline.
C0213	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Three things can author a version of an artifact: the agent in the stage's role, a human [backstopping](../what-humans-do.md) that stage (11, 12), and the **gate component**, which fires every gate and is where a human takes [Edit in place](03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md).
C0214	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	So the writer of a version cannot be whichever of the three authored it.
C0215	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The **artifact store** is that writer, and the three are callers of it.
C0216	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	One more call authors nothing: at install, and at a first start on an upgrade that changed shipped words, the factory itself calls the store to enter what shipped, with the factory's own start as the actor.
C0217	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	**A version names its author.**
C0218	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The authorship attribute says which of the three wrote it; the author says which one — the model version for an agent, the person for a human at the stage or at a gate.
C0219	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Both are fields, because a per-author prior is computed from that author's own work and the attribute cannot answer whose work a version is.
C0220	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Two agents in different roles on one model are one author under two actors, which is the same fact _Item and agent_ states above, and it is why the author is the model version rather than the role or the [fleet entry](10-fleet/README.md) the agent ran as.
C0221	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The one exception is the entry nobody wrote — the shipped version the factory enters at install or at an upgrade's first start, per the call above — which carries the author and the authorship attribute both empty, the empty pair saying exactly that, and is read by no prior.
C0222	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	The entries install writes precede any gate and enter in force, a factory with nothing decided in it having to run; every entry an upgrade writes awaits its gate, the empty author resolved rather than weighed there — a human decides whatever the formula returns, as [_the score_](04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) states — and a chain the upgrade starts has nothing in force until that human does, only the work that would have used it waiting.
C0223	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	stated	Backstop duties (11, 12) are this and nothing more — a human authors one stage instead of an agent.
C0224	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Taking over is not leaving the factory.
C0225	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	A bug the factory finds and fixes itself is an item like any other.
C0226	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	It appears in [_Work_](11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), takes the same stages, and is [auto-passed](03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) only where the score allows.
C0227	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	There is no second, invisible path, and nothing ships unrecorded.
C0228	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	There is no bypass, including for incidents.
C0229	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	stated	A human at a gate is not a delay: the emergency action is approve now, not skip.
C0230	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	A change that should not have shipped is caught by the [_analysis window_](08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), not by a faster route around the pipeline.
C0231	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Waiting in [the merge queue](05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) is one place a queue forms, and its order is settable — an urgent item goes to the front.
C0232	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	built	Reordering a queue changes when an item reaches the gates; it does not change which gates it passes through.
C0233	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	[Numbered releases](06-releases/04-the-release-number.md) are the other queue, and it forms only where something is holding deploys: the service already holds as many windows open as its [window limit](08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) allows, its [error budget](08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) is exhausted, or a [rollback](06-releases/06-rollback.md) whose revert has not shipped is holding every deploy but that revert.
C0234	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	It also forms where a [declared dependency](02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) is not its service's [current release](06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md), where [_Drift detection_](08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) has found the service's record disagreeing with what runs, or where a human is holding at the [production deploy gate](03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md).
C0235	how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md	unbuilt M1	That queue's order is not settable: the release number orders builds, so they deploy to production in that order, [_the revert a rollback is waiting on_](08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) excepted.
C0236	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	Constraints (2) arrive here too and are not intents: they are never decomposed into items and never ship.
C0237	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	One record with a reach field rather than two record types — a constraint names what it binds, and the four values it may take are the ones duty 2 sets out in [_What humans do_](../../../what-humans-do.md), where the three widest make a constraint permanent and one intent is the narrowest, a constraint arriving with a single request and binding every item decomposed from it.
C0238	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	Intake writes it at arrival whichever [screen](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) an owner supplied it on, because the constraint's own rules — the actor, the chain — are implemented once rather than once per entrance, and a second write withdraws it.
C0239	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A permanent one is withdrawn at [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), which lists the constraints in force by reach, so ending one is done from that list rather than by finding the request it arrived with; one whose reach is a single intent is read and withdrawn at [_Work_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), on the intent it arrived with.
C0240	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	It is never edited: correcting one is a withdrawal and a second record, so what was in force when a [stage](../../01-one-pipeline.md) was authored is reconstructible from arrival and withdrawal alone.
C0241	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	What the reach decides is what the factory reads when it drafts, and drafting — a stage authoring one of an item's [artifacts](../../01-one-pipeline.md) — is per item: the constraints in force over the factory, over the item's service's project, over the item's [area](../03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) or any area above it, plus whatever arrived with this intent.
C0242	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	The interview reads less than that, and deliberately — the factory's own and the intent's own, and not the project's or the area's, because before decomposition which project the work is in is a guess and an intent's items may not share one.
C0243	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A constraint's other field is its **kind**, and it decides where a constraint of that kind is checked: three kinds reject at a [gate](../../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md), a fourth holds at dispatch instead, a fifth writes a factory parameter directly, and a sixth is shown to a reporter.
C0244	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A constraint stated as a diffable build is decided against the implementation and rejects at [_Implementation_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md).
C0245	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A licence policy is of that kind, not a document: the [build](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) names every package it resolved and the licence each declares, so a constraint over what may be depended on is diffed against that set and rejects there.
C0246	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A build whose set is **could not derive**, or whose [recorded coverage](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) leaves out what the constraint reaches, has nothing to diff, and the constraint then resolves a human to that gate the way an [unavailable factor](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) does, the [factor vector](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) naming what the resolver did not cover, rather than rejecting nothing.
C0247	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A predicate the factory can decide against an observed run is decided on [_the candidate environment_](../../05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md) beside the [acceptance criteria](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md), and rejects at the [Merge to master](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) gate on the same terms.
C0248	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	What the run had to show is not what it happened to show: the [screen state machines](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/04-the-screen-state-machine.md) in force for the build enumerate the states the predicates are decided over, and one the run did not render leaves them undecided and rejects there too, the reading and its cost stated where the run is decided.
C0249	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A document is decided by nobody, and what enforces it is a [safeguard](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) putting a human at a gate.
C0250	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	A document-kind constraint may also require [seam 5](../../../deferred.md#security-comes-last) enforced, read at [dispatch](../05-dispatch.md), where the item waits until the [factory-wide settings record](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) says it is.
C0251	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A licence policy is authored against a **product licence**, a field an owner writes on the [service record](../03-decomposition/README.md) the way they write a [deploy target](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), decomposition never writing it.
C0252	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	stated	Whether a resolved licence is compatible with the product licence is refused as a mechanical check, because which licences are compatible with which is legal judgment that differs by jurisdiction and by how the product is distributed, and the design keeps that judgment in the owner's constraint rather than deciding it for them.
C0253	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A package whose entry declares no licence rejects at [_Implementation_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) as a build-kind constraint the factory supplies rather than an owner authors, the shape the [security predicates](../../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) already take there, because nothing can be authored against an unknown.
C0254	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A fourth kind is decided at dispatch rather than at any gate: a predicate over the [processing location](../../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) a [fleet entry](../../10-fleet/README.md)'s credential resolves to.
C0255	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A constraint of that kind restricts which entries may take the items its reach covers, and [dispatch](../05-dispatch.md) matches an item against only an entry whose processing location satisfies the predicate.
C0256	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	An item with no conforming entry waits at dispatch the way an item whose stage matches no entry already does.
C0257	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	The match covers every run dispatch puts an entry on and not items alone.
C0258	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	[The grouper](02-reports.md) is matched under the constraints in force over the project whose reports it reads, and the two roles put on an intent under the factory's own and the intent's own, the set [_The interview_](../02-the-interview.md) reads.
C0259	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A report arriving under a project with no conforming grouper entry waits ungrouped, and dispatch writes the hold on the project rather than on an item.
C0260	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A fifth kind takes effect on arrival rather than rejecting or holding anything: intake, in the same write, calls [Factory](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) to set [the retention floor](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) on the [factory-wide settings record](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), rather than leaving anything to be read at drafting.
C0261	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	stated	A records-retention law is of this kind, its reach always the factory.
C0262	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A sixth kind is read by no drafting stage and rejects nothing: a **notice**, the text the [way in](02-reports.md) shows a reporter before anything is submitted, its reach one project.
C0263	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	stated	What a customer owes their end users at collection is law, per jurisdiction and per the customer's own use of the words, so the text is the owner's to author and never the factory's to write, the ground the product licence above stands on.
C0264	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	It is a constraint so that it is versioned the way every constraint is: never edited, replaced by a withdrawal and a second record, in force from arrival.
C0265	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	The [report store](02-reports.md) reads the record in force when the way in opens and the way in renders it, so a notice moves without the service building again, and a report names the notice record in force when it arrived, or that none was.
C0266	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	Where an owner authored none the way in shows none, and [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) lists each service under no notice beside its refused count, the treatment the channel's rates get where none is authored.
C0267	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A law is a constraint whose reach is the factory, and three things about a law are carried by neither the reach nor the kind, so a constraint carries three more fields, each optional and each read by something.
C0268	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	A constraint may name the date it binds from, and each date it names carries the time zone it is read in, as [_The screens as software_](../../11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md) requires of every authored calendar value.
C0269	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	Drafting reads it as in force from the start of that date in that zone and not from its arrival, and the conformance raise below runs at that instant, so a regulation phased in next year is supplied now and reaches shipped services then.
C0270	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	It may name a date it is to be reviewed by: past the end of that date in its zone, with no withdrawal and no replacement naming it, it is a row in [_Work_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) for whoever holds (2), delivered by mail and chat and never a page, nothing deployed being worse for a constraint nobody re-read.
C0271	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	The constraint goes on binding regardless, a review date ending nothing on its own, because a law that lapsed in the owner's diary has not lapsed.
C0272	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	And it may bind the factory's own conduct rather than its items: a period, and what starts it.
C0273	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	Conduct has no drafting moment for a constraint to be read at, so the constraint states the period and a trigger, and what the trigger reads is a record: an [advisory](03-advisories.md) matching a shipped build, an [incident](../../08-operations/06-incidents.md) on a service in the constraint's reach, or a human marking an intent at Work as meeting the trigger.
C0274	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	stated	The human is the only reader of a report's prose the design admits, the reports floor at [Spec](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) already putting one there.
C0275	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	The deadline is a field of the intent, written by intake when the trigger occurs: the trigger's own time plus the period, the time of a report's arrival and not of the mark, because the clock a law starts started then.
C0276	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	The period is counted in a time zone the constraint carries beside it, the jurisdiction's whose law it is, so a period in days ends at that jurisdiction's midnight and not the author's, and the deadline written is an instant in UTC like every other time.
C0277	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	It is the one field in the factory computed from a calendar, the [page hours](../../08-operations/07-pages.md) being authored and read against one but computing nothing.
C0278	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	It pages whoever holds (2) for that constraint at the moment it is written, which [_Pages_](../../08-operations/07-pages.md) admits as the one condition outside its own: the software is no worse, the customer is, for every hour of the wait.
C0279	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	The page is answered when the intent ends or the human marks the obligation met.
C0280	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	And Work shows the deadline on the intent and every item under it, so a human ordering a queue has it to order by.
C0281	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	It moves no priority itself, gates nothing, and teaches the [score](../../04-risk-score/README.md) nothing: an obligation is the owner's to meet and the factory's to make visible.
C0282	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A permanent constraint in force whose record no intent names, with shipped services inside its reach, is a [detector](README.md)'s condition.
C0283	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A detector reads the constraints in force on a pass of its own, the way the [health monitor](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) reads a service's numbers, and calls intake to raise one intent where the condition holds.
C0284	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	Each pass overwrites a [last check](../../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) of its own, carrying its interval, so a pass that stopped is a named row on [the home view](../../11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) rather than silence.
C0285	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	The evidence is the constraint record, which keys the raise like every detector's, and the condition stands until the intent exists, so a pass that stopped half-done raises nothing twice and finishes on the next one.
C0286	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	The condition is deliberately stricter than the unfinished-intent rule [_Intake_](README.md) states for a repeating detector: an intent that ended is not raised over again, whether it was delivered, [partly delivered](../04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md) or dropped, a second raise being the factory reopening work a human ended.
C0287	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	stated	The residue is stated rather than looped on: what a dropped conformance intent left out of conformance stays so until the owner asks again or withdraws the constraint.
C0288	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	The constraint is the intent's evidence, so the interview and every stage hold it through that link rather than through the project's list [_The interview_](../02-the-interview.md) does not read.
C0289	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	Decomposition is what enumerates the reach: an item goes to each service inside it that the work changes, and a service already conforming gets none, which is the floor decomposition already sets.
C0290	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	It may also find nothing departing at all, this intent alone being able to ask for a state that already holds, and then it yields no items.
C0291	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	The finding attaches to the intent, which waits at [_Work_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) until a human ends it as dropped — the end [_The interview_](../02-the-interview.md) gives an intent, deciding nothing was outstanding.
C0292	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	What an item can be checked against is what the kind already decides: a build is diffed, a predicate is decided against a run, and a document's items end at a safeguard (9) putting a human at a gate, like the rest of that kind.
C0293	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	Withdrawal ends the condition with the record.
C0294	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	A replacement names the record it replaces, and the pair is one write.
C0295	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	Where that record's intent has not finished, the replacement's raise attaches its evidence and sends the intent back the way a [rework request](../../03-gates/06-going-back-up.md) naming it does: unrefined again, the interview reopened, decomposition run again against what is now in force and superseding what it replaces.
C0296	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	built	An intent that is [escalated](../../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) or [re-decomposing](../03-decomposition/README.md) takes only the attachment, an escalation staying a human's to clear and an open Decomposition firing closing first, the send-back landing then.
C0297	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	The narrowest reach needs no raise: a constraint arriving with one intent binds what is decomposed from it, and nothing shipped precedes it.
C0298	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A factory-wide constraint on a mature install decomposes into conformance items across every service that departs from it, each holding an [environment](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) like any other item and ordinary in everything after: interviewed, decomposed, gated, counted in [cost per feature](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C0299	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	One thing is not ordinary: a conformance item's [tier](../02-the-interview.md) sits above every tier a requester or a detector proposes, so the law it answers to is never queued behind a feature the owner ranked higher.
C0300	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	stated	So an intent delivered means the reach was worked through and not that the constraint is met, and a departure that survives is found the way any shipped defect is.
C0301	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	A design system is not a record of its own: it is a constraint whose reach is one project and whose subject is what every screen of the product is built from, and its kind decides everything below.
C0302	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	It is the permanent constraint a project with a user interface always has, and it is chosen as much as supplied: the project uses one the factory ships with or one an owner uploads.
C0303	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md	unbuilt M8	One the factory ships with is content of the product rather than a record.
