C0675	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md	unbuilt M2	Dispatch sends such an item to the stage its own field names, and the version the interrupted run wrote stays on the record beside the second run's.
C0676	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	An [area](02-what-an-item-names.md) carries its **hazard severity**: the worst harm the software in it can do while it behaves as a release makes it behave, graded by what the factory can do about that harm afterwards.
C0677	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	It is one of three values.
C0678	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	`negligible` is an area whose software answers requests and writes its own store, and does nothing outside itself that outlasts a traffic shift.
C0679	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	`recoverable` is one whose software acts outside itself where a later [item](../../01-one-pipeline.md) corrects what it did, such as an amount posted wrong or a message published to a consumer that a compensating message retracts.
C0680	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	`irreversible` is one whose software does what nothing the factory ships afterwards corrects: money paid out, a message delivered to a person, data erased, an actuator driven, a disclosure made.
C0681	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	The value in force for an item is the highest named anywhere on its area chain up to the project.
C0682	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	It is authored outright with nothing supplied, for the reason the [_service level objective_](../../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) is: nothing the factory observes says what harm the software can do.
C0683	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	Where no area in a chain names one, the value in force is `negligible`.
C0684	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	unbuilt M1	[_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports how many declared areas name none, so an install reading every area as a marketing page is visible rather than silent.
C0685	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	At [_Implementation_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) an `irreversible` value is [resolved rather than weighed](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md), a human deciding whatever the formula returns.
C0686	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	unbuilt M1	The [_rollout strategy_](../../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) bounds how much of production such a release may take before the comparison has cleared any of it.
C0687	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	And the vector a gate firing writes names the value in force, so the impact half of the [score](../../04-risk-score/README.md) has one input a human can argue with rather than a term nothing supplies.
C0688	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	An area graded `irreversible` also names its **hazardous operation**: the call in the software that does what nothing afterwards corrects, the payout, the send, the erasure, the actuator call.
C0689	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	Beside the name the owner authors a **bound**: the count of that operation the service may perform per period, the period authored with the count.
C0690	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	built	The grade is not written without the two; the write at Factory refuses it.
C0691	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	stated	A second grade for magnitude, kept apart from recoverability the way MIL-STD-882 and ISO 26262 keep severity apart from controllability, is refused.
C0692	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	unbuilt M1	At [_Spec_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md) the bound is a criterion the factory derives, its provenance [hazard-derived](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md) and naming the area: once the count of the operation in the current period has reached the bound, the service refuses the operation and emits a [failure record](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) naming it.
C0693	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	unbuilt M1	The count is kept in the service's own store, so every instance on every target reads one count.
C0694	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	unbuilt M1	The software also emits a count of the operation per interval, which [_The health monitor_](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) keeps beside the three quantities and the [window](../../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) reads as a fourth.
C0695	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	unbuilt M1	A release performing the operation at a rate its control does not is a crossing, and `passed` is not reached until that rate is ruled unchanged, which on an operation performed rarely is never inside the cap.
C0696	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md	stated	What the field does not reach is the [rollback](../../06-releases/06-rollback.md), which restores code and no effect, and a harm that moves no count: a wrong amount paid at the usual rate is inside the bound and flat against the control, and only the human at Implementation and the rollout schedule stand between it and production.
C0697	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	A service ends the way it began, by an owner's write on its record, and nothing else ends one.
C0698	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	The owner writes **retired** at [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), and the write is refused while a [consumer contract](../../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) in force names the service, while an unmerged [item](../../01-one-pipeline.md) names it, or while an unmerged item's declared dependency points at it.
C0699	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	unbuilt M7	What depends on the service is moved off it first, through the migration [_Deprecation_](../../07-contracts/08-deprecation.md) sets out, so a retirement never orphans a consumer and never strands work in progress.
C0700	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	The write calls [the deployer](../../08-operations/09-the-deployer.md), which performs a **removal**: it ends every instance of the service on every target of every persistent [environment](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), through the operation of seam 4 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../../deferred.md) that ends instances, and writes a [deploy record](../../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) per environment naming no release, complete per target as the instances there end.
C0701	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	[Current release](../../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is read from the newest complete deploy record, and a removal's names none, so a retired service has no current release.
C0702	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	unbuilt M7	A declared dependency on it would hold at both deploy gates, which none can by the refusal above, and a [candidate environment](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) composed for a dependant is composed with nothing for it.
C0703	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	unbuilt M7	[The drift detector](../../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) reads the removal as intended state, so an instance still running on a target is a mismatch.
C0704	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	unbuilt M7	[The health monitor](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), the [advisory](../01-intake/03-advisories.md) detector, and the pass over [the constraints in force](../01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) skip a service with no current release, each recording on its [last check](../../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) that no further pass is owed.
C0705	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	unbuilt M7	Every record stays, its retention following [the log's](../../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) the way every record's does, and the repository and the store are the owner's to remove, as creating them was.
C0706	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	A retired service is never revived: software the owner brings back is adopted as [a service that already exists](01-a-service-that-already-exists.md).
C0707	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	A persistent environment other than production ends by an owner's withdrawal at Factory, refused while any deploy record on it marks a target complete for a release: the owner has the deployer remove each service from it first, the same removal performed for that one environment, called from Factory.
C0708	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	built	A [project](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) is ended at Factory once every service in it is retired, and its production environment ends with it.
C0709	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md	unbuilt M7	A target leaves an environment's field the same way: the owner removes the address, refused while any service's deploy record marks that target complete for a release, so the deployer's removal on that one target comes first, and the [window](../../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md)'s target set and the drift detector's reading follow the field.
C0710	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	One intent becomes one item or several, and an item names exactly one service.
C0711	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	None is the outcome only for the conformance intent [_Constraints and the design system_](../01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) raises, whose decomposition may find nothing departing, attach that finding, and write no item, no gate firing over an empty set.
C0712	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	How many go to one service is a floor and not a cap: a service the work changes gets at least one item, and gets more where one item would be larger than decomposition is aiming at, or where a change cannot ship as one.
C0713	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Decomposition is where [_No single item may break a contract_](../../07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md) is applied rather than discovered: "an item that cannot ship by itself was decomposed wrong" is a statement about this stage.
C0714	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A migration with one consumer is four items over two services, three of them on the producer: one adds the new form, a second runs the [brownout](../../07-contracts/08-deprecation.md) of the old one, and a third removes it.
C0715	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	The factory raises the last two itself, the way [_Deprecation_](../../07-contracts/08-deprecation.md) sets out, the brownout when the [derived consumer contracts](../../07-contracts/08-deprecation.md) naming the old form are gone and the removal on the brownout's evidence; decomposition does not write them ahead of conditions nobody can schedule.
C0716	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A service the work changes may not exist yet, and nothing about decomposition changes: one item creates it, and whatever calls it is a second item declared to wait on the first.
C0717	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Decomposition writes the service record in the same write as that item, because an item names one service and the record has to exist for the item's only outbound link to point at anything.
C0718	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The [project](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) the service is in is a field of that record and part of its identity, written in the same write from [the project intake wrote on the intent](../01-intake/README.md), and no later write moves it.
C0719	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Its other writer is an owner at [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), putting the [window limit](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) and the [analysis window](../../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md)'s parameters on it (8), and the seam between the two is the field: decomposition writes the service's identity, its project among it, and never a parameter, an owner writes parameters and never creates a service.
C0720	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A service is one repository with one long-lived branch inside it, and no repository holds two: [master](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), [the release number](../../06-releases/04-the-release-number.md), and the queue's ordering are all per service, and two services sharing a repository would give it two divergent trunks, so a build of either would be made from a tree that contains the other's stale code.
C0721	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Master itself does not exist until the first release: the [implementation role](../../01-one-pipeline.md) commits the [candidate branch](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) with no base, and the queue's first fast-forward is what creates master.
C0722	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Nothing writes the service's place in an environment either — a [deploy record](../../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is keyed by service and environment, so a service is in an environment by having been deployed there.
C0723	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	stated	Decomposition refuses to create a service record for a deliverable [_What the factory does not build_](../../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) refuses, the same five kinds and the same reason: no service record and no item are written for it.
C0724	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	So the service record carries one more owner-written field, **provisioned**, written at [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) once the repository exists with the factory's credentials holding write access to it and the service's store exists on every persistent environment.
C0725	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The field also records which shape the repository host gave those credentials, the two seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../../deferred.md) names, master restricted to the merge queue's, or one credential on a host that cannot restrict by branch.
C0726	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Decomposition never writes it, which keeps the seam above.
C0727	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	An item on a service not provisioned is stopped where the factory would first reach for what is missing: [dispatch](../05-dispatch.md) does not dispatch its implementation stage, and both deploy gates hold on it as they hold on a declared dependency that is not current.
C0728	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	The stop at dispatch is [a wait no gate could compute](../../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), written into the log naming the service, so the row [_Work_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) shows says a repository or a store is missing rather than that a stage failed.
C0729	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A wait with no holder widens to the owner, which is where a [substrate](../../../what-humans-do.md) obligation already goes.
C0730	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A further four fields are the deployer's, not the owner's or decomposition's: a target it reaches, instances the platform can replace, a rollback path, and an [emission](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/02-the-encoding-and-the-emission.md) the [health monitor](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) can read.
C0731	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The deployer populates them at adoption and at every first release, and no one authors them the way a parameter is authored.
C0732	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	They are distinct from provisioned: provisioned says the repository and the store exist, and these four say what runs on them can be reached, replaced, undone, and read.
C0733	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The set of non-production values the service's configuration takes on a candidate is also owner-written on the service record, beside what [the candidate's store starts with](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md), and the deployer writes it onto the candidate environment when it [composes one](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md).
C0734	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	So is the [mutant cap](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md), authored with a fixed default and bounding how many mutants [Implementation](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md)'s mutation score may spend per item.
C0735	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A fixed default is a value the product ships, among [what a release carries](../../../deferred.md), read wherever the field stands unauthored; this document names none of the numbers, and a reader of a decision taken under one reaches it through the release that shipped it.
C0736	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Another field on the service record, authored the same way as the mutant cap, is the cap on how many distinct keys a release may hold open per interval for its [failure records](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md): a fixed default, and a safeguard (9) may lower it and never raise it.
C0737	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A third, authored the same way, is the bound above which a criterion is [unreliable](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md): a fixed default, and a safeguard (9) may raise it and never lower it, lowering being what takes a criterion out of the gate.
C0738	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A fourth, authored the same way, is a bound on how long an [incident](../../08-operations/06-incidents.md)-raised item may be worked before a human is reached: a fixed default, on the service record beside [the objective](../../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) and the hours a service pages within.
C0739	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Passing it [pages](../../08-operations/07-pages.md), stated there on the same ground as the other clocks.
C0740	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A fifth field, authored the same way, is the [product licence](../01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) the service's software ships under: the owner writes it and decomposition never does, so a licence policy constraint has it to author against.
C0741	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Shipping by itself is a minimum and not a size — it admits an item that touches forty files and one that changes a string.
C0742	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	What sizes an item between them is a target the score supplies, which an owner authors, or safeguards per area (9), with the rest of gate policy (8).
C0743	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	It learns from what a bad size does in each direction, both among the numbers [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports: too coarse shows as attempt-limit escalations (12), with everything spent on the item thrown away, and too fine shows as cost per feature and rework rate, a whole pipeline for a one-line change.
C0744	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	That number reaches model spend and nothing the host bills: the environment and fleet a release keeps are hosting costs, which the factory does not read and the spend ceiling does not reach.
C0745	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	The minimums do not move with the target: it ships by itself, and no single item may break a contract, which is four items for an interface and five for a store, however small the target.
C0746	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	What the target is not is a definition: an item is not one feature.
C0747	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Factory computes cost per feature as what the intent and its items spent, which already makes a feature an intent.
C0748	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The target's unit is the count of its intent's [requirements](../02-the-interview.md) an item answers — the field decomposition already writes per item.
C0749	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	So the target binds a quantity already on the record at the moment decomposition aims, and still there when [the Decomposition gate](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md) decides the set.
C0750	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Decomposition also records the order.
C0751	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Where one item cannot be verified until another has shipped — the producing release of a migration — that dependency is declared here, and both deploy gates hold on it: [_Deploy to candidate environment_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/06-deploy-to-candidate-environment.md) until the dependency is live, [_Deploy to production_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) if it has stopped being.
C0752	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Ordering is a property of decomposition, not something discovered at deploy time.
C0753	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The declared order is checked against the whole relation and not against the set.
C0754	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	What waits on what is a graph over every unmerged item, since a re-decomposition repoints edges that leave the set, and the invariant is that the graph is acyclic.
C0755	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A write that would leave a cycle in the graph is refused where the items are kept, which is what reaches a decomposition yielding one item, whose gate never fires; decomposition is the relation's only writer, so a check there sees every edge there is.
C0756	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	And a set whose dependencies form a cycle is rejected at [the Decomposition gate](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md) mechanically, the way a build that does not compile is at [_Implementation_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md), so a wrong order comes back as feedback rather than as a stage that could not write.
C0757	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The relation the two guards check is the union of what decomposition declares and what a [rollback hold](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) imposes, not the declared edges alone.
C0758	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	While a rollback hold stands on a service, every unmerged item of that service other than the revert waits on the [revert item](../../06-releases/06-rollback.md), and that wait is an edge of the graph, recomputed at each firing the way the hold is and held by no record.
C0759	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Decomposition reads those edges at every write from what the production deploy gate reads the hold from, and refuses a write that would close a cycle through one, naming the hold in the refusal.
C0760	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A re-decomposition also repoints what stands: an item left standing that waited on one now superseded waits on the replacements, written by decomposition in the same write that creates them, which is the inverse of the pointer a superseded item already carries.
C0761	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	An item left waiting on one replaced by nothing has that wait resolved in the set the gate decides, what waits on what being part of what is approved.
C0762	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	What the set answers is checked at the same gate and as mechanically.
C0763	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Decomposition names, per [requirement](../02-the-interview.md) of the intent, the item that answers it; a requirement is one statement of the reading the requester confirmed.
C0764	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A requirement one item answers alone is assigned to it whole.
C0765	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	One the split spreads over several items is assigned to none of them: decomposition derives one requirement per item, stating that item's share in the requester's terms, and each is a requirement record of its own, attached to the intent, pointing at the one it was derived from, and written by intake at decomposition's call.
C0766	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	The derived requirements are part of the set the gate decides, beside what waits on what.
C0767	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A requirement is answered by the item assigned it, or by the items answering every requirement derived from it, so the whole the requester confirmed is covered exactly where each share is.
C0768	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A set that leaves a requirement named by no item and derived into none is rejected there, and so is one that leaves a derived requirement named by no item.
C0769	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	And so is one with an item that answers no requirement, whole or derived — work nobody asked for, whose criteria at Spec would trace to nothing.
C0770	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Every item decomposition writes therefore answers a requirement whole or carries a derived share of one, the item that creates a service and each step of a migration included.
C0771	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	[_Spec_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md)'s per-item check reads the derived requirement where there is one, and a re-decomposition supersedes a derived requirement with the item that carried it, the pointer [_The interview_](../02-the-interview.md) gives a superseded reading.
C0772	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A requirement decomposition judges unanswerable is named by no item and marked with a tagged reason, intake writing the mark as it writes the record, which is the treatment [_Spec_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) already gives a sentence fitting no pattern.
C0773	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Decomposition has a gate of its own, named for the stage and scored like every other, and it fires where the stage yielded more than one item.
C0774	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	What is approved is the set: how many items, which service each changes, and what waits on what.
C0775	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A rejection re-decomposes the set rather than sending one item back, because what the gate decides on is the decomposition and not an item, and each round counts against the attempt limit on the intent rather than on an item — the items of a rejected round are superseded and their replacements start at nothing, so a count kept on them would never reach a limit however many rounds were spent.
C0776	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	It is the parameter the interview already counts its rounds against, one stage later and on the same record, in a field of its own beside the rounds and never the same field: the two are different stretches of work, an owner answering an escalated interview clears that count alone, and one field would spend an interview's rounds out of decomposition's budget.
C0777	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Intake writes it, called by decomposition, which is the one write decomposition makes to an intent rather than to items.
C0778	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	While rounds remain, a human re-decomposes by hand with [Edit in place](../../03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md), the gate still deciding and the set still editable.
C0779	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	An intent that exceeds the limit gets **escalated** written on it and appears in Work as an escalation (12) — the second of the two things that write that value, and the two are told apart by which of the intent's counts exceeded the limit rather than by the value itself.
C0780	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Decomposition also runs again on an intent in progress, which is what three other sections send a problem to: a group [_Reports_](../01-intake/02-reports.md) says the grouper preferred to make, a spec that comes back too large, and a change [_No single item may break a contract_](../../07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md) says cannot ship as one.
C0781	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A [rework request](../../03-gates/06-going-back-up.md) naming decomposition re-decomposes the set, as does a gate's reject naming it, and what either produces is decided at this gate whatever it yields — the one-item condition is the first decomposition's, and a re-decomposition of work in progress always fires, because what it decides is which existing items are superseded rather than how many are proposed.
C0782	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The intent goes to re-decomposing and every unmerged item of it stops while that firing is open, the way they stop while the interview is: without it a sibling merges between the rework request and the verdict, and the set approved would supersede a release.
C0783	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	What stops them is the intent's state, which [_The interview_](../02-the-interview.md) makes a precondition on the gate component and the merge queue too and not on [_Dispatch_](../05-dispatch.md) alone: an item already approved at Merge to master merges with no agent on it.
C0784	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	The item that sent it back stops with them, its stage unchanged and its attempt booked on the intent, so it resumes where it was if the re-decomposition does not replace it.
C0785	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	The items it replaces are superseded and point at their replacements, the record shape a rejected round already uses; what differs is that an item in progress has spent artifacts, a build, and an environment, and superseding it throws all of them away.
C0786	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A merged item is out of reach, since a rework request may be raised no later than the merge to master, so the re-decomposition leaves shipped work alone and declares the new set's order against it, which is what [_When an intent's items do not all ship_](../04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md) already says about the siblings that got there first.
C0787	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Each round counts against the same re-decomposition count, so a set that keeps being re-decomposed escalates the intent rather than dividing forever, and its items stop there rather than resuming.
C0788	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	They appear in Work under it until a human takes one over (12) or drops them, the factory not going on to build a decomposition it has just said it cannot make.
C0789	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	One verdict covers the whole decomposition, however many services it changes.
C0790	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	A holder who wants to decide at decomposition for their own service gets that with a safeguard (9) on their area, which is what a safeguard does everywhere else.
C0791	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Decomposition writes the items, and the gate decides over records that already exist.
C0792	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	A rejected set is superseded rather than discarded.
C0793	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	Each rejected item points at the items of the re-decomposition that replaced it — a list, since a re-decomposition may replace four items with two — written by decomposition in the same write that creates the replacements, so what was decomposed wrong is readable beside what replaced it rather than only in the decision's feedback.
C0794	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	Where a re-decomposition replaced an item with nothing the pointer stays unwritten, and what says why is the item's superseded stage beside the decision that rejected the set.
C0795	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	unbuilt M1	The Spec gate cannot do this job — it is per item, so approving one item's spec never ratifies the decomposition that produced four.
C0796	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md	built	So the gate fires where there is a set to ratify and nowhere else: a decomposition that yields one item produced none, and putting a gate in front of every intent, most of them single-item, would be a decision about nothing.
C0797	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	built	Nothing writes it down: an intent is partly delivered when at least one of its items stopped without reaching production and at least one sibling is live.
C0798	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	unbuilt M1	An item is live when a production [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) names its release, as the one it deployed or in the list of releases a [revert](../06-releases/06-rollback.md)'s deploy delivered, and marks it complete on every production target — and not before.
C0799	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	built	An intent whose items are all still moving is in progress rather than partly delivered, one none of whose items shipped is stopped, and one whose superseded items were replaced by a re-decomposition is judged on the replacements.
C0800	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	built	It is not terminal either: a human taking over an escalated item (12) can finish it, and the intent stops being partly delivered with no event anywhere, which is why a field would have to be unwritten and is not a field.
C0801	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	built	So the reading is only as cheap as those inbound edges are indexed, and an item merely held at the production deploy gate never makes its intent one of these however long it waits.
C0802	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	unbuilt M1	The shipped ones stay shipped.
C0803	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	unbuilt M1	Undoing the whole thing is one revert item per shipped sibling, joined by the same intent, and never a single undo.
C0804	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	unbuilt M1	That value is written where every item is live and the person who asked confirmed what shipped, so an intent standing here reaches no acceptance round and nobody is asked whether the half that shipped was worth having.
C0805	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md	unbuilt M1	An item that fails stops only its dependents: whatever it depended on shipped before it, and whatever depended on it was still held at a deploy gate.
C0806	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	With the decomposition approved, the factory puts its own agents onto the items — a model in a role with a scope, as [_One pipeline_](../01-one-pipeline.md) has it.
C0807	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	Dispatch gets no gate: it decides who authors, and everything authored is reviewed at a gate anyway, where the score reads the [per-author prior](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) of the model that wrote it.
C0808	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	Where the intent's state and an unexpired claim do not already stop it, six things stop a dispatch, each a [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) in the [decision log](../../deferred.md) rather than a timeline that stops.
C0809	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	They are a stage no [fleet entry](../10-fleet/README.md) covers, a stage whose role has no [role prompt version in force](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md), [a credential already known unreachable](../10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md), [a credential at its spend ceiling](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md), a [constraint](01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) of the document kind requiring [seam 5](../../deferred.md#security-comes-last) enforced on an item within its reach, and a constraint of the dispatch-decided kind whose predicate no entry's processing location satisfies.
C0810	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	Dispatch writes one row per item and stage for each of the six, with itself as the caller and the actor; for a run on no item it writes one per intent and role, or one per project for [the grouper](01-intake/02-reports.md), under whichever of the six conditions reaches a run of that kind.
C0811	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	The row names which condition held and the values the match was made on: the role, the item's [area chain](03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) and its service's [project](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) where no entry's scope covered them, the credential name where that was the cause, and the constraint named where a document-kind constraint was the cause.
C0812	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	Where the dispatch-decided kind was the cause, the row also names the processing location no entry's credential satisfied.
C0813	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	The account exhausted part-way through a stage, and a [spend ceiling](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) reached part-way through one, are each a row of its own, written by whoever could not reach or proceed, and dispatch's rows are about the stages that never started, so neither stands for the other and a credential twelve waiting items share is twelve rows and not one.
C0814	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	Dispatch re-matches its open holds when a record able to clear one arrives: an owner writing a fleet entry at [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), [the gate a version fires](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) putting one in force, a credential reachable again, an owner clearing a ceiling, or the seam 5 field turning on.
C0815	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	It then writes the second row of every hold the match lifts, the mid-stage one it did not open included, so no hold outlives its condition and none is left for a component that has stopped to close.
C0816	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	No [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md) fires and no attempt counts, for the reason a hold neither pages nor counts: nothing deployed is worse for it, and no agent worked.
C0817	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	What limits how many items move at once is infrastructure, an environment per candidate, and that cost is the factory's rather than a human's.
C0818	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	Where more is ready than that infrastructure admits, dispatch orders it by [tier](02-the-interview.md) first, an item's [priority](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) breaking a tie within one.
C0819	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	What a tier orders is admission, never a bound already reached: the spend ceiling, [the platform's own room](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/06-deploy-to-candidate-environment.md), and [the backlog cap](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) each stay a stop no tier lifts, the same as a hold above.
C0820	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	Where a human authors a stage instead of an agent (11, 12), nothing about dispatch changes: authorship is an attribute of the stage, and the item is still one timeline.
C0821	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	built	Dispatch is report-driven — [_What an item names_](03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) has every stage reporting a transition to it — and an agent can stop without reporting anything: its host restarts, its process dies, its provider connection hangs.
C0822	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	So a dispatch is a claim that expires unless the agent renews it, renewal being activity rather than progress, and a claim that expires unrenewed is itself the report: dispatch sends the item back to the stage to be entered again, the way an agent's failed attempt does, so the count rises on re-entry and enough silent stops walk the item to an [escalation](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) (12) instead of leaving a timeline that never advances and never escalates.
C0823	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	A stage with an unexpired claim is matched to nobody, which is what keeps a slow agent and its replacement from authoring one stage at once.
C0824	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	A claim on a stage with a hold open against it is the one expiry that is not that report: dispatch releases the claim, counts nothing, and leaves the item on the hold, because an agent that stopped when its credential could not be reached renews nothing and a hold is not a failed attempt — without that, every hold written mid-stage would walk its item to an escalation at the expiry rate.
C0825	how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md	unbuilt M7	The [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) screen is where an owner watches this: [_the fleet_](../10-fleet/README.md), what each agent is doing, and how well.
C0826	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	There is a gate at every [stage](../01-one-pipeline.md) boundary: after [decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) into [items](../01-one-pipeline.md), after the spec, implementation plan, tasks, and implementation are authored, and before each merge and each deploy.
C0827	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Two kinds: an **artifact gate** decides over a document a stage has written, and an **event gate** decides whether an event — a merge, a deploy — happens at all, which is what makes [hold](04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) a stop rather than an undo.
C0828	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	stated	The mechanism is permanent — it does not weaken as the factory improves.
C0829	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The factory scores each change and auto-passes what it judges low risk.
C0830	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Two things reach a gate from the score, not one: the number against the [risk threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md), and the [factor vector](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) it was computed from — a vector recording any factor [resolved rather than valued](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) is decided by a human whatever the number is, so no threshold an owner authors and no formula the score publishes can auto-pass it.
C0831	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The same [score](../04-risk-score/README.md) picks the [_rollout strategy_](02-the-rollout-strategy.md).
C0832	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	Humans override by adding a safeguard — a gate held always-on, or a strategy that keeps a [control](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) — and can undo a change after it shipped.
C0833	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A gate **firing** is one gate deciding one thing at one moment, and it writes one **decision** into the append-only log seam 2 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../deferred.md) describes, which is that log's single [writer](../../what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) with [the gate component](../01-one-pipeline.md) as its caller.
C0834	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	What fires it is the item arriving at the row: at an artifact gate, [the artifact store](../01-one-pipeline.md) writing the version the stage submits; at an event gate, the event it decides becoming next, which needs the row above approved and, at a merge, the [candidate](../06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md)'s run ended.
C0835	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	A gate fires again on the same item and row for four reasons and no fifth: [Edit in place](03-actions-at-each-gate.md), below; a row re-fired to another holder of its duty, below; a refer, below; and a [hold](04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) a human set, the row firing again when that human or another holder of the row's duty releases it at [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C0836	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The gate component fires no row on an item while an open event on that row is pending, the one exception being the open event Edit in place appends naming the row it supersedes, so one gate on one item has at most one pending row, and the rows one item writes are bounded by its arrivals and those four.
C0837	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A hold the factory sets holds the open row rather than firing the gate again, which _What a gate may change_ sets out.
C0838	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A decision is two rows and not one: an **open event** appended when the gate fires, and a second row appended when the decision ends, naming the row it ends.
C0839	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	That second row is a **close event** where a **verdict** is given: approve, reject, hold, or refer.
C0840	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A row is chained to its predecessor when it is appended, so a verdict written onto the open event afterwards would be a rewrite of a chained record.
C0841	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	The [_deploy record_](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) advances in place instead, and that is not a counterexample — it is an ordinary record rather than a row of the log, and nothing chains it.
C0842	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Opened, it names the gate row, the item, the [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) where one exists, the [artifact version](../01-one-pipeline.md) under decision at an artifact gate, the vector and the number, the [policy version](../../what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md) and the [score version](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md), the values actually applied, and the [duty](../../what-humans-do.md) or named human the row waits on.
C0843	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	[Edit in place](03-actions-at-each-gate.md) authors a new version through the [artifact store](../01-one-pipeline.md) while the row is still waiting, so a verdict naming only the item would point at whatever the artifact says when it is read rather than at what was decided over.
C0844	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The version is named by its identifier and by its content digest, a hash the [artifact store](../01-one-pipeline.md) computes over the version's text when it writes it and keeps on the version; the row at [_A role prompt or a skill_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) names the version under decision the same way.
C0845	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	At Decomposition it names the [intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) and the set decomposition proposed rather than one item, that row being the one gate above a [timeline](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) rather than on it.
C0846	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	At [_A role prompt or a skill_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) it names the version under decision and no item at all, that row belonging to no timeline, no [service](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), and no build.
C0847	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The close event names the open event, the verdict, its actor, when that actor opened the row where the actor is a human, and where the verdict sends an item back [what it returns to](06-going-back-up.md).
C0848	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	It names neither the policy version nor the score version: what a decision was made under is a fact of the firing, so it is written once where the firing is and two rows cannot come to disagree about it.
C0849	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	Both are written against the item and the build and never against the [release](../06-releases/02-the-release-record.md), including at the production deploy gate, which fires after the release exists — one rule for all eight rows, so no reader has to know which side of the merge a gate is on.
C0850	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	On a reject or a hold the close event also carries the reason: the text a human wrote, which is the feedback the action's name promises, or the check the gate component rejected on where the reject was mechanical.
C0851	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The log's writer refuses a reject carrying none, so no item is sent back with nothing saying why.
C0852	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A decision that will never receive a verdict is ended in the log rather than left open.
C0853	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Its second row is an **abandonment**: it names the open event and why no verdict is coming, with the component that ended the decision as caller and actor, and it carries no verdict, a close event being reserved for one.
C0854	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Three things write one.
C0855	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	An item stopped at [_the attempt limit_](05-the-attempt-limit.md) leaves a row nobody is deciding, ended by the component that writes the escalation onto the item.
C0856	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	An item superseded by a [re-decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) or dropped ends its open rows with it, written by the component that wrote that value onto the item.
C0857	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	And [Edit in place](03-actions-at-each-gate.md) while the row waits re-fires the gate rather than leaving the verdict to bind to a version the human rejected by editing it.
C0858	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Authoring the new version appends a new open event naming the version and the row it supersedes, with a vector computed from what is now under decision, and an abandonment ends the superseded row.
C0859	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A decision may carry a third kind of row between its open event and the row that ends it.
C0860	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	An **acknowledgement** names the open event and a human, and is appended when a holder of the row's duty says at [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) that they have the row, the gate component calling for it as it calls for a close.
C0861	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	It decides nothing, ends no wait, and excludes nobody: the row stays pending, stays in Work for every holder, and the first verdict still decides.
C0862	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	An open event admits one acknowledgement per human and any number of humans, and a second from the same human is refused as a second close is.
C0863	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Where the row also pages, one act at Work writes both, the gate component calling [the notifier](../08-operations/07-pages.md) for the page's acknowledged event as the component that ends a wait calls it.
C0864	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	[Factory](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reads them beside the close event.
C0865	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	The rows a human acknowledged and another holder decided are reported per named human beside the rows each decided, so time a shared duty spent is charged to everyone who took the row and not only to whoever closed it.
C0866	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	And the wait on a shared duty is split at the first acknowledgement, the way [_The page channel, and what reached a human_](../11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md) splits it at the first accepted delivery: before it the wait is the duty's, after it a human's.
C0867	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A human put at a row may be unable to judge what they were shown, and **refer** is the verdict that says so: not a fault found, which is a reject, and not a stop on the event, which is a hold, but a reading the instrument could not take.
C0868	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A referred row re-fires to another holder of the row's duty who has not referred it, and to the owner where none is left, the re-firing an [Edit in place](03-actions-at-each-gate.md) already takes.
C0869	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A refer with nobody left to refer to is refused, the screen saying so, and what that human has left is a reject whose feedback says what they could not read: a diff too large to read is a fault of the artifact and not of the reader.
C0870	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A refer counts no attempt, sends nothing back, and teaches the score nothing: the [pair Factory reports](../11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md) counts approvals and their undos, and a refer is neither a gate the factory needed nor a human agreeing.
C0871	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	The log's writer refuses five closes and nothing else.
C0872	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A reject carrying no reason is refused, as above.
C0873	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	An open event admits exactly one close event, so a second close naming one already closed is refused, and where [a duty is held by more than one human](../08-operations/07-pages.md) the first verdict decides and a later one does not.
C0874	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A close naming an open event an abandonment has ended is refused, so no verdict binds to a version its human edited away or to an item that stopped.
C0875	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A refer with no holder left to refer to is refused, as above.
C0876	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	And a close event whose actor is the author named on the artifact version its open event names is refused wherever another holder of the row's duty exists, the row re-firing to that holder rather than closing to its own author.
C0877	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	That is the rule the gate component applies at Decomposition, Spec, Implementation plan, Tasks, and [_A role prompt or a skill_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md); [_A safeguard's withdrawal_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md) already applies it to one row alone.
C0878	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Where no second holder exists, the row still fires to the editor and the close event carries a self-approval field, which [Factory](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports per human beside what each approved and how often it was later undone; an [install](../../what-the-factory-does/README.md) with one owner is allowed, and its trail says what it is.
C0879	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	Who holds the row's duty is read from the [People](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) declaration at the close, and every write to that declaration appends a [policy version](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), so the newest one before the close event in the chain is what the refusal was evaluated against, and an auditor re-reads it there rather than trusting the field.
C0880	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	Each refusal is a state the screen shows: the row already decided, already ended, or waiting on a different holder, with what caused it, never an error about nothing.
C0881	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A pending gate is then an open event with neither a close event nor an abandonment, a predicate over the log alone that needs no join against the item's stage.
C0882	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A gate fires only where the item's intent permits it.
C0883	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	[The intent's state](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) is read before the firing, and no row of an item whose intent is unrefined, re-decomposing, escalated, or dropped is fired at all: no open event is appended, so nothing is decided, no attempt is counted, and the score learns nothing.
C0884	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	The vector is shown while the human decides and the number only beside the verdict once it is written: [_Implementation_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) derives an encoding's expected behaviour from the criterion's sentence and never from the code, because a reader of the code agrees with it, and a reader shown the score's conclusion first agrees with that.
C0885	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	What put them there is a mark on the row and never the absence of one, which is what makes it readable without the number.
C0886	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A vector naming a resolved factor says the number decided nothing.
C0887	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	built	A [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) among the values actually applied says an owner put them there whatever the number was.

C0904	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	A failing rollout rolls back on its own, inside its [_analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) — no human involved, no waiting.

C0908	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	It narrows that way and no other: with one item per release, a change is never harder to undo because it shipped alongside nine others.
C0909	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md	unbuilt M1	What overlapping analysis windows add is not difficulty but reach — a rollback undoes every release above its target, up to the [window limit](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md): the one set of items the factory ships together.
C0910	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	A **rollout strategy** is how a release takes live traffic from the build it replaces.
C0911	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	It attaches to a production deploy and to no other, which is narrower than [master](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md)-fed: what a strategy decides is whether a control runs, a control is the instances a comparison is made against, and that comparison exists only in production because **organic traffic** — the requests real users are already making — does.
C0912	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	The schedule is an attribute of the first row rather than a strategy of its own.
C0913	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	They differ in how much traffic the release is exposed to and not in what is provisioned: on every schedule the build being replaced keeps the instances it had until the [window](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) that could return to it closes, because those instances are what a rollback shifts production onto and a share is not a capacity.
C0914	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	Both rows replace instances, and neither drops a request doing it: the operation among the named ones of seam 4 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../deferred.md) that replaces an instance stops new requests reaching it and lets the ones it holds finish before it ends, and the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) advances a target only when that operation reports it done.
C0915	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	stated	What neither row offers is a switch inside the build — code shipped dark and turned on later.
C0916	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	A change turned on by a flag is a change that took no gate, reached no environment as the thing it is, and opened no [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) when it started serving, which is the second path [_One pipeline_](../01-one-pipeline.md) refuses; so the only correction after a window closes is a pipeline pass, behind [a queue whose order is not settable](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) after the merge.
C0917	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	A [configuration value](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) is a repository file, so takes that path.
C0918	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	How much of production a release may take before the comparison has cleared any of it is bounded by the [hazard severity](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md) of the item's area, which is the second thing deciding a schedule.
C0919	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	At `irreversible` the score may pick only the schedule that widens as the comparison stays clear.
C0920	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	The one that switches all of production at once is not available and neither is a fixed share, both of them moving the rest of the traffic in one step rather than as a reading clears it.
C0921	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	Otherwise the [window's cap](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) is the only thing bounding how much traffic is served before anybody reads a result, and on a quiet service the cap is hours.
C0922	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	Where the platform serves no share there is no schedule to pick, every deploy there going without a control, so an `irreversible` area's [deploy to production](07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) is a human's whatever the formula returns, and what that human accepts is an exposure the platform gives the factory no way to limit.
C0923	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	A schedule is a share of traffic inside one target, and a production environment has [targets](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), plural, so the rollout has a second structure over them.
C0924	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	The deployer reaches the targets in the order the environment record lists them, one at a time, on either row.
C0925	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	A target is not reached until the target before it is marked complete and the targets already reached have served the **bake volume**: the traffic those targets serve before the next is reached.

C0927	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	A [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) may raise it and never lower it.
C0928	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	It is not one of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md)'s eleven rows, the way the [explicit threshold](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) is not.
C0929	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	The window's cap bounds the whole.
C0930	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	built	Once it has run, the window closes timed out and the deployer reaches the remaining targets with no hold between them, since a quiet service that never serves the bake volume would otherwise never complete a deploy.

C0933	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md	unbuilt M1	A service's first release has no control whatever the score prefers: there is no build being replaced, so nothing can keep serving beside it and there is no control to run.
