C0940	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Refer is on every row because it is about the human and not the event: a holder who cannot judge what they were shown passes the row to another holder, on the terms [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) states.
C0941	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Those eight rows are the default path, not the whole set.
C0942	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	There is a gate before every deploy, so a customer that defines more environments gets a row for each.
C0943	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	It gets no more merge rows: one long-lived branch is the promotion path, so an extra environment is deployed into and never merged through.
C0944	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Five further rows are the factory's own and belong to no item: [_A role prompt or a skill_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md), where a version of what an agent is told is decided, [_A safeguard's withdrawal_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md), where the record that removes a human from a gate is decided, and [_A halt's withdrawal_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md), where the record that ends a [halt](../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) is.
C0945	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Two more take [_A safeguard's withdrawal_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md)'s shape (Approve and Reject with feedback, a human always, routed away from the author) and are stated beside the record each decides: the row that decides shortening decision-log retention, at [_Retention_](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md), and the row that ends a legal hold, at [_A legal hold_](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md).
C0946	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Each is outside the eight because everything the eight share is an item: a stage to be at, a build to point at, a release to reach.
C0947	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	They are fed from master, and the candidate-fed row stays the factory's own.
C0948	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	unbuilt M5	A human who wants to see a pre-merge change sees it on that candidate's own environment, where a safeguard (9) puts them, and the delay is that item's alone.
C0949	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	What actions a new row has follows from what feeds it, not from which row it was copied from.
C0950	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Fed from master, every new row has Approve and Hold for the reason Deploy to production has them: the merge has happened and the [release number](../06-releases/04-the-release-number.md) is already assigned, so hold is the only stop and what is left is a human's undo of a shipped change (10).
C0951	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	Reject is available up to the merge to master and nowhere after it.
C0952	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md	built	That is the actions and only the actions: the [_strategy_](02-the-rollout-strategy.md) and the [_analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) follow production rather than master, so a new row fed from master gets neither — the release number is already assigned, and what makes a control worth running is organic traffic the row does not have.
C0953	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	At a gate, artifacts are editable by hand.
C0954	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	unbuilt M2	Code is not: a gate approves or rejects an implementation, it never hand-patches one.
C0955	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	A human who wants different code authors it upstream and [sends the item back](06-going-back-up.md) to have it built again.
C0956	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Merge and deploy gates edit nothing — what they decide is an event, not a document.
C0957	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Reject sends the item back to a stage — the one its verdict names, and the nearest authoring stage above the row where it names none, which [_Going back up_](06-going-back-up.md) sets out; hold leaves the event queued with the change still good, which is why only the deploy rows offer it.
C0958	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	What a hold waits on differs by gate.
C0959	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	At the production deploy gate it is the service's open windows already at its window limit, a rollback whose revert has not shipped, a declared dependency that is not live, a [halt](../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) or a [change freeze](../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) an owner authored, or a record the [drift detector](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) found disagreeing with what runs.
C0960	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	At the candidate deploy gate it is a dependency that is not live yet, the producing release of a [contract migration](../07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md), since a candidate environment is composed from its dependencies' [current releases](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) and never from another service's candidate.
C0961	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	It is also a platform with no room for another environment, the one condition here decomposition could not have declared.
C0962	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	At both deploy gates it is also a service its owner has not yet marked [provisioned](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md): a repository or a persistent environment's store not yet written as existing.
C0963	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The two are different answers and have to stay distinguishable: the score learns from a reject and should learn nothing from a hold.
C0964	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	There are three kinds of hold, and only one is written as a decision.
C0965	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	A hold a human sets is the verdict of that firing's decision record, with that human as the actor.
C0966	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	A hold the factory sets over a record that already exists writes nothing: an open window, an [error budget](../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) exhausted, a dependency that is not current, a rollback whose revert has not shipped, a mismatch the drift detector found.
C0967	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	It is recomputed at the firing and at every re-evaluation of the open row while it stands, which is what checking at the moment of firing already requires.
C0968	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The row stays open while such a hold stands, one open event and never one per re-test.
C0969	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The gate fires once on the arrival, and the gate component re-evaluates the pending row whenever a record a hold of this kind reads changes, and on a fixed interval at the least.
C0970	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	When none stands the row closes as it would have at a firing with no hold.
C0971	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Where the row carries no [mark](01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) sending a human and the number on the open event is under the threshold, that is an auto-approve with the gate component as actor.
C0972	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Otherwise the row goes on waiting on the human it names, who may already have approved through the hold by naming it.
C0973	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	So a pending row is one open event however long the hold stood, the [pending predicate](01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) returns one row per gate per item, and the score's join of open row to close row has no unpaired rows from a hold.
C0974	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	A wait the factory could not compute at a firing, because no gate fired or because the condition is not a record, is written into the log by the log: a credential that cannot be reached, a platform with no room, a [stage dispatch matched nothing for](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md).
C0975	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	unbuilt M2	The component that met it is the caller and the actor, so [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) has a row to show.
C0976	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	It is two rows rather than one, the shape a [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md) already has for the same reason.
C0977	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The first row is written when the component meets the condition, naming the condition and the values it was met on.
C0978	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The second is written when the condition is found gone, by whichever component next reaches the work the wait stopped rather than by the one that met it, which may have stopped with it.
C0979	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	unbuilt M2	A first row with no second is what Work reads as still holding, and the pair is what makes how long it held a subtraction.
C0980	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	unbuilt M2	Neither row points at a [credential](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) or a [fleet entry](../10-fleet/README.md), which is what keeps the rule that no record written for traceability points at either.
C0981	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	unbuilt M2	None of the three counts against the attempt limit and none teaches the score.
C0982	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	An approve at a row where a hold stands names every hold standing, and holds at a deploy row are a set and not one: a dependency not current, an advisory match, and a freeze stand together in an ordinary incident.
C0983	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The verdict is an approve naming the set it goes through, on the [close event](01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) beside the verdict, and the gate refuses two shapes: an approve naming a hold not standing at that re-evaluation, and an approve whose set leaves out one that is, of which a bare approve while one stands is the case with nothing named.
C0984	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The factory's own holds are recomputed at every re-evaluation of the row, so the set to name from is already in hand, and a hold that arrived between the human's reading and their verdict refuses the approve, the human naming it or not on a second reading.
C0985	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Where the set holds the rollback hold below, the row waits on that hold's decider, an approve through the set being an approve through it.
C0986	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	A [halt](../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) and a [change freeze](../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) are holds of that second kind.
C0987	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Each is a record that already exists, neither writes anything at the firing, and both are recomputed at the firing and at every re-evaluation like the rest.
C0988	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	The halt is the one exception to the approve above, and the gate refuses a third shape for it: an approve at a firing where a halt stands.
C0989	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	There is no verdict a halt admits, because approving through one is ending it, and ending it is decided at [_A halt's withdrawal_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md) by the human that row routes to.
C0990	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	A freeze takes no exception at all and is approved through by naming it, the way an exhausted error budget is.
C0991	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	[A rollback whose revert has not shipped](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) is the one hold with a routing of its own, for what approving through it does.
C0992	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	Its row waits on the [duty](../../what-humans-do.md) that undoes a shipped change (10), on the named human a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md)'s routing field gives where one names it, and on the owner where nobody holds it.
C0993	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md	built	It never waits on whoever is at [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) when the hold is met, which is the separation [_A safeguard's withdrawal_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md) already makes between the actor and the decider, and the verdict carries the reason that human states.
C0994	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	Every stage has an attempt limit, authored with the rest of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md) (8).
C0995	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	An item that exceeds it stops being retried and appears in [_Work_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) as an escalation (12) — the factory saying it cannot do this one.
C0996	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	What the limit is compared against is the item's own count for the stage it is at, which [_What an item names_](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) keeps per stage and [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) writes: counting the rejects in the log instead would miss every attempt that failed before a gate fired, which is exactly what a high limit spends [agent](../01-one-pipeline.md) time on.
C0997	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	A deploy is not an attempt: the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) performs it, no entry is dispatched to it, and dispatch writes no count for it.
C0998	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	It is one parameter and not three: [_The interview_](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) counts its rounds against the same limit, and decomposition counts its re-decompositions against it, both on the intent and both upstream of an item's first stage, the interview having no gate of its own and decomposition's gate deciding a set rather than an item.
C0999	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	Holds do not count against the limit; a hold is not a failed attempt, for the same reason the score does not learn from one.
C1000	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	That spending is bounded by the [_spend ceiling_](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) where an owner authored one on the credential the agent runs on (a limit on units spent through it, reaching which is a hold), and by the quota on the [provider account](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) behind it otherwise.
C1001	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md	built	The two limits are one construction: this one counts attempts and the ceiling counts units, and each compares against the factory's own record rather than against anything at the provider.
C1002	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Every stage above moves an item toward a release; this is the one way back.
C1003	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	An item goes back three ways — a gate rejects it, the author working the stage sends it, or [the merge queue](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) rejects a candidate that failed its own re-verification — and what happens to the work below is the same in each.
C1004	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	A reject names the stage the item returns to.
C1005	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	By default that is the stage that fired — which is what a reject already meant: another attempt at the same artifact.
C1006	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	At the two event gates that reject there is no stage of their own, so the default is the nearest authoring stage above, which is Implementation for both; the merge queue's rejection defaults there as well, and whichever of [its three readings](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) it found — a candidate that no longer passes against what it will actually ship beside, or an encoding that could not hold its answer — names no stage above it.
C1007	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Decomposition names nothing at all: its reject re-decomposes the set rather than sending an item anywhere, so the field its close event would carry stays unwritten there.
C1008	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Where what is wrong is upstream, the verdict names the stage that owns it and the item goes there directly: an implementation rejected because the spec says two things returns to Spec, and Implementation plan and Tasks are not visited on the way.
C1009	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Nothing limits the jump to one step: a walk back one stage at a time arrives at the same place having paid an attempt and a rejected artifact at every stage in between.
C1010	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	The latest anything may be sent back from is the merge to master — the ceiling reject already has, and for its reason: after the merge the release number is assigned and the release exists, so what is left is [undoing the change after it shipped](../../what-humans-do.md) (10) and not a stage to re-author.
C1011	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Spec is the earliest of an item's own stages, and below it two things that are not among them may be named, because a defect above the item is not repairable inside it.
C1012	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	[_Decomposition_](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) is the first: an item that should have been three names it, decomposition runs again over the intent, and the set that comes out is decided at Decomposition like any other.
C1013	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	[_The interview_](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) is the second and the earliest thing reachable: a stage that cannot author because the request itself is underdetermined names the intent, which returns to the unrefined state an intent starts in and raises a question for whoever holds (3).
C1014	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	The stage's agent can send an item back too, and that is where the defect is usually found first: the agent working from a spec is the one that discovers it says two things.
C1015	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	It ends its attempt without authoring an artifact and writes a **rework request**, naming what owns the defect and the defect it found, and no gate fires, there being no artifact for one to decide over.
C1016	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	The defect stated is the request's reason, the field a [reject](01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md)'s close event carries, and the stage it names is handed it the same way.
C1017	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	A human [backstopping](../../what-humans-do.md) the stage (11, 12) sends one the same way, authorship being an attribute of the stage and the rework request being the stage's.
C1018	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	It is written into the log by the log, with whoever was authoring as the actor, which is the arrangement the third kind of hold already takes for something that happened where no gate fired.
C1019	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	A rework request is not a hold, which is the record it most resembles: it counts an attempt where a hold counts none, and where it names a stage it is an outcome on the author of that stage's artifact, which the [per-author prior](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) reads like every other outcome on that author's work.
C1020	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	Naming decomposition or the intent moves no prior at all: decomposition proposes a set rather than an artifact, and an intent is not an artifact either — the person who asked is not its author in the sense the prior keeps, and moving theirs would score an owner for having asked a question the factory could not answer.
C1021	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	A rework request is never a reject, since no gate fired and no verdict was given, so no threshold learns from one either way.
C1022	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	The attempt is counted at what the item is sent to, and that is the rule for a reject naming a target as much as for a rework request: the rework is booked against the thing that was wrong.
C1023	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Against a stage that is the stage's own count on the item; against decomposition or the intent it is one of the two counts the intent keeps, its re-decompositions and its interview rounds, which is why neither target needs a counter of its own.
C1024	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	The stages below the target then re-author counting nothing, the rule [_The merge queue_](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) already keeps for candidates re-verifying behind someone else's failure: they are being redone because of somebody else, and a count that rose for that would escalate the stage that noticed the defect rather than the one that made it.
C1025	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	So a spec returned to four times escalates at Spec (12), and that is what ends the loop: an item cannot go around the pipeline indefinitely, because every rework request costs an attempt at the stage it names.
C1026	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	Dispatch matches a stage against a [role](../01-one-pipeline.md) and writes the transition it is told about, so a rework request is the one thing that moves an item where neither a match nor a gate decided it: an agent decides that its own item goes backwards, and nothing decides over that.
C1027	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Nothing but the count limits it, so an agent that sends back every spec it is given spends that item's attempts at Spec and turns work the factory could have finished into an escalation (12) a human clears.
C1028	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	An item sent back to a stage re-authors every stage below it.
C1029	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	Sent past its stages it re-authors what the answer or the re-decomposition actually changed and no more: an item the re-decomposition left standing resumes where it was, and one it replaced is superseded and authors nothing again.
C1030	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	What was already authored is not removed: the versions stay in the chain the artifact store keeps, so a decision naming one still points at what was decided over.
C1031	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	But nothing below the target stands as approved, and a plan authored from a [spec version](07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) since superseded is not this item's plan; leaving it approved would have the [_Implementation_](07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) gate decide a build against it.
C1032	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	The gates below fire again on what is re-authored.
C1033	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	A spec version authored on a rework request introduces and withdraws [acceptance criteria](07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) like any other, and an item returning to Spec to drop a behaviour has to withdraw its criterion explicitly.
C1034	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	A criterion is a record of the service, written once and never written again, so one introduced by the version being replaced stays in force until a version withdraws it, and leaving it out of the new version withdraws nothing.
C1035	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	built	A rework request to a stage ends nothing.
C1036	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	The item keeps its [branch](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) and its builds, which are freed for good when it merges, is dropped, or is superseded by a re-decomposition — the last of those being the one way a rework request ends an item rather than sending it back anywhere.
C1037	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/06-going-back-up.md	unbuilt M1	Its [environment](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md), where it has one, is reclaimed while the item is stopped above Implementation and composed again when it next reaches the candidate deploy gate, which [_An environment per candidate_](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) sets out.
C1038	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md	unbuilt M5	The one gate where approving admits several timelines at once.
C1039	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md	unbuilt M5	Everything below it is per item, and it fires only where decomposition yielded more than one.
C1040	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	A criterion is a record of the service and the spec does not restate it.
C1041	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	The record is written exactly once, by the [artifact store](../../../01-one-pipeline.md) in the same call that submits the spec version introducing it, and never written again.
C1042	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	Withdrawal is recorded on the withdrawing spec version, so a version the gate rejects takes its withdrawal down with it.
C1043	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	A criterion's provenance has three sources and a default, read at introduction.
C1044	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	**Constraint-derived** is a field the artifact store writes once, in the same call, naming each [constraint](../../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) record the drafting stage held as its evidence for the criterion.
C1045	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	It is a link and not a mark, so that two questions are queries over the field: which criteria in force stand for a constraint, and which criteria in force were drafted under a constraint since withdrawn or replaced.
C1046	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	unbuilt M1	[Factory](../../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) lists both, the second beside each withdrawn constraint whose criteria still stand, so a rule no longer in force and still enforced is visible rather than silent; withdrawing such a criterion is an item like any other, and the constraint's own withdrawal removes none of them.
C1047	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	**Human-confirmed** is not a field but a query over the introducing spec version's [decision](../../01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md), which carries the actor and the resolved vector, the way in force is already a query.
C1048	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	Everything else is factory-drafted.
C1049	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	**Hazard-derived** is the third source, a field written the same way and naming the [area](../../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md) whose hazardous operation the criterion bounds.
C1050	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	The drafting stage derives one such criterion for a build in an `irreversible` area where none is in force, and this gate rejects, mechanically and whatever the score returns, a build in such an area with no criterion in force naming its operation.
C1051	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md	built	Which criterion controls a named hazard is then a query over the field, the question an auditor and the human at an `irreversible` Implementation gate ask.
C1052	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	Which criteria are in force for a build is then a query, and a build is a set of items: the ones merged into the repository it was made from, plus the item whose branch it is.
C1053	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	A criterion is in force for a build where the item that introduced it is in that build and no spec version in that build withdraws it.
C1054	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	Both halves have to read the same set: a criterion introduced by a sibling that has not merged is a promise this build's tree could not keep, so holding it in force would reject every candidate decomposed in parallel with the one that introduced it.
C1055	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	What a service promises now is the set in force for its current release, and [Factory](../../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports how many are in force, how many were admitted as exceptions, how many have never failed, and what share of them are [unwanted conditions](03-the-six-patterns.md), so drift is visible rather than re-read.
C1056	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	A criterion also carries an outcome history, derived and never authored: each run's results already attach to the build and carry the environment's composition with them, so the history reads the rate at which the criterion's outcome disagrees across builds that were composed from one [seed version](../../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/01-the-store-and-the-configuration.md) and whose diffs do not reach the requirement it names.
C1057	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	Above a bound the criterion is **unreliable**, what test practice calls flaky.
C1058	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	The bound is a field of the service record with a fixed default, which a safeguard (9) may raise and never lower, lowering being what takes a criterion out of the gate.
C1059	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	While unreliable, its failure rejects nothing, counts no attempt, and moves no prior, the treatment a [hold](../../04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) already gets, and [Merge to master](../07-merge-to-master.md) reads it as absent; it still runs and its result is still recorded.
C1060	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	Becoming unreliable raises one intent keyed by the criterion, the way a [detector](../../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) raises one, the [deployer](../../../08-operations/09-the-deployer.md) calling intake at the write that records the crossing result, so the encoding is re-authored through [the pipeline](../../../01-one-pipeline.md); a second raise while that intent is open joins it.
C1061	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	It leaves unreliable when a version of its encoding the intent introduced is in force and the rate falls under the bound.
C1062	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	built	Withdrawal is not silent for the three provenances that name an authority.
C1063	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	Withdrawing a **human-confirmed**, **constraint-derived**, or **hazard-derived** criterion is a [resolved factor](../../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) at this gate, routed to the human its provenance names rather than to the owner by default.
C1064	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	For a human-confirmed one that is the actor of the introducing decision, or another holder of that row's duty where that actor no longer holds it.
C1065	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	For a constraint-derived one it is whoever holds (2) over the [constraint](../../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) the field names, the named human a [safeguard](../../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md)'s routing field gives where one names it, and the owner where the constraint is withdrawn and nobody holds it.
C1066	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M3	For a hazard-derived one it is whoever holds (9) over the [area](../../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md) the field names, and the owner where nobody does; and the withdrawal stands only where the area is no longer graded `irreversible`, since a build in one with no such criterion in force is rejected at this gate.
C1067	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md	built	A criterion names the [requirement](../../../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) it answers.
C1068	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md	unbuilt M1	Where several items answer one requirement, the one named is the requirement [decomposition](../../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) derived for the item's share.
C1069	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md	built	This gate rejects in both directions over that field, mechanically and whatever the score returns: a requirement assigned to the item that no criterion in force for it names, and a criterion naming a requirement assigned elsewhere.
C1070	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md	unbuilt M1	Criteria are predicates, not prose, for the reason a [consumer contract](../../../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) is: what cannot be decided against an observed run is checked by nobody, and in the steady state that is most of them — the factory drafts its own criteria and a human reads them only where the score or a safeguard (9) puts one there.
C1071	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md	unbuilt M1	An intent grouped from [reports](../../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) has its source resolved rather than weighed, so this gate holds a human whatever the number is and no threshold an owner authors reaches it: the one floor this gate carries, owned by [_Reports_](../../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) and stated here because this is the gate it lands on.
C1072	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md	built	Each states one testable behaviour in one of a closed set of six sentence patterns, drawn from EARS.

C1188	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md	built	Its actions are Approve, Reject with feedback, and Edit in place, which is what every authoring row offers, and for the same reason: what is under decision is a document, and a human who wants different words writes them.
C1189	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md	built	There is no Hold, because a Hold would name a state the row is already in: a version not approved simply is not in force, and the one below it goes on being in force — or, for a chain an upgrade started, nothing is, and only the work that would have used it waits — where every other gate's hold exists to stop an event that would otherwise happen.
C1190	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md	built	What it reads is the factor set [_Factors, at least_](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) names for a version, and its threshold is a field of the [factory-wide settings record](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) rather than of an [environment](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md): there is no project here to have a production environment, and the row exists before any project does.

C1194	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md	built	Reject sends nothing back, there being no stage above this row to send it to, so [_Going back up_](../06-going-back-up.md) does not apply.

C1196	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	What it decides is the record [_A safeguard is not edited_](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) makes a withdrawal, a second record naming the [safeguard](../../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) it ends, and a withdrawal is not in force until this row approves it: the value in force reads the newest approved record for that subject, and a withdrawal nobody approved leaves the safeguard standing.
C1197	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	unbuilt M7	It has a row because it is the one operation in the factory that removes a human from a gate, which the document refuses to any mechanism; a record that did it with no decision on it would be that mechanism with an actor field for a conscience.
C1198	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	Its actions are Approve and Reject with feedback: there is no Edit in place, a withdrawal naming one record having nothing in it to edit, and no Hold, for the reason [_A role prompt or a skill_](09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) gives — a withdrawal not approved is already not in force.
C1199	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	Two more rows take this row's shape and are stated beside the records they decide: the shortening of decision-log retention at [_Retention_](../../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) and the ending of a legal hold at [_A legal hold_](../../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md), each removing a protection and so decided rather than written.
C1200	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	It reads no factor set and no threshold.
C1201	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	A human is at it always, the way a resolved factor puts one at a gate whatever the number — the [score](../../04-risk-score/README.md) deciding to remove a human is what [_How it learns_](../../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) refuses, and a row the score could auto-pass would be the refusal with a gap in it.
C1202	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	Who that human is follows the safeguard's own routing field: the [duty](../../../what-humans-do.md) or named human its rows route to, and the owner where it names none — and never the actor on the withdrawal.
C1203	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	Where those are one person, the row still fires and the decision is still written: an install with one owner cannot separate the two, and what the row buys there is a chained [decision](../01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) with an actor and a time on it rather than a second pair of eyes, which is what an auditor reads two months later.
C1204	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	Reject leaves the safeguard in force and the feedback beside the withdrawal, and [_the attempt limit_](../05-the-attempt-limit.md) does not reach this row, there being no stage and no item.
C1205	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md	built	The actor on both records is self-asserted until [seam 1](../../../deferred.md) carries a principal, so what the row records is who claimed to decide, still one more thing than a field flipped in place.
C1206	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	What it decides is the second record that ends a [halt](../../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md), and a withdrawal is not in force until this row approves it, so a halt nobody has ended stands.
C1207	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	Its actions are Approve and Reject with feedback: there is no Edit in place, a withdrawal naming one record having nothing in it to edit, and no Hold, a withdrawal not approved being already not in force.
C1208	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	It is a row of its own rather than the safeguard row widened, because the two rows decide different records and route differently.
C1209	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	A safeguard's withdrawal routes to the human that safeguard's own routing field names.
C1210	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	A halt has no such field, its subject being the factory, so this row routes to the owner, which is what an [unheld row](../../08-operations/07-pages.md) already does.
C1211	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	It reads no factor set and no threshold, for the reason the safeguard row reads none: a row the [score](../../04-risk-score/README.md) could auto-pass would be the factory deciding to stop being stopped.
C1212	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	The actor on the withdrawal is never the human this row waits on, the separation the safeguard row already makes between who writes a record and who decides it.
C1213	how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md	built	Where an install has one owner the two are one person, the row still fires, and what it buys is a chained [decision](../01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) with an actor and a time on it rather than a second pair of eyes.
C1214	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Four groups of factor, named so a human can argue with them, and the set is open.
C1215	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	**The change** — size, how much of the system it can affect, churn in the item's [area](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md), reversibility.
C1216	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Test coverage is deliberately not among them: it would be computed from the [encodings](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) the scored agent authored in the same [stage](../01-one-pipeline.md) as the code, so the scored author would set the factor.
C1217	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	It returns if something ever derives it independently of the author, the same condition [_Implementation_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) states for making the encoding itself mechanical.
C1218	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	[Mutation of the build](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/03-what-the-encoding-rests-on.md), which tests whether the encodings notice a seeded defect, is that derivation: it is a reading on the build rather than a factor of this group, decided at [_Merge to master_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) on the terms [_Implementation_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) states.
C1219	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	**The author** — the **per-author prior**, one per human and per AI model version, computed from that author's own work with the changes a human [undid after they shipped](../../what-humans-do.md) (10) and the [rollbacks](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) marked.
C1220	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A [revert's deploy](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) delivers several releases under one window, and that window's close moves no prior in either direction: one comparison over several changes is a label the evidence does not support, toward fault and toward trust alike.
C1221	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	Only the release the [search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) names takes a failed batch's outcome, and where no search runs or it names nobody, the batch teaches nothing — the reading an absent input already gets.
C1222	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A deploy has no author: the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), the component that performs every deploy, is not one, so a deploy accrues no [attempt](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) and moves no prior, and the outcome that does move one, the [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) closing on the release, is read against the artifact's author and never against what deployed it.
C1223	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A prior read as a list of only the bad outcomes could never narrow toward trust: what narrows it is the work nobody undid, so every outcome on that author's [artifact](../01-one-pipeline.md) moves it.
C1224	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Which [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) exits are outcomes is stated exit by exit and never as _closing without failing_, because three of the four close without failing and only one of them concludes anything.
C1225	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	`passed` narrows the prior and `failed` widens it, each being a comparison that ruled something out.
C1226	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	`timed out` and `skipped` move it in neither direction: a comparison the cap closed unresolved, and a release a rollback undid before anything measured it, each reports no outcome and so teaches the prior nothing.
C1227	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The [held-out sample](02-how-it-learns.md) gets the same silence in [_How it learns_](02-how-it-learns.md), where an exit reporting no outcome reports none there either.
C1228	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	Two outcomes are excluded for reasons of their own: a rollback a human [marked as not caused by the release](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), evidence about something other than the author; and [a queue rejection laid to something that moved under the candidate](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md), a dependency's release or the [design-system record](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) between the two runs, evidence about what moved and not the author.
C1229	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	How wide the prior is, how far it sits from the trust an author's evidence could justify, follows the same distinction: it may not narrow past the width its own count of `passed` and `failed` closes supports.
C1230	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	So a service too quiet to close a window on evidence keeps the prior it was installed with however many releases it has shipped, and windows that ruled nothing out buy no trust however many of them elapse.
C1231	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	That width and that count are written on the vector beside the factor, so an owner reading a low number can see whether it was earned or waited out.
C1232	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	It is kept per model version and not per family, which is how a new version accumulates a prior of its own; the cost: moving a [fleet entry](../10-fleet/README.md) to a newer version starts its evidence over, and keeping the old name for a new version reads the old version's outcomes as the new one's.
C1233	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The [effort](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) an entry runs at and the [role prompt](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) an agent works from change how one model behaves under one name too, and both are deliberately left out of the key — [_Paid for is not authored by_](../10-fleet/04-paid-for-is-not-authored-by.md) refuses the split and states what refusing it costs.
C1234	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Both are recorded on the vector a gate firing writes instead, which is where a fact a human argues with belongs.
C1235	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It starts wide for an author the factory has not seen and narrows with evidence, or starts at the prior the product shipped for that model version where it shipped one.
C1236	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	A version the instrument finds departed from its own earlier results takes [the treatment a drifted factor takes](02-how-it-learns.md) — the prior resolved rather than weighed, a human deciding whatever the formula returns, because a prior earned before the departure is evidence about a different author.
C1237	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The restriction [_The analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) keeps, that only a rollback or a human's undo traceable to the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) counts, is that window's parameters' rule and not this one's.
C1238	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The window learns from what the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) could have seen; the prior learns from every outcome on that author's work, a change undone for being the wrong feature included, because building the wrong thing well says something about the author and nothing about the health monitor.
C1239	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	**Exposure** — what the change reaches that the service did not reach before, read from the same diff: an outbound call added, a credential named or read, an authorization check removed or weakened, and a dependency change.
C1240	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A dependency change is the [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md)'s resolved set diffed against the set of the service's [current release](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md)'s build, each package added or moved named with its version and its declared licence.
C1241	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	An unpinned range that resolves differently with nothing in the manifest changed is read as the change it is.
C1242	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It only ever raises the number: a diff adding none of that reads as nothing, and nothing in this group lowers a score, its absence not being evidence of safety.
C1243	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It does not learn from outcomes: the signals [_How it learns_](02-how-it-learns.md) reads are misbehaviour signals, and a change that posts credentials outward moves neither error rate nor latency, so a [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) closing `passed` on it says nothing about it.
C1244	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	A release whose [security predicates](../05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md) rejected nothing closes that same window the same way, an outcome that teaches this factor nothing either: the class those predicates do not cover stays exactly as blind.
C1245	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Its weight is published and calibrated like the rest, and the only outcome that ever speaks to it is a later [advisory](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md), report, or undo, arriving on the prior and not on this factor.
C1246	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	And it reads the diff and never the intent: an injected change and a mistaken one add the same call, which is what makes it the one factor a stranger's sentence in a [report](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) cannot talk its way past.
C1247	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Its evidence is a list on the vector (each call, each credential name, each check, each package with its version and licence, and the file and line of each), so what the human at [_Implementation_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) argues with is that list beside the diff [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) renders.
C1248	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	stated	The list is not the diff, and where the two disagree the human decides over the code, the disagreement being what the list's derivation missed.
C1249	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It is derived by the factory from the diff and the build record, independent of the author, which is the condition test coverage failed above.
C1250	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	What extracts a call or a credential read is per toolchain, as [_What a consumer declares_](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md)'s derivation is, so where no extractor runs for a toolchain the factor is unavailable and resolves the way an unavailable factor does below (a human at the gate whatever the formula returns, the vector naming why), and never as nothing.
C1251	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	**Context** — what this change touches in this customer's business, and which sibling services consume what it publishes.
C1252	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	What says which of the two an item is in is the [hazard severity](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md) of its area, this group's one declared input and the only term in the vector an owner writes rather than the factory derives.
C1253	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	An `irreversible` value is resolved rather than weighed: at [_Implementation_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) the decision is a human's whatever the formula returns, one the [held-out sample](02-how-it-learns.md) may not select past.
C1254	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	That gate and not the three above it, because a human at all four would stop the factory authoring in such an area at all, and Implementation is where the diff and the exposure list are already in front of one.
C1255	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	Not [_Deploy to production_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) either: a human at every release of a service is what a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) already buys an owner who wants one.
C1256	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The [intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md)'s source is here too, and it is the second of the two values in this group resolved rather than weighed.
C1257	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	An intent whose evidence carries text the factory did not author, that no gate has admitted, resolves the source value toward the [Spec](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) gate: a human at the gate whatever the formula returns, one the [held-out sample](02-how-it-learns.md) may not select past, resolution being what it already never overrides.
C1258	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	An intent grouped from [reports](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) is the existing case: that channel is the one way in the factory cannot authenticate, which [_Reports_](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) states and owns.
C1259	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Withdrawing a criterion whose [provenance](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/01-the-record.md) is human-confirmed or constraint-derived is a resolved value at Spec, and so is a screen revision that removes a forbidden transition a human-confirmed machine declared, which [_In force, and withdrawal_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) and [_The screen state machine_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/04-the-screen-state-machine.md) state.
C1260	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The change group's reversibility factor takes that same resolution where the diff destroys stored data: resolved rather than valued, a human deciding at Implementation with the diff in front of them.
C1261	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Exposure above [the bound](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) gate policy sets takes it too, the [held-out sample](02-how-it-learns.md) barred from selecting past it.
C1262	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	What makes a diff destroy stored data is read per toolchain by the extractor the exposure factor already rests on, and a diff that extractor cannot read resolves the factor rather than valuing it.
C1263	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The exposure factor reads a credential name added or removed in the service's configuration file the way it reads one added or removed in code, since a change to that file is a diff like any other.
C1264	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A version of what an agent is told is scored on a set of its own, because the change group is computed from a diff and a [role prompt](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) has no code to have sized and no area to have churned.
C1265	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Three factors replace that group at [_A role prompt or a skill_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) — the share of the factory working from this version; **departure**, how far it differs from the version in force; and reversibility, which is high for every version, [withdrawal](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) being a second record and nothing having been deployed.
C1266	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	The author and context groups are unchanged, the author being the agent that wrote the version.
C1267	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	One version has no author at all — the [shipped entry](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) an upgrade's first start enters, install's own firing no gate — and that absence is resolved rather than classed: a human at the gate whatever the formula returns, decided here so that a narrow shipped skill cannot auto-pass at an upgrade as a side effect.
C1268	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Likelihood and impact stay separate until the last step.
C1269	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	This is also why one score decides three things about a change: the gate reads mostly likelihood against impact, the [rollout strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) reads mostly impact against reversibility and how fast a problem would appear, and the boundary of the [_analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) reads mostly impact.
C1270	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Cost is not a factor and not a strategy input.
C1271	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	stated	The strategy is the same case: what a control costs is the owner's to weigh, and the levers are theirs — the [window limit](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), the [fraction of its instances a release keeps](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), and a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) on the strategy.
C1272	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	Model spend has a lever of its own in the [spend ceiling](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) an owner authors on a credential, which reaches nothing a control costs.
C1273	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The defaults the score supplies where an owner authored nothing are not a fourth factor.
C1274	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	[_Gate policy_](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md)'s eleven parameters are each a field of the record its scope names: a service, an area, an environment, or the [factory-wide settings record](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md).
C1275	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	Ten of them move as outcomes arrive, where the change, exposure and context groups are computed from the one change under decision.
C1276	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	They are fields of a **score version**, a row of the [decision log](../../deferred.md) appended by the log when the score calls for it and chained like every row there.
C1277	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It names the published formula with the weight it gives each factor under each factor set, the factor sets, and every value the score supplies, with a supplied value for ten of the eleven rows and none for the [list of allowed predicate kinds](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md), which no outcome teaches.
C1278	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The weights are a field of the version from the first row and not a fact kept beside it: a decision re-scored under the version it names has to return the number it was decided on, which is what the version is recorded for, and a formula recorded without its weights is a shape and not a function.
C1279	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Every decision names the version in force.
C1280	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	The values the product ships are fields of the score version the install's first start appends — the starting threshold, each set's weights, the value of the impact factors above which the strategy keeps a control, and the width of a [band](02-how-it-learns.md).
C1281	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	stated	So this document names no number, and a decision taken under one names the version that carried it.
C1282	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A version that differs in a supplied value having moved takes effect as it is appended — gating each would gate the learning itself.
C1283	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A version that changes the published formula or the factor set is a different event, because what an owner authors is compared against the number the published formula returns: under a changed formula the same change gets a different number, so the authored value would go on being displayed while meaning something else, and an owner who set a [risk threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) after an [incident](../08-operations/06-incidents.md) would hold a number the score had silently redefined under them.
C1284	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	So such a version does not decide a gate an authored threshold binds until the owner has confirmed or re-authored that threshold against it (8), the version in force at those gates staying the prior one meanwhile — the arrangement [_A role prompt or a skill_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) already gives a version nobody approved, which simply is not in force.
C1285	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A recalibration is the third thing that writes a version, and it is stated here because it falls in neither branch on its own.
C1286	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It is the score refitting the weights over what [_How it learns_](02-how-it-learns.md) reads, and it writes a version differing in the weights and in nothing else.
C1287	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It takes the second branch: under new weights the same change gets a different number, factory-wide and at once, which is the redefinition that branch exists to gate, so a recalibration is in force as appended where no owner authored the threshold and waits on the owner's confirmation (8) where one did.
C1288	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The resolution ends at a scope when the recalibrated version is in force there and not when it is written: at once at a scope with no authored threshold, and at an authored scope when its owner confirms or re-authors, the prior version and the resolution standing meanwhile.
C1289	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	The version waiting is shown beside the threshold it waits on, on the screen the threshold is authored from, so the owner whose silence keeps their gates resolved is the one who sees why.
C1290	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	The per-author prior narrows as outcomes arrive, so the same change scores lower over time and an authored threshold auto-passes a different share of changes month over month with no version having changed the formula at all.
C1291	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	That movement is not the redefinition above and is not gated like one: it is the factory earning trust, which is what a learned score is for, and what holds it to earned trust is the width rule this section already states — a prior narrowing no further than its own count of `passed` and `failed` closes supports.
C1292	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	What separates the two is a reading rather than a rule, so the reading is recorded and published.
C1293	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	The realized auto-pass rate at a threshold when it was authored is a field of the [policy version](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) that set it — read back as the newest version that set that threshold on that scope, which is that section's own rule — frozen there because a version stays in force while the rate moves under it, so no query over the decisions taken under it could serve as the reference.
C1294	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Against it sits the rate at that threshold now, and beside both the share of held-out releases that failed within each band of the number, which [_How it learns_](02-how-it-learns.md) defines and [_What the factory auto-approved, and what was undone_](../11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md) reports.
C1295	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	Read together the three say which of the two happened: a rate that rose while each band's failure share held is a score that got better, and a rate that rose with the shares rising under it is the number drifting beneath the threshold an owner set, which re-authoring that threshold answers (8).
C1296	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The vector is recorded where it was computed and never recomputed.
C1297	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A gate firing writes it onto that firing's [_decision_](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) beside the number, the score version, and the [policy version](../../what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md), so every firing produces a vector of its own.
C1298	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It names the per-author prior's width and the count of resolved window exits behind it, the pair being what tells a narrow prior earned on outcomes from one that only waited: a width is a claim about how much the factory knows, and a claim with no count beside it is one a human at the gate cannot weigh.
C1299	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Where an agent authored what is under decision, the vector also names the effort its entry ran at and the versions of the role prompt and the [skills](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) it worked from.
C1300	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It names the [input manifest](../01-one-pipeline.md) the artifact was authored from too, so the input that varies most between two runs of one model is read back with the words and the effort that were fixed.
C1301	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Recomputing one later reads today's prior, today's factor set, and today's formula, not the vector the decision was made on — and the vector a human argues with has to exist while they are deciding, which is why the decision is opened at the firing rather than at the verdict.
C1302	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	At the four [artifact gates](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) the change factor is computed from the artifact under decision and at [Decomposition](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md) from the set [decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) proposed, so it is weakest exactly where the decision is cheapest to get wrong.
C1303	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Which gate a firing is at also decides which factors are in the set at all, and that is what keeps the resolution below from swallowing the gates above a build.
C1304	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Exposure is read from a diff and a [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) record, and the first build of an item exists when [Implementation](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) finishes, so at Decomposition, Spec, Implementation plan and Tasks the exposure group is inapplicable and not unavailable — no vector there records it, and nothing there is resolved on its account.
C1305	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	One threshold is read against three factor sets, so the number every set returns has to be on one scale, or the threshold would mean three things.
C1306	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The four rows below a build weigh four groups; the four above weigh three, exposure being inapplicable there; and [_A role prompt or a skill_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md)'s row weighs the set of its own stated above.
C1307	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	So each set's weights are fitted apart, on the held-out decisions taken on that set alone, to the one scale every set shares: the number estimates the share of held-out windows that failed among decisions taken at that number on that set, which is what the bands [_How it learns_](02-how-it-learns.md) publishes per set read.
C1308	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A set whose held-out decisions are too few to fit keeps the weights the product shipped for it, and the counts on its bands say so.
C1309	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	A factor the score cannot compute is resolved and never valued.
C1310	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The vector records which factor was unavailable and why rather than leaving a gap a reader has to interpret, and a decision whose vector records one is a human's whatever the formula returns.
C1311	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	It binds that firing and no other, a vector being computed at one firing and never recomputed, so how far a resolution reaches is decided by whether its cause still holds: a supplier still down, a factor still drifted, a toolchain still without an extractor are found again by the next firing's own vector and resolve it again on their own, and a supplier that came back is not carried forward as a resolution nothing can lift.
C1312	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	One cause does not recur on its own and is carried instead.
C1313	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	A [truncated read](../01-one-pipeline.md) is a property of the artifact version, not of the moment, and a stage authoring from a truncated artifact produces one that is complete in form again — so the withholding is written onto every artifact version an item's later stages author from a truncated one, and every gate below the truncation resolves too.
C1314	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	That covers an input the score could not read, a [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) whose diff it could not take, an evaluation that failed outright, a factor [calibration found drifted](02-how-it-learns.md), a toolchain with no extractor behind the exposure list, and an artifact version authored from [a truncated read](../01-one-pipeline.md).
C1315	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	Direction is not sufficiency, so what must not depend on a component being up is the decision and not a term in a sum.
C1316	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The formula still runs on the factors that were computable and the number is recorded beside the resolution rather than left out, so that calibration has the reading it would otherwise lose on every resolved decision; it is shown when every number is, beside the verdict and never while the human decides, which [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) already requires and a resolved row needs more than any other — the number there decided nothing, and a human shown it first would read it as though it had.
C1317	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	built	The resolution being a recorded fact and not a low number is also what lets the [held-out sample](02-how-it-learns.md) honour its own exclusion: it may not select past a resolved vector, which is read off the decision rather than inferred from where the number landed.
C1318	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md	unbuilt M1	What tells the two apart is that the naming is read in aggregate as well as per decision: [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports how many gates a resolved factor put a human at, grouped by which factor, over the same span as its other numbers — so an outage in one supplier is one count rising and a score that got stricter is not, and no owner has to open decisions to find out which happened.
C1319	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The score is learned, not fixed.
C1320	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Every bad decision feeds back and refines it: an [auto-passed](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) change that a human undoes (10), a low-risk change its analysis window rolled back, a gate the factory would have passed but a human rejected.
C1321	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	stated	Outcome feedback is the sharpest signal but not the only one — any source that improves the score is admissible, and the input set stays open.
C1322	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A supplied value moves on both ends of its own row, and [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md)'s table is what says where the second end is: every row there states what goes wrong at each end, and both are evidence.
C1323	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	One end is something going wrong — a rollback, a human's undo, a [crossing](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) of the boundary the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) could have seen and did not, an [item](../01-one-pipeline.md) the [attempt limit](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) gave up on.
