C1324	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Which parameter a rollback or an undo moves is a fact of the record rather than a judgment about the regression: one on a release whose window closed [passed](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) is evidence about the power, the exit that rules a regression out having cleared one it should have caught, and one on a release whose window timed out is evidence about the size, nothing having been ruled out at all.
C1325	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The other is the parameter costing more than it returns, which is observable for most of them: attempts nothing has ever needed, a [cap](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) far longer than any window of that service takes to resolve, a [window's size](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) finer than its traffic can rule anything out at, a [power](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) whose windows run to the cap on volume a lower one would have closed passed within it.
C1326	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	So the two are read apart — the size's own end is the finest size the traffic reaches, which the window computes and reports, and the power's is a volume a lower rate would have closed within — and reading one event as both ends at once would move two supplied values against a single piece of evidence.
C1327	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Calibration also reads each factor's distribution over the decisions that named it, against that factor's own history.
C1328	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A factor whose distribution has moved is measuring something other than what the formula was calibrated on: an agent that learned what the factor rewards, an area whose shape changed, a fault in what supplies it.
C1329	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A factor found drifted takes the treatment [an unavailable factor](01-factors-at-least.md) takes, resolved and not valued, a human deciding whatever the formula returns and the vector naming it, until a recalibration is in force at that gate.
C1330	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A recalibration writes a [score version](01-factors-at-least.md) differing in the weights, and _Factors, at least_ says where such a version is in force at once and where it waits on an owner.
C1331	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The [per-author prior](01-factors-at-least.md) is exempt from that reading and gets one of its own, because its distribution moving is what it working looks like: it narrows as outcomes arrive, so read against its own history every earned narrowing is a drift, and the treatment would put humans at gates factory-wide for the score having learned something.
C1332	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	What replaces it is the reading a learned value can fail — the prior each held-out decision was taken on, against what that release's window then closed.
C1333	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A prior that no longer separates the held-out releases whose windows failed from the ones whose windows passed is drifted whatever its distribution did, and takes the same treatment until it is recalibrated.
C1334	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	It is read on the held-out sample and nowhere else, for the reason the threshold rises on nothing else: outside the sample the prior itself decided which changes a human read, so the outcomes there are the ones its own decisions selected.
C1335	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A prior standing drifted stops the sample from selecting on that author at all, the resolution being one the sample may never pass, so no new held-out evidence about that prior arrives while it stands: what recalibration reads is the held-out decisions already recorded, and recalibration is the only exit.
C1336	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A [truncation](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) of the decision log that removes every held-out decision on a drifted author removes what its recalibration would read, and the resolution does not stand waiting on evidence that can no longer arrive.
C1337	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The prior restarts as an unseen author's: at the width [_Factors, at least_](01-factors-at-least.md) gives an author the factory has not seen, or at the prior the product shipped for that model version, with the sample selecting on the author again.
C1338	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Where some held-out decisions on the author survive the cut, recalibration reads them and nothing restarts.
C1339	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The [gate row that decides a shortening](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) names each author whose prior stands drifted and whose held-out decisions the cut would remove, so the human at it weighs that and not the trail's length alone.
C1340	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Two of the window's parameters have no second end here and are stated as such rather than left to be discovered.
C1341	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The [power](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) is not a third, which is worth saying because it is the confidence's opposite number and the reader will expect it to behave like one.
C1342	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	What shows a power too high is not a counterfactual: a window running to the cap on volume a lower one would have closed passed within it, read off that service's own windows.
C1343	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	No outcome says [the confidence a comparison must reach](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) was too high.
C1344	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	What would show it is a rollback that turned out unnecessary, and the one thing that establishes a failed release was fine is a human [marking the rollback as not caused by the release](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md).
C1345	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	That says the comparison was confounded, nothing about how confident it should have been, so it excludes the rollback from what learns and teaches the confidence nothing.
C1346	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	And the other end of the [item-size target](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) is cost per feature and rework rate, both of which are [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md)'s to report, cost per feature converted to one number only where the owner authored rates.
C1347	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	Beside them the target reads [environment-hours](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) per item and [instance-hours](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md) per release as they arrive, figures the design already records rather than values it would have to estimate.
C1348	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	Both are allowed to move one way because neither compounds: the traffic a comparison needs grows as the log of the confidence, where it grows as the inverse square of the size.
C1349	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	stated	So an owner may have to author over one of them, which [authoring being an override](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) is exactly the case for.
C1350	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	Cost per feature read here is the same figure [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) shows, and it moves only on intents that reached an [outcome](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) at close.
C1351	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	Where none has, the item-size target's cost end holds still rather than moving on a number nothing yet grounds.
C1352	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A human's rejection is an input the threshold falls on, and nothing in the record can bear one out the way a window bears out an approval: the rejected version never ships, so a rework that then passed is consistent with the rejection having been right and with it having been a false alarm.
C1353	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	What the record does hold is how the rejection resolved, and the threshold reads a rejection only once it has.
C1354	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Every rejection is written with what the human named, and it resolves one of four ways: the re-authored version approved, differing by content digest in what the rejection named; approved without differing there; rejected again; or the item reaching the [attempt limit](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md).
C1355	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The first, third, and fourth are read as a gate the factory needed, and move the threshold down; the second is read as a false alarm, moves nothing, and is published per human at [Factory](../11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md) beside what each approved and how often it was undone.
C1356	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	How far it moves is one band of the reading over the number this section publishes below, the share of held-out releases that failed within each band, and never below the lowest band.
C1357	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	A rise on the held-out sample takes the same step, so the two are one number, the band's width, a field of the [score version](01-factors-at-least.md) an owner can read on the record.
C1358	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The [risk threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) is a third exception and not for want of an observable.
C1359	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	So the threshold moves down on outcomes and up on one thing only, which is the next paragraph.
C1360	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The factory holds out a random sample, auto-passing what it would have gated under the longest analysis window there is, to keep unbiased signal on the authors and areas it has stopped trusting.
C1361	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	How much it holds out is the **held-out sample rate**, authored with the rest of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) (8) rather than chosen by the score, for the reason every other bound there exists.
C1362	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Where an owner authors none the score supplies it, the same division the [window's size](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) uses, and a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) is a ceiling over it — sampling less is a human kept at a gate.
C1363	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	It is a field of the [factory-wide settings record](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), the sample being one formula's and no service's, and a safeguard on a service, a project or an area is what narrows it there.
C1364	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A held-out window that timed out is not evidence for raising a threshold.
C1365	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The sample exists to produce an outcome, and the exit that reports no outcome reports none here either: counting it as good would put back exactly what the sample was built to remove — an outcome recorded only for what the factory let through, this time as silence read as success.
C1366	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The [per-author prior](01-factors-at-least.md) reads the same silence the same way, and that is what keeps the ratchet to one thing: a prior that narrowed on unresolved windows would fall underneath the threshold at the same time and from the same quiet, so more changes would clear a fixed authored threshold while the threshold itself was the only thing an owner was watching.
C1367	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The sample is also the only evidence there is for raising a threshold, which is more than the paragraph above asks of it.
C1368	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The sample is the score's own to hold out of, and it reaches nothing an owner safeguarded.
C1369	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A safeguard (9) can only add, and a gate held always-on is a human added; a sample that could pass one would be the single mechanism in the design that removes a human from a gate — exactly what a safeguard exists to prevent.
C1370	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	So what the sample may auto-pass is what the score itself would have gated on a vector that resolved nothing — never the human [a resolved factor](01-factors-at-least.md) put there, whether the score could not compute that factor or computed a value the design resolves on, the score being in no position to auto-pass a gate its own number never decided.
C1371	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	The selection is recorded on the decisions rather than as a set of its own, in two fields that must not be one.
C1372	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	**Held out** says the score selected this [item](../01-one-pipeline.md), written on every decision on it from the selection onward, so an item selected once auto-passes every gate the score itself would have gated, never one [a resolved factor](01-factors-at-least.md) put a human at, and reaches production with a human removed at each of them.
C1373	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	**Why it auto-passed** says, at this firing, whether the auto-pass came from the number being under the threshold or from the selection, and reads threshold at a gate the score would have passed anyway.
C1374	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	Beside the first field the decision carries the rate in force at the selection, after every safeguard clamping it on that item's service, project and area, so a held-out outcome can be weighted by the probability that selected it, per service or per author, at whatever scope the rate was authored.
C1375	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	The health monitor runs the window to the [cap](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) on the selection and not on the provenance, because what the sample is evidence about is a [release](../06-releases/02-the-release-record.md) reaching production with a human removed somewhere above it.
C1376	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A held-out release also takes a strategy that keeps a [control](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) wherever there is a build to start one from, whatever the vector's impact half would have picked: the sample exists to produce outcome evidence, and a deploy [without a control](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) leaves only [the weak fallback](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md).
C1377	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	A service's first release has no control whatever the score prefers, so the sample cannot usefully select one.
C1378	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	One reading is over the number rather than over a factor, and the sample is the only place it can be computed: the share of held-out releases whose windows failed within each band of the number, each band carrying the count of resolved held-out windows behind it.
C1379	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	built	It is published per factor set, and within each set per service and factory-wide, beside the realized auto-pass rate at the [threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) in force on that set.
C1380	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md	unbuilt M2	It is also the reference the [risk threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) is authored against, [_Factors, at least_](01-factors-at-least.md) being where what the pair of rates distinguishes is stated.
C1381	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/README.md	unbuilt M1	A vector of named factors, reduced to one number by a published formula.
C1382	how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/README.md	unbuilt M1	Both halves matter — the number is what a [gate](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) compares against a [threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md), the vector is what a human reads while deciding, and the number is what they see beside their [verdict](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) once written, for the reason [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) gives.
C1383	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Environments are records, not names in code.
C1384	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	One record type with a **kind** fixed at creation and three values — production, one a customer defines, and a [candidate](../06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md)'s own.
C1385	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	[Gate](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) thresholds, credential references, and the targets an environment is reached at are fields of both persistent kinds; the strategy default is production's alone, because a [strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) decides whether a [control](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) runs and a control exists only where [organic traffic](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) does.
C1386	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A candidate's environment holds nothing an owner authored, being created at the gate that decides its deploy and so unable to hold the threshold deciding it.
C1387	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	What it does hold is what no deploy can happen without: the targets the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) created for it and the credential reference it reaches them with, both written by the deployer at creation rather than authored by anybody.
C1388	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Production exists everywhere, every candidate gets one of its own, and customers define more per [project](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C1389	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A service's non-secret configuration is a file in its repository, so a change to it is an ordinary [item](../01-one-pipeline.md): a diff, a build, a gate, a candidate environment, a release number, an [analysis window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md).
C1390	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	It does not reopen the second path [the rollout strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) refuses for a flag, since the value comes from the file at deploy and never from a branch left in the running build.
C1391	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Each address at which the service reaches another service's interface is an entry of that file naming the service and the interface, which is what [_Which producer a consumer reaches_](../07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md) resolves a call site against and what the deployer writes per environment.
C1392	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Its secrets are the exception: held by name in the file and resolved to a value at deploy through the resolver that seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../deferred.md) names, so no build and no diff ever carries one.
C1393	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	An environment names its **targets**, plural: the addresses a deploy is performed against.
C1394	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	They are a field, not records of their own, so nothing holds a reference that must survive an address change.
C1395	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A target is never an environment and an environment is never a target: an environment of any kind holds targets, and the kind says which writer created the record, not how many addresses it holds, so a customer with four addresses has one environment naming four targets and not four environments.
C1396	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is keyed by [service](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) and environment rather than by target and carries completion per target as a field, a mismatch names the address that differs, and [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) reads each in turn.
C1397	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The field is ordered, and the order is the one a [rollout](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) reaches the targets in.
C1398	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The owner writes it when they declare the targets, and it is the only order there is: on a platform that serves no share, it is the whole of what limits how much of production a release reaches before anything has read it.
C1399	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Which of an environment's targets a service runs on is a field of the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), owner-written at Factory beside the parameters, and where the owner writes none the service runs on every target of the environment.
C1400	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Every reader of targets reads the service's set.
C1401	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	[Current release](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is completion on the service's targets; the deploy record's completion per target, the [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md)'s target set, and the [rollout](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md)'s order are over them; [the drift detector](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) reads them and no other for that service; and the [score](../04-risk-score/README.md) picks the row with a control only where every target in the service's set serves a share.
C1402	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A target removed from the service's set is a [removal](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md) on that target first, as a target leaving the environment is.
C1403	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	An environment also declares its [platform](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md): the credential the deployer composes a [candidate environment](02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) through, resolved by name through seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../deferred.md), and whether that platform can compose an environment on demand.
C1404	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A production environment declaring one that cannot is refused at [adoption](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) and at decomposition for its services, an environment per candidate being the shape [_What the factory does not build_](../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) admits and nothing else.
C1405	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	So a candidate environment is composed on the platform the production environment record of the item's service declares.
C1406	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The deployer's [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) for a platform and the [maximum concurrent candidate environments](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/06-deploy-to-candidate-environment.md) are keyed by that record, one of each per production environment, so an install whose projects run on two platforms adds neither count across them.
C1407	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	An environment also declares, per target, whether the platform behind it serves a share: whether it can decide what fraction of arriving traffic reaches each of two builds.
C1408	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The owner writes it with the target, and it is the fact the [score](../04-risk-score/README.md) reads when it picks a [strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md).
C1409	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	On an environment with a target that serves no share the row with a control is unavailable, so the score picks the other row rather than a rollout the deployer cannot perform, and a strategy the platform cannot perform stops being something the deployer discovers.
C1410	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) names the strategy performed beside the one picked, so a target declared as serving a share on a platform that moved instances instead shows there as a control that did not run.
C1411	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The kind is also the [seam](../../deferred.md), two writers never writing a record of the other's kind.
C1412	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	An owner writes the persistent kinds on [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md): production's at the creation of the project, which an owner does not choose because production exists everywhere, and each further one when they define it.
C1413	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) writes a candidate's, creating it at the approval of [Deploy to candidate environment](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/06-deploy-to-candidate-environment.md), tearing it down for good when the [item](../01-one-pipeline.md) merges, is dropped, or is superseded by a [re-decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), and reclaiming one meanwhile as [_An environment per candidate_](02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) sets out.
C1414	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The only component that reaches a deploy target is the one that reaches environments, and a gate decides an event and edits nothing.
C1415	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A persistent environment contains a whole project, every service in it, while a [release](../06-releases/02-the-release-record.md), a [release number](../06-releases/04-the-release-number.md), a master branch, and an [analysis window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) are all per service.
C1416	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A deploy puts one service's build on one environment, so an environment fed by a twelve-service project has twelve independent promotion paths.
C1417	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A candidate's environment contains no project: it is composed for one candidate, which [_An environment per candidate_](02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) sets out.
C1418	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [gate row](../03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md) a customer's environment adds describes that environment; what fires is one [decision](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) per deploy into it.
C1419	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A customer's extra environments are places master's releases are deployed to and not ordered stops — nothing makes a release pass through one before production, and the [production deploy gate](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) gains no [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) on another environment's deploy.
C1420	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	An owner who wants a human to look at a release running somewhere first adds a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) putting one at that gate (9).
C1421	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Its history is not a field.
C1422	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The deploys that name a record are inbound edges, the arrangement the release record already uses, and [incidents](../08-operations/06-incidents.md) and [rollbacks](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) exist on production environments alone, because the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) that opens an incident and the strategy that keeps a control both attach to a production deploy and to no other.
C1423	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	One long-lived branch is each service's promotion path: master.
C1424	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A candidate has a branch of its own until it merges, is dropped, or is superseded by a re-decomposition.
C1425	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Merging and deploying are separate events and so are separate gates — a merge admits a change to master, a deploy puts a build on an environment, and either can happen without the other.
C1426	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A deploy can be rerun; a merge cannot be unmerged the same way.
C1427	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A candidate is not a record.
C1428	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	It names an item and the build currently in force for it, and a build on a candidate branch belongs to one item, so the pair and the build pick out the same thing — a record per pair would repeat what the build and the item say already.
C1429	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The [criteria](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) results and the candidate deploy attach to the build.
C1430	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The branch and the environment are the item's and persist across a rebuild, the environment being a record already.
C1431	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	There is no firing to open a row at, the [Merge to master](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) gate's own having closed as an approval, and no [factor vector](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) computed for it.
C1432	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	It is counted as an attempt against [_the limit_](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) at the [stage](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) the item is sent back to.
C1433	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A **build** is a record, one per commit built.
C1434	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The **build runner**, the component that performs every build, reaches no deploy target, and never crosses [seam 4](../../deferred.md), writes it when called to build.
C1435	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The first two: the implementation stage when it finishes, so that the [Implementation gate](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) decides over a build that exists and the [score](../04-risk-score/README.md) has a diff to compute the [change factors](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) from; and the candidate deploy where a rebuild is needed.
C1436	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The other two: the merge queue at [re-verification](03-the-merge-queue.md), which builds the candidate branch onto the master it will actually merge into; and the [search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), whose builds are of commits on no branch, decided by no gate and bounded there rather than here.
C1437	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A rebuild is a new build, so an item holds one build per time it was built and the release names the last one.
C1438	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Every record naming a build says which: a gate decision names the one in force at the firing, a factor vector is computed from that same build, a [consumer contract](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) names the one it was derived from.
C1439	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The commit is a field of the build and not a record, one commit per build and nothing pointing at a commit that has no build.
C1440	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A build also names the [shipped-bundle identity](../../deferred.md#the-products-release-channel) of the release of the product that made it, which is what says which [way in](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) the build carries.
C1441	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	So is what the build resolved: every third-party package, the version resolved to, and the licence the package declares, written by the build runner from the build it performed.
C1442	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The same set, diffed against the [current release](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md)'s build's, is the dependency change the score's [exposure](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) factor reads, and a licence policy is decided against it as a build-kind [constraint](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md); which services ship a package at a version is a query over current release, build, and set, and not an estimate.
C1443	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Each entry therefore records the source it was resolved from, a registry or a repository named the way a deploy target is, the version, and the digest of the content the resolution produced, with the declared licence kept as the claim it is.
C1444	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The build names beside them the digest of the artifact the build runner produced from it, which is what a deploy puts on a target and what [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) and a [rollback](../06-releases/06-rollback.md)'s redeploy read it against.
C1445	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [advisory](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) match is keyed by source and name rather than name alone.
C1446	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [exposure](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) factor's diff reads a digest moved under an unchanged version as a package moved, which is what it is.
C1447	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	And an owner may state as a build-kind [constraint](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) which sources a service may resolve from, a package from any other rejecting at Implementation, which is the one mechanical answer to a package substituted from a registry nobody chose.
C1448	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Where the resolver produced no digest or no source for an entry, the entry says so.
C1449	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	That is the resolver's coverage, which the build records beside the set per ecosystem and per entry: a constraint reaching such an entry resolves a human to the gate, and an advisory match over it is unassessed rather than clear.
C1450	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Each entry also records what required it: the manifest in the repository that declares it, for a package the service names itself, or the entry that pulled it in, for one that arrived through another, so the set is a graph from the manifests down and not a flat list.
C1451	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [advisory](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) match at Implementation and the [exposure](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) factor each name, for a package added or moved, whether the diff touched a manifest on its chain; a package the agent named itself, with no manifest on its chain, is how a registered look-alike name arrives.
C1452	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	An advisory item's plan reads the same edge, and where an entry's chain reaches no manifest the service holds, the item moves the entry that requires it.
C1453	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Resolution and execution are two steps, in that order, because a package's install-time code would otherwise run before anything read the set.
C1454	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The build runner resolves and fetches first, with install-time scripts disabled where the toolchain allows it, writes the set, and reads the source constraint in force against it before it runs anything: a package from a source the constraint excludes stops the build there, recorded as a build that did not run, and Implementation rejects on that record.
C1455	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The licence constraint and the advisory match are decided at the gate as before, over a set the fetch produced and the build did not alter.
C1456	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Where a toolchain cannot separate fetching from running, the coverage says so, and a build on it resolves a human to Implementation the way a set without digests does.
C1457	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	At [the queue's re-verification](03-the-merge-queue.md) the re-resolved set's digests are compared to the approved build's too, and a difference rejects there on the terms a candidate that fails its own merits already does: the item goes back up to Implementation and counts an attempt.
C1458	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Recovering it is per toolchain, so a resolver is a component of the factory shipped with it, and which toolchains have one is published as a fact of the factory's version, the arrangement [_What a consumer declares_](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) gives its extractors.
C1459	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A lockfile names the declared ecosystem's packages and nothing else, so the build records what the resolver covered beside the set: which ecosystems it read, whether the base image's packages, vendored source and statically linked code were enumerated, and per entry whether the package is present at run time or only at build time.
C1460	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	Where no resolver covers the build, or resolution fails outright, the field is **could not derive**, a record and not an empty set, because "nothing vulnerable was resolved" and "nothing resolved was visible" call for opposite responses.
C1461	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Every reader of the set reads the coverage with it, and none reads an absence as clean.
C1462	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [advisory](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md) detector matches only inside what was covered, and a service whose current release's set is could not derive or covers less than the feeds watched is listed as unassessed rather than clear.
C1463	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A licence [constraint](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) over such a build cannot be decided mechanically and resolves a human to Implementation the way an [unavailable factor](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) does, the vector naming what the resolver did not cover.
C1464	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [exposure](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) factor reads a could-not-derive set as that factor unavailable, resolving the same way, and never as no dependency change, which would lower the number on the build the factory knows least about.
C1465	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The build runner produces one more field from the resolved set, in the same write: a **notice file** naming every package's source, version and declared licence.
C1466	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Where the resolved set is could not derive, or its coverage leaves out what the file would list, the notice file is could not derive too, and the release naming this build reads the same at [Merge to master](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md).
C1467	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A build in a project with screens names one thing more: the [design system](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) constraint record in force for that project when the build was performed, written by the build runner beside the resolved set and for the same reason.
C1468	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A constraint is never edited, so the record is the version.
C1469	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The [search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md)'s builds, which are of commits on no branch, are the exception: a search build names the record the release it was built from named, so what the search's exit isolates is the revert's change and not a design system that moved since.
C1470	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	What a run on a candidate environment produced attaches to the build, written by the deployer when the run finishes and not a record of its own: each criterion decided, and each consumer contract checked against the exchanges observed there.
C1471	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The composition in force at the run is copied onto the build with them, from the field [_An environment per candidate_](02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) has the deployer write, because that field is rewritten at a recomposition and what an earlier run ran against survives on the build or nowhere.
C1472	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	Which environment the run happened on is the item's, which the build names — the build could not name one itself, being written when the implementation stage finishes, one gate above the approval that creates the environment.
C1473	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The identity is the build, the run, and the criterion id.
C1474	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A run is one deploy of the build onto the item's environment, numbered per build by the deployer in the order it performed them, and the composition is copied per run and not per build.
C1475	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A gate reads the build's latest run, and every earlier run stands with the composition it was decided against, which is what [undecided](04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md), the queue's three readings, and the criterion's [outcome history](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) each compare.
C1476	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The producer's own [contract diff](../07-contracts/04-enforcement.md) is not among them.
C1477	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	What is written is what was observed; what is decided is decided at the gate.
C1478	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	The encodings that declare no environment are the exception on the other side: the build runner decides them in the build's own process when it performs the build, and writes their results onto the build beside the resolved set, so the [Implementation gate](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) reads them before any environment exists.
C1479	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	At [the queue's re-verification](03-the-merge-queue.md) the new build carries them again, and one failing there fails the candidate as a criterion failing on the recomposed environment does.
C1480	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	The build runner performs a build in a process of its own holding nothing of the factory's: no target, no credential, no reach to the store, the log, or the resolver, and network reach to the sources the set names and to nothing else.
C1481	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	stated	Wherever the build runs, a container runtime or the host itself, that boundary is a requirement on the host, stated the way the artifact's reachability is.
C1482	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	A package's install-time code and a build plugin run inside it, so a compromised dependency reaches the clone it was fetched for and the artifact it builds, and never the factory's records or another service's credential.
C1483	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	unbuilt M2	An encoding declared for the build that needed an environment fails there rather than passing against nothing.
C1484	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md	built	A criterion the build decided is never repeated over one build: [undecided](04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md) cannot arise for it, and an encoding of that kind that cannot hold its answer shows only as two builds disagreeing.
C1485	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/01-the-store-and-the-configuration.md	unbuilt M3	Its [store](../../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) is created with it, and what that store holds is stated.
C1486	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/01-the-store-and-the-configuration.md	unbuilt M3	The deployer applies the service's schema to it the way a production deploy would: every change master carries plus the item's own, the same step and the same actor [the deploy record names](../../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md).
C1487	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/01-the-store-and-the-configuration.md	built	What the store starts with is authored per service on the [service record](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), beside [the explicit threshold](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) and at the same cost: a snapshot the owner has anonymised, or synthetic rows they supply, and where they author nothing it starts empty.

C1489	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/01-the-store-and-the-configuration.md	unbuilt M3	A check the run decided over no row is [undecided](../04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md), the reading [_The store is a contract too_](../../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) gives a predicate no path exercised, and undecided rejects at Merge to master.

C1520	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md	built	Nothing is shared, so nothing queues behind anything: a candidate being repaired delays nobody, and a candidate waiting on a human delays only itself.

C1530	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	So merging is a queue: a candidate entering it re-verifies against master plus every candidate ahead of it, and fast-forwards only if that passes.
C1531	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	A failure invalidates the speculation behind it, and those candidates re-verify against the master that actually resulted.
C1532	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	A candidate that fails its own re-verification — against the master that actually resulted, not against a speculation ahead of it — is rejected by the queue: the item [goes back up](../03-gates/06-going-back-up.md) [the pipeline](../01-one-pipeline.md) to Implementation, the queue naming no other stage and there being none between, and counts an attempt there against [_the limit_](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) — the rework is real work, which is what the limit bounds.
C1533	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	unbuilt M3	Candidates behind a failure count nothing, for the same reason they re-verify without an attempt counted against them: they failed because of someone else.
C1534	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	That it failed on its merits is established rather than assumed, because a re-verification is never a repeat of the run that passed: the build is new and the environment recomposed.
C1535	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	unbuilt M3	So a criterion that passed on the candidate environment and fails here changed its answer between two runs that differ, and what differed is on the record — each run's results name [the composition it was performed against](01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), the [seed and value-set versions](02-an-environment-per-candidate/01-the-store-and-the-configuration.md) among the releases it names, and each build its commit.
C1536	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	Such a criterion is run once more, over the re-verification's own build and composition — once, and never until green.
C1537	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	A repeat that disagrees is the disagreement over one build that makes a criterion [undecided](04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md), and the way back is authoring the criterion's encoding again — the check [_Implementation_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) authors beside the code to decide it.
C1538	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	The score learns from it the way it learns from a [reject](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), an encoding that cannot hold its answer being a defect of the item's own build.
C1539	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	unbuilt M3	A failure that repeats is real, and what the score learns turns on the criterion and the two compositions: an [unreliable](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) one teaches nothing.
C1540	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	Where they match, the candidate failed against the master it will actually merge into, and the score learns as from a reject.
C1541	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	Where a dependency's release moved between the runs, the score learns as from a [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) and the [per-author prior](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) does not move: what changed is a release the author's work never saw, and a prior kept per model version and factory-wide would carry one moved dependency's noise onto every item that model authors.
C1542	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	The rejection the queue writes into the log names which reading it was, and the moved release where there is one, so the one rejection [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md)'s gate rejection rate cannot see is legible on the item's timeline in [_Work_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C1543	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	The attempt counts on every exit, so an item a moving dependency keeps rejecting still walks toward an [escalation](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) — deliberately: work the environment will not let finish is work a human should see, and the limit bounds spend, which the rework is.
C1544	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	On the third reading the attempt and the prior part ways: counted like a reject, taught like a hold, a combination no gate produces.
C1545	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	A re-verification also compares one field no criterion reads: where the two builds name a [design system](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) constraint record and the records differ on a component or a token the candidate's build uses, the candidate fails whatever its criteria answered.
C1546	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	The rejection takes the reading a moved dependency takes, whatever else failed beside the move: the score learns as from a hold, the per-author prior does not move, the attempt counts, and the log entry names the replaced record, with the moved release where one also moved — an owner's move being no defect of the author's.
C1547	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	The comparison is of two fields, and a build naming no design-system record compares as nothing.
C1548	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	The queue restores what one shared environment used to provide — the commit that was verified is the commit that merges.

C1556	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md	built	Master's head the queue reads from git, the system that holds it.

C1558	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	built	A criterion has a third outcome and it is not a kind of pass.
C1559	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	built	An encoding that produced a failure and a pass over the same build decided nothing, because what a repeated run measures is the repetition, so the criterion is **undecided** for that build and undecided rejects the way a failure does.
C1560	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	built	For a criterion the disagreement is between two [runs](../01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) over one build whose compositions match, and there are two ways to get one: [the queue's confirming run](../03-the-merge-queue.md), which repeats a re-verification over its own build and composition, and a reclaimed environment composed again for the same build with no dependency moved.
C1561	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	built	Two runs over one build against compositions that differ are two answers to two questions and make nothing undecided; the gate reads the latest, and the queue's three readings say what the difference taught.
C1562	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	The confirming run happens after the Merge to master gate approved, so a criterion undecided there is rejected at the queue, and one undecided by a recomposition is rejected at Merge to master.
C1563	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	What still reads at that gate the way a failure does is a [consumer contract](../../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) or a [store declaration](../../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) undecided by a path the run never exercised, whose disagreement needs no repeat.
C1564	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	A predicate-kind [constraint](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) reads the same way, and the [screen state machines](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/04-the-screen-state-machine.md) in force for the build enumerate where it is decided.
C1565	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	Each of their states is a place the predicates are decided, once per condition the [design system](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md)'s content declares: an appearance, a viewport width, a text scale, a text direction.
C1566	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	The run drives the screen into each as [_Implementation_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) authored.
C1567	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	Within a render, each component the state shows is put in each state the design system's contents field lists for it, and the predicates are decided there too.
C1568	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	A declared state the run did not render in every declared condition leaves every predicate over that screen undecided, and undecided rejects at Merge to master the way an unexercised consumer contract does.
C1569	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	Each verdict names the state, the condition, and the component states it was decided in, so a pass is a pass at a named width and appearance and never at whichever the run happened to open.
C1570	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	A build whose items declare no screen has no state to decide anything over, so an item on a service with no user interface is stopped by nothing.
C1571	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	What it does not reach is a screen no state machine declares: the predicates decide nothing there, and what is left is the diff a [design system](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) arriving as code carries.
C1572	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	A run that could not be performed produced no outcome, this third one included, and what could not be performed is narrow: the environment failed to compose, or a dependency it needed was unreachable before the run began.
C1573	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	A run that composed, started, and ended without the encodings finishing is not among them: the item's own service not answering is behaviour of the build, and its criteria fail as criteria.
C1574	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	For the narrow kind, nothing is recorded against any criterion, no attempt is counted, and the run is performed again: an absent input is not a failed check, the reading [an unavailable factor](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) already gets, recorded as absent rather than read as a result.
C1575	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	A run whose second attempt also could not be performed stops retrying and waits, [a wait no gate could compute](../../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), written into the log by the log with the deployer as caller and actor, counting no attempt and teaching the score nothing, read in [_Work_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) like any other wait.
C1576	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md	unbuilt M3	What ends it is the run performing.
C1577	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	Merge to master is where a candidate becomes a release and gets its number.
C1578	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	Everything from there is machine: numbering, strategy selection, rollout, monitoring, rollback.
C1579	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	The verdict is score-gated like every other gate, so it is not the last place a human decides and there is no last one: the same score decides at each gate whether a human decides there, and a safeguard (9) puts one back.
C1580	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	stated	Where one is there, what they are doing is [UAT](../../../what-humans-do.md) (7) — the environment is no longer named for it, because in the steady state nobody is there.
C1581	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	Where it auto-passes, the verdict is the factory's own, taken against acceptance criteria authored from a reading of the [intent](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) that the person who asked confirmed at [the interview's last round](../../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md).
C1582	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	The criteria are confirmed by a human (6) only where the score or a safeguard put one at [Spec](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md), which in the steady state it does not, an intent grouped from [reports](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) excepted.
C1583	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	Its source is resolved rather than weighed there, so a human is at that gate whatever the number is.
C1584	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	stated	The alternative was to separate that verdict by origin, permanent for human-originated features, auto-passable for factory-originated fixes, and that is the second, invisible path the pipeline forbids, separated by a worse predictor than the score's own factors.
C1585	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	What catches a wrong thing built well is the person who asked confirming the factory's reading of the intent at [the interview's last round](../../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md), which is not score-gated, and the criteria, where the score or a safeguard put a human at the [Spec gate](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) to confirm them (6).
C1586	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	The same list includes end users (4, 5) and [a human undoing the change after it shipped](../../../what-humans-do.md) (10); an owner who wants more adds any of the four back per service or per area with a safeguard.
C1587	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	None of the four is present on every item.
C1588	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	The first is absent wherever nobody asked: an intent the factory raised itself takes no [last round](../../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md), its evidence being the request, so an item decomposed from a detector's intent has the first on no path at all, and reaches production with none of the four wherever the second is absent too.
C1589	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	The second is absent in the steady state except on the reports path, where the source resolves a human to Spec and it is present on every item.
C1590	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	Which of them an item gets is therefore read off the decisions that fired and not assumed from the source: a detector's intent never carries the first, and whether it carries the second is whether that item's Spec decision closed on a human's verdict, which the row says and no rule about the source predicts.
C1591	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	A number above the threshold usually puts one there and does not always: an item the [held-out sample](../../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) selected auto-passes the gates the score would have gated, and its **Why it auto-passed** field is what tells that from a number under the threshold.
C1592	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/02-the-verdict.md	unbuilt M3	A safeguard and a [resolved factor](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) each put one there whatever the sample did.
C1593	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	unbuilt M3	Up to the merge, what moves is a candidate and deploys are plain: no strategy, no rollout, and no [_analysis window_](../../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), because a candidate environment has no organic traffic, and a comparison computed from one human exercising a screen is noise that looks like evidence.
C1594	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	unbuilt M3	A [quality criterion](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) is not that comparison: it is decided under load its own encoding generates, an absolute reading establishing nothing past this environment's hardware.
C1595	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	unbuilt M3	What the environment decides is every criterion whose encoding declares it, as [_Implementation_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) states.
C1596	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	built	A criterion whose encoding declares the build was decided by [the build runner](../01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) as it built; this environment decides the rest.
C1597	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	unbuilt M3	The candidate runs on production-like infrastructure against its dependencies' current releases, every consumer contract is checked against it, and the predicate-kind [constraints](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) in force are decided against what the same run shows.
C1598	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	built	The factory's own list of security predicates is decided against the same run, beside the criteria, with one set of derivations per toolchain; a toolchain whose derivation could not run reads as [could not derive](../../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md).
C1599	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	unbuilt M3	A [store's](../../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) is checked the same way, against a state rather than an exchange: the store the run leaves behind, seeded as [_An environment per candidate_](../02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) states, carrying the item's own schema change.
C1600	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md	unbuilt M3	A criterion that fails is a rejection at the Merge to master gate on the same terms a consumer contract failing there is, [unreliable](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) criteria excepted.
C1601	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	A second [release](../06-releases/02-the-release-record.md) naming one service and one number is refused where the records are kept, so a duplicate mint is a failed write and never two releases answering to one name.
C1602	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	And the fast-forward and the record write are one operation restartable from either side: the record is keyed on the commit, so writing it again writes nothing, and on start the queue reads [master](01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) before it mints, every start, a crash and an upgrade of the self-hosted factory being the same stop.
C1603	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	What it reads is master against the service's release records, and it reads the same way before every mint.
C1604	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	Master's head being the commit the service's newest release names is the ordinary case.
C1605	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	A commit on master past that one is compared against the [builds](01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) the records hold.
C1606	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	One that a build of a candidate approved at [Merge to master](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) names is the queue's own unfinished merge, and the queue completes it in master's order with the write the fast-forward already implied.
C1607	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	One no such build names is a commit the queue did not put there: a human's push, an agent's, or a merge whose records a restore lost.
C1608	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	It is told from the queue's own by the records and not by the queue's memory of its work.
C1609	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	On that commit the queue mints nothing for the service and writes [a wait no gate could compute](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) into [the log](../../deferred.md), naming the service and the commit.
C1610	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	unbuilt M2	The wait has no holder, so it widens to the owner and goes out by mail or chat; it [pages](../08-operations/07-pages.md) nobody, since production is no worse for it, and it stops work rather than production the way [a fleet entry with no credential](../10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md) does.
C1611	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	Master returned to the commit the newest release names is repository administration, outside the factory as [_What the factory does not build_](../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) places it, and the queue reads it at its next pass.
C1612	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	Or a human accepts the commit at [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), a log row naming them as [actor](../../deferred.md), and the queue builds it, re-verifies it as it re-verifies a candidate, the [contract checks](../07-contracts/04-enforcement.md) included, and mints its release in master's order naming the build and no item.
C1613	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	A re-verification that fails leaves the wait standing with the failure on it.
C1614	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	The queue writes the [contract version](../07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md) each of its interfaces publishes in the same write as the release, as it does at a fast-forward, the producer's contract diff being among the checks the re-verification ran, so a consumer reaching an interface the commit changed reads a version naming that release.
C1615	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	A release naming no item is one no gate decided: no [consumer contract](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) is derived for it, since none is authored at a stage it never passed, its [authorship rollup](../06-releases/02-the-release-record.md) names nothing the factory wrote, no [prior](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) moves, and every traversal from it ends at the acceptance rather than at an intent.
C1616	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	A release record naming a commit master does not hold is git restored behind the graph, the members of the [recovery unit](../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) having landed apart.
C1617	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	The queue mints nothing for the service and writes the same wait, naming the release and its commit, and the wait ends only when master holds the commit again, from the repository restored to a later point or from any clone that holds it, because a release is written once and cannot be unwritten to match.
C1618	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	So the single recovery point the unit requires is checked at every start rather than assumed: members restored apart are a service held at the queue, and never a candidate branched from a master missing work already deployed.
C1619	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	unbuilt M2	A restore leaves the deploy targets and the artifact store where they were, both outside the unit, so production keeps running releases the restored records no longer hold.
C1620	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	unbuilt M2	Every production [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) falls behind what runs, which is a [mismatch](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) per service that pages and holds already, and the reading above finds master whole, git having been restored with the graph.
C1621	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	The [install event](../../deferred.md) says there was a restore, so the first mint on a service after one takes the higher of two readings and adds one: the highest number among the service's release records, and the highest number among the releases of that service the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md)'s store names.
C1622	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	The mint writes the numbers it skipped into the log, so the gap stands beside the install event rather than as a puzzle on [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C1623	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	built	And every merge to a service is held whenever master holds a commit the queue did not make or lacks one it did, until a human ends the wait or master is whole again.
C1624	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	unbuilt M2	One case the reading cannot cover: a release that served no request between its deploy and the restore emitted nothing, so its number is taken by the next mint.
C1625	how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md	unbuilt M2	Two builds then answer to one name until the drift detector's digest comparison, which reads bytes and not names, holds that service and a human reads the install event beside the mismatch.
C1626	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md	built	**One item per release. Always, at every stage, permanently.**
