C1627	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md	unbuilt M1	The single timeline of an [item](../01-one-pipeline.md) never splits: [rollout](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) stays item-scoped like everything before it, and a human's [undo of a shipped change](../../what-humans-do.md) (10) is the rollback of exactly one item rather than extracting one change from a set of ten.
C1628	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md	unbuilt M1	One exception, limited and named, and it runs in both directions: master is linear, so where analysis windows overlap a rollback undoes every release above its target, up to the [window limit](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) — and the revert release that follows redelivers the ones that were only [skipped](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), which were never failed and whose code is still on master.
C1629	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md	unbuilt M1	One deploy then delivers several items' changes, measured by one window against the restored build.
C1630	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md	unbuilt M1	What ships is still one item, and each half is bounded: the rollback by the window limit, and the revert's deploy by the [backlog cap](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), which counts every release that deploy would deliver — the skipped ones and the ones merged while the revert is authored alike, merging not being held — and is the window limit until an owner separates them.
C1631	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md	unbuilt M1	An environment added later follows the rule rather than changing it.
C1632	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	A release is a record, and it is where [the graph](../../what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) joins.
C1633	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	It names what is known at the merge: the item that caused it, and the build and commit it is made of.
C1634	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	The [contract versions](../07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md) it publishes are not a field of it — they name the release instead, each carrying that release's own number, because ordering versions is ordering releases and answering "the version in force at release N" by reading release records would make every reader of a contract a reader of releases.
C1635	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	A version that names the release cannot also be a column here without the same fact standing in two places, written at one event by one writer.
C1636	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	unbuilt M1	So "the release names the contract versions it publishes" is an inbound edge, which is what every deploy of a release already is, and the cost is the one this record's single writer already carries: a reader asking what a release publishes indexes that edge rather than reading a field.
C1637	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	unbuilt M1	The [gate decisions](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) name the item and the build and never the release, whether they were written before it existed or, at the [production deploy gate](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md), after; every deploy of it to every [environment](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) is written afterwards and names the release.
C1638	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	unbuilt M1	Ask anything about a shipped change — from what [intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), on whose approval, under which [policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md), running where, rolled back when — and the answer is reached by traversing edges at the release record, outbound or inbound.
C1639	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	[_The merge queue_](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) writes it and mints the release number with it.
C1640	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	Master's only inbound path is the queue, so the fast-forward is the event and the queue is what performs it, and the serialization that stops two merges taking one number is the per-service ordering the queue keeps already.
C1641	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	A commit that reached master by another path gets a release record from the same writer only once a human accepts it, and that record names a build and no item, the treatment [_What the queue reads before it mints_](../05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md) states; until then the service's merges are held.
C1642	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	It is written once and never written again, which is what keeps that one writer.
C1643	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	unbuilt M1	The release's notice is one such inbound edge: the **notice file** the build runner produced from the build's [resolved set](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), read by following the release's own link to the build it is made of.
C1644	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	unbuilt M1	Where the build carries no notice file because the resolved set [could not derive](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md), the release reads could not derive too, which [Merge to master](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) already treats like an undecided criterion.
C1645	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	built	A release's authorship rollup is also a query and not a field: which of the three [authorship attributes](../01-one-pipeline.md) wrote each stage of the item this release names, read from the [artifact store](../01-one-pipeline.md)'s record of that item's artifact versions.
C1646	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md	stated	[_What the customer runs it under_](../../what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md) reads it the way it reads the rest: a fact a rights question over the produced software asks for, answered from a record rather than argued.
C1647	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md	unbuilt M1	A build [the search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) calls for is not a third row: made by [the build runner](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) from commits master keeps, on no branch, it is never a candidate and never merges, so it takes neither name and is named only by the deploy record that carries it.
C1648	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md	unbuilt M1	A [mutant](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) is not a third row either: the deployer alters a candidate's artifact and deploys the alteration onto the item's own environment, no build record is written and no commit exists for one, so it takes neither name and survives only in the mutation score it feeds.
C1649	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md	unbuilt M1	A rejected candidate never needed a release number, and the number is not a third row here either: it is a field of the release, minted at the same event, and [_The release number_](04-the-release-number.md) is where it is set out.
C1650	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md	unbuilt M1	A build has one name at a time and the vocabulary stays at two, however many environments it runs on: a customer who defines five pre-production environments still has candidate and release, one build, and five deploy records — the names do not multiply with the environments.
C1651	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	The **release number** is an ordinal, per service, assigned at merge to master.
C1652	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	It orders builds and names rollback targets, and does no more; compatibility is the contract's business, not the release's.
C1653	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	Numbers are never reused.
C1654	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	A release that is rolled back keeps its number, and the fix that follows takes the next one.
C1655	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	Never reused is enforced where the records are kept and not only by the queue: a second release naming one service and one number is a refused write.
C1656	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	A numbered release that has never run anywhere is normal, not an anomaly.
C1657	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	The release number is minted at merge, one gate before production, so it records that a change was accepted — not that it is live.
C1658	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	Where a release is running is a deploy record and never the release number.
C1659	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	Master's only inbound path is [_The merge queue_](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md), and a candidate fast-forwards only after re-verifying against the master it will actually merge into.
C1660	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	The commit that was verified is the commit on master.
C1661	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	A commit that reached master by another path is found by [the reading the queue makes before every mint](../05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md) and is never numbered unseen: the queue holds the service until a human accepts the commit, and the release it then mints names no item, so a release no gate decided is readable as such.
C1662	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md	built	The queue's own re-verification still runs on it as on a candidate.
C1663	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	A deploy that ships a schema change names it.
C1664	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	built	The [store rule](../../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) makes a breaking change five items, so what one deploy carries is one item's change, applied by the [deployer](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) to the service's store before the build takes any traffic, through the credential the [environment's record](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) holds — the deployer is the only component that reaches a deploy [target](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), and the store is reached the same way.
C1665	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	The revert's deploy is the one deploy that can carry more: delivering releases that never deployed on their own, it applies each delivered release's schema change that no deploy applied, in release order, before its build takes traffic.
C1666	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	The record says whether the change completed, so a schema change is on the trail an [incident](../../08-operations/06-incidents.md)'s links walk.
C1667	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	A change that fails to apply stops the deploy before any traffic shifts: no target is marked complete, the previous release stays current, and the failure stands on the record for [Ops](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C1668	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	Of the five items, the added form is read by nothing until the backfill's record marks it complete, and the item that moves reads to the new form ships after that; the backfill writes only what the old form already holds and is rerun from where it stopped.
C1669	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	built	The drop ran only once no [declaration in force](../../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) named the old form and the backfill's record marked it complete, so a store between two forms is one that every release a rollback can restore still reads and writes.
C1670	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	built	Before it applies a change that destroys stored data (the drop, and any diff the store rule forbids without one), the deployer takes a snapshot of the store through the credential the environment's record holds, verifies it, and names it on the deploy record beside the change.
C1671	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	A snapshot the deployer cannot take and verify is a deploy not performed: the deploy record is marked failed at that step, no target is marked complete, and a [page](../../08-operations/07-pages.md) fires, the exit an unverifiable digest already takes in [_Rollback_](../06-rollback.md).
C1672	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	built	The snapshot is kept on the service's own store hosting, so the deploy record says where what the change destroyed can still be read.
C1673	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	What it costs: such an item cannot ship where the service's store offers no snapshot until the owner supplies one.
C1674	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	built	A snapshot is a whole copy of the store, and the one erasure it can take is its own deletion: a [redaction](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) names words inside a record the factory wrote, and nothing the factory holds says which rows of a customer's store are one end user's.
C1675	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	The deployer deletes it at the end of the [retention](../../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) the [service record](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) authors, on its own pass, or earlier when an owner calls for it from [Ops](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) on the deploy record that names it, the call the deployer already takes for a [mitigation](../../../deferred.md).
C1676	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	Either way it writes the deletion on the deploy record beside the snapshot's name, so the record says where what the change destroyed could be read and, after that, that it no longer can.
C1677	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	The snapshot sits on the service's store hosting, outside the factory's [recovery unit](../../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md), so a restore of the factory's records brings none back and the [erasure list](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) carries no row for one; whether that hosting copies it is [substrate](../../../what-humans-do.md).
C1678	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	built	Which changes a store carries is read from the [schema history](../../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) the deployer keeps in the store, never from this record: a deploy applies the changes its build declares that the history lacks, and the record reports what it did.
C1679	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	unbuilt M3	The store is one per service per environment, and that is a shape the factory refuses to widen rather than an assumption.
C1680	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md	stated	A service whose [targets](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) each hold a store of their own, a database per region or a set of shards, is refused at [adoption](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) and at decomposition the way [_What the factory does not build_](../../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) refuses a library or a scheduled pipeline.
C1681	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	A production deploy record also names, per [target](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), how many instances of [the release a rollback of this one would return to](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) the [deployer](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) is keeping there: the capacity that release had, times [the fraction its owner authored](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md).
C1682	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	The deployer writes it when the deploy starts, which is when the window opens over it, and it is the only fact about the rollback path that exists before the rollback does.
C1683	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	unbuilt M3	[_Drift detection_](../../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) reads what runs against what the record names, and a count no record holds is one nothing can be read against.
C1684	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	**Instance-hours** is the same fact carried into a duration.
C1685	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	Three sets of instances run for spans this record already dates — the release's own, the [control](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md)'s, both named on it, and the kept instances above.
C1686	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	The dates are when the deploy started and the window opened over it, and when a kept or a control fleet is torn down.
C1687	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	Instance-hours per release sums that count across that span for the three together, a recorded fact of fields and timestamps the record already carries and not a value computed apart from them.
C1688	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	Where the [service record](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) carries an instance-hour rate, an owner-authored figure in currency per instance-hour, in force at a given write, the deployer writes the converted amount for that span beside the dates, fixed at the write and never repriced by a rate corrected later.
C1689	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	unbuilt M3	[_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports it beside [environment-hours](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md), per service and per item.
C1690	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	built	At deploy the deployer also records on the deploy record a digest over the resolved value set of the service's configuration, beside the build's digest, computed through the resolver that seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../../deferred.md) names.
C1691	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md	unbuilt M3	The configuration version so named is restored with the release at a rollback.
C1692	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/03-a-control-above-a-release.md	built	A control started above a release runs the release [a rollback of it would return to](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) — the newest release below it whose window closed without [failing](../../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) it, past every release a rollback would descend past, and not the ordinal predecessor.
C1693	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/03-a-control-above-a-release.md	unbuilt M3	Defining both by one rule is what [_Overlapping windows_](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) needs so that the build the comparison is made against and the build a rollback returns to are the same one, and neither is something the factory has already ruled out.
C1694	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	A service's **current release** is the one its production deploy record marks complete on every production [target the service runs on](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) — what is running, not what is newest.
C1695	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	Merged-and-never-deployed being normal is exactly what makes those different facts, and every cross-service check reads the current one: an environment composed from its dependencies is composed from what runs, and a promise is kept by what serves it.
C1696	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	A deploy record is written by the [deployer](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), the one component that reaches a deploy target, through seam 4 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../../deferred.md), when the deploy starts, and marked per target as the deploy advances, with the values set out below.
C1697	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	The record names the release it puts on the environment once, and beside each of the environment's targets whether that target has it yet.
C1698	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	A revert's deploy also lists the releases it delivers: the ones the [hold](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) was holding, whose code is in its build.
C1699	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	That list sits beside the one release the record names, the shape [_Rollback_](../06-rollback.md)'s record already keeps for the releases it undid, so a release delivered by a later release's deploy is on a deploy record and not only on master.
C1700	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	A deploy [the search](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) calls for names the build and no release, there being none.
C1701	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	What it names as the thing deployed is the build it put there, and the release as well wherever a release exists to deploy: [_Deploy to candidate environment_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/06-deploy-to-candidate-environment.md) fires one gate before the release number exists, so a record naming a release there would name one nothing had minted.
C1702	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	Naming the build on every deploy is also what makes what is running comparable to what the record says: a target reports the build it is running and not the name that build has on master, which is a fact of this store and not of the target.
C1703	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Every reader of what is running reads the records that name a release, a [candidate environment](../../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) being a place where nothing is current.
C1704	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	One rollout produces one, and [_Rollback_](../06-rollback.md) is a deploy event and produces its own.
C1705	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Each deploy record carries a digest of the token the deployer minted for the [way in](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) at that deploy, and never the token itself; the way in calls the report store under that token, as seam 5 states.
C1706	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Beside that token the deployer hands every instance it places the record's own identity, which is what [the health monitor](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md)'s emission names to tell the control's instances from the ones of the same build placed by an earlier deploy.
C1707	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	No agent performs a deploy: deploying is not a stage an agent is dispatched to, so the record's [actor](../../../deferred.md) is the deployer and never an agent — a program the factory runs and can check, where which model an agent is is only what its [fleet entry](../../10-fleet/README.md) says.
C1708	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	What that costs is that an agent's [scope](../../01-one-pipeline.md) says nothing about a deploy target, so where a deploy may go is authored on the [environment record](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) and never on an entry.
C1709	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	What the record does not say is what share of traffic each build takes — that is the rollout's and its [_control_](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md)'s, and a deploy log recording every shift would scale with the schedule for a fact only the rollout reads while it runs.
C1710	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	It is keyed by service and environment and not by [target](../../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md): one record names one release for the whole environment, and completion is a field per target on that record rather than a record per target.
C1711	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	A record per target stays refused, because four records could each name a different release and each be right; one record with one release and completion per target cannot.
C1712	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	What one record with completion per target expresses is the state the earlier shape could not: a deploy that reached three targets of four is a recorded partial deploy, the fourth target marked not yet complete, rather than an incomplete one caught as a mismatch after the fact.
C1713	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	[_Drift detection_](../../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) reads each production target against what the record marks for that target, so a target marked complete and serving the old build is a mismatch whether or not a window is open.
C1714	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Every cross-service reader reads completion per target and needs a rule for what current means while they differ, and the rule is the strictest: a release is current only once every production target is marked complete, and until then the previous release is.
C1715	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	A consumer whose [declared dependency](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) is on three targets of four is held at [_Deploy to production_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) until the fourth lands, which is what stops one call in four reaching a producer without the [contract](../../07-contracts/README.md) form the consumer verified against.
C1716	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Service and environment are the record's grain and not its identity: a rollout, a rollback, a revert's deploy, and a deploy [the search](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) calls for each write one for the same pair.
C1717	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	A record is identified by that pair and a sequence number the deployer assigns per pair as it begins each deploy, so two deploys of one release onto one environment are two records.
C1718	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	The step the deployer's [restart](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) must not repeat is keyed on the record and the target, never on the release and the target: a rollback returning to a release whose earlier deploy completed is a new record whose targets start unmarked, and a search's deploy, naming a build and no release, is keyed the same way.
C1719	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	What the sequence number costs is one more part to the key, and a reader of current release ordering by release number and never by it.
C1720	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Every deploy the deployer begins has a record from its first step, the steps before traffic included: a [schema change](01-a-schema-change.md), the snapshot before a destructive one, and the digest [_Rollback_](../06-rollback.md)'s slow way verifies.
C1721	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Per target the record holds one of three values: not reached, complete, or rolled back, the last written target by target as the record of the rollback that undid it completes on each, so a rollback that stopped undoes nothing on the record.
C1722	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	The record as a whole is marked failed where the deployer stopped it before any target was complete, naming the step that stopped it; that is where a failure stands for [Ops](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), what the restart leaves alone, and what [_Overlapping windows_](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md)' two queries descend past.
C1723	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Every reader of what is running reads a target marked complete, so a failed record moves no reader.
C1724	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	unbuilt M3	A deploy the health monitor never calls for begins nothing and has no record: a failed exit that finds no release to return to, or one while a mismatch stands, is recorded by the [page](../../08-operations/07-pages.md) at that exit and by nothing else.
C1725	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	Current is the highest-numbered release whose deploy completed and was not rolled back, and not the most recently started, and it is none once a [removal](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md)'s record, naming no release, is complete on every target; the two disagree exactly while a rollout runs.
C1726	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md	built	The number rather than the completion time is what orders them, because rollouts overlap and differ in length.
C1727	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	Rollback is a deploy event, not a version event: it shifts traffic onto the instances of [_the release it returns to_](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), still running at full capacity, and writes a deploy record, minting and retiring no number.
C1728	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	It applies no schema change either — the schema moves only forward, staying at the newest release's form however far traffic moves back, which is what the store's [forward promise](../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) exists to make survivable.
C1729	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	It is not a record of its own — every field is on the deploy record already, and a second writer on the fact of what is running is the fact the drift detector exists to check.
C1730	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	That record names the failed release as a field distinct from the list of releases the rollback undid, whose own deploy records the same event advances to rolled back on every target, a target the release never reached included, because the hold and the one-revert-per-failed-release rule both need the two apart.
C1731	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	It also names a **source** beside its [actor](../../deferred.md), the shape [intake](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) already uses: the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) at the [failed](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) exit, or the named human at [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) with the reason they state.
C1732	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	The actor stays the deployer that performed it.
C1733	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Which release that is is [_the rollback's target_](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), and it is not always the ordinal predecessor — where windows overlap, the release below a bad one may still be under watch itself.
C1734	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Where the rollout kept no control, the same target is reached the slow way, by redeploying the build.
C1735	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	That the restored build still works is not luck: [no item may break the store](../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) it runs on, and that rule applies in both directions and over writes as well as reads — what the newer release wrote while it was live is still readable by the one being restored, and what the restored one writes still lands, a constraint its declared writes would violate being a diff [the declarations in force](../07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) rejected at the merge.
C1736	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	The slow way redeploys the artifact the target's [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) names by digest, and the deployer verifies that digest before it deploys.
C1737	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Where the digest differs, the artifact store no longer holds what the build recorded, so the deployer shifts no traffic, marks the rollback's deploy record failed at that step, and the failed release keeps serving.
C1738	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	It [pages](../08-operations/07-pages.md) at that exit, the condition a target credential unreachable during a rollback already meets: production is running a release the factory has just failed, and nothing the factory has will improve it.
C1739	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	stated	How long an artifact stays reachable is a requirement on the host, stated as a shape the way the span of [the store the health monitor reads](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) is: at least while its release could be named as [the rollback's target](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md).
C1740	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	Per service that is the build of the target computed below the oldest open [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), every build above it, and the [last known-good release](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md)'s build always.
C1741	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	stated	A build older than the floor is retained as a matter of cost or not at all, the host's to decide.
C1742	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	A revert is an ordinary item [decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) writes, from an intent intake wrote at the rollback — the health monitor calling intake at the failed exit, the named human at Ops calling it when they ask for one; "revert" names what raised its intent and nothing about the record.
C1743	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Nothing on the item says it is one, on the precedent that [the project is not a field](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) — the release it reverts is reachable through the intent's [evidence](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), and a field would let the two disagree.
C1744	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Intake writes that link the same way for a human's revert as for the health monitor's: the human at Ops names the failed release when asking for one, and the intent links it.
C1745	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Either source is reached the same way and told apart by source alone, and a halt or freeze passes a revert by reading the link, never the source, as [_Stopping the factory_](../09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) reads it.
C1746	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	One failed release is one revert item, and an owner undoing a whole [partly delivered intent](../02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md) asks for one intent decomposed into one revert item per shipped sibling.
C1747	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Where a failed exit finds no release to return to, the health monitor calls for no deploy, so none is recorded: the failed release keeps serving, and it raises the revert intent itself and [pages](../08-operations/07-pages.md) at that exit.
C1748	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	A failed exit while a [mismatch](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) holds the service's production deploys takes this same treatment, for the reason [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) states: the target would be computed from the records just shown wrong.
C1749	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	A rollback restores code and no effect.
C1750	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	It shifts traffic onto the instances of an earlier build, so what the failed release did while it was serving stands: an amount posted, a message published, a notification sent, a row erased, an actuator driven.
C1751	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	What the [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) watches is the rate at which the software fails and how long it takes to answer, which is what the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) reads, and it watches nothing else.
C1752	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	So in an area whose [hazard severity](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md) is above `negligible`, a rollback returns the traffic and never the state, and what was done is undone by an item or by nothing.
C1753	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	A rollback restores the configuration version the deploy record named for that release beside its code, the digest being what the slow way's redeploy resolves again and what the fast way already has running.
C1754	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	Undoing a release whose window has closed is not a rollback.
C1755	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	The instances of the release it would return to are gone, master keeps the change, and the correction is a revert — a new item, its own timeline, its own number.
C1756	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	When the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) is the thing that is wrong, no path inside the factory recovers: it performs every deploy and every rollback, so one fault stops both, and what makes the state readable is its own [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) record.
C1757	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	stated	What a human does then is reach the platform directly, outside the factory and through the credential the [environment's record](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) holds, and put the target back on the build of the rollback's target.
C1758	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	stated	The factory is not told and is not meant to be.
C1759	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	The target then runs a build no deploy record names, which is a [mismatch](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) like any other, holding that service's production deploys until a human clears it at the detector.
C1760	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	Clearing it is the record of the manual act and the only record there is, so what that human writes there is the whole of what a later reader gets.
C1761	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	built	A service's first release has no target: nothing below it closed passed or [timed out](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), no earlier build is running under it, and there is none to redeploy.
C1762	how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md	unbuilt M1	A human's undo (10) begins at its second phase there, so the correction is a revert with no rollback before it.
C1763	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	stated	Nothing here permits or forbids one service calling another: code calls what it calls, and the factory is not in the network path.
C1764	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	built	A contract is a record: one published interface of one service, identified by that service and the interface's own name in its [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), with its kind — a published interface or a [store](09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) — as the field everything else follows from.
C1765	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	built	[The merge queue](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) writes it at the fast-forward of the first [release](../06-releases/02-the-release-record.md) that publishes it, in the same write as that release's first version, because a contract changes only inside its service's items and every write to it happens at a release.
C1766	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	built	The kind has to be single-valued across versions, which is why it is a record and not a name for a service plus an interface name: two versions disagreeing about whether the thing is a store would enforce two promises on one interface.
C1767	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	built	So there are two versioned things, and they must not be combined into one.
C1768	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	unbuilt M5	A release number orders the builds of a single service.
C1769	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	built	A contract version is a compatibility promise to whoever calls it, and semver is precisely what that is for: major means a consumer breaks.
C1770	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md	built	One service can publish several contracts — an API, a gRPC service, three topics — and they move at their own rates.
C1771	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md	built	**No single item may break a contract.**
C1772	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md	unbuilt M5	A breaking change is four items: the producer adds the new **form** — what the interface's build declares, its fields, operations or messages and their types — beside the old, each consumer migrates, the producer runs a [brownout](08-deprecation.md) of the old form, and the producer removes it.
C1773	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md	unbuilt M5	Each ships alone, is verified on an environment of its own, and is independently reversible.
C1774	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md	unbuilt M5	Where a change genuinely cannot be decomposed, that is an [escalation](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) (12), not permission to batch — and taking it over gives a human no power the factory lacks, since a human authoring a [stage](../01-one-pipeline.md) instead of an [agent](../01-one-pipeline.md) changes the author and not the stages, the gates, or this rule.
C1775	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md	built	The four items are also where the version moves, and it moves twice: the addition is a minor, each consumer's migration leaves it where it is — so does the brownout, which changes behaviour and not the form — and the removal is the major.
C1776	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/03-what-a-contract-promises.md	built	Every contract promises **backward** compatibility — the new build reads what the old one wrote — which is what a consumer of an interface needs.
C1777	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/03-what-a-contract-promises.md	built	A store promises **forward** as well, and over writes as well as reads: the build being restored reads what the newer one wrote, and the schema the newer one installed still accepts what the restored build writes.
C1778	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/03-what-a-contract-promises.md	built	Both are enforced on every diff after that.
C1779	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/03-what-a-contract-promises.md	built	Nothing is declared.
C1780	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/03-what-a-contract-promises.md	built	Which promise a contract makes follows from whether it is a store, which the factory already knows, and there is no third case: forward alone has no user, because anything a rollback reads is read going the other way too.
C1781	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	The factory diffs the contract a candidate publishes against the one in production: a diff the contract's promise allows passes, and a breaking diff without the migration already shipped ahead of it is a rejection at the Merge to master gate.
C1782	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	Which a diff is depends on the position of the element it touches, because compatibility runs one way for what an interface returns and the other for what it accepts.
C1783	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	In output position an element added is compatible and one removed or redeclared is breaking.
C1784	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	In input position an element added as required, an accepted range narrowed, an accepted value withdrawn, or an argument dropped is breaking, where an element added as optional or a range widened is not.
C1785	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	The breaking edit in either position mints a major and takes the four items of a [migration](02-no-single-item-may-break-a-contract.md).
C1786	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	The [consumer contracts](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) in force are decided the same way in both positions: a candidate whose request form no longer accepts what a consumer's send-side predicate says it sends is rejected at Merge to master on the same terms as one whose response fails a read-side predicate.
C1787	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/04-enforcement.md	built	The [same graph](../../what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) answers who is affected — the factory knows which services consume which contracts, so "what does this break" is a query rather than an estimate, and it feeds the [context factor](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) of the risk score directly.
C1788	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	built	A unit belongs to a field's identity rather than to a note about it — `send_time_millis`, not `send_time` — and so does whether the field is always populated.
C1789	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	built	A change of units is then a rename, a rename is a breaking diff, and the four items of a migration handle it with nothing new.
C1790	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	stated	**The producer profiling its own fields is refused.**
C1791	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	built	**A consumer's assumptions are checked against the candidate.**
C1792	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	unbuilt M5	The factory already knows who consumes what, so the candidate running on its own environment is checked against every consumer contract, and a failure is a rejection at the Merge to master gate like any breaking diff.
C1793	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	unbuilt M5	No [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) limits it — a consumer called once a month is covered exactly as well as one on a hot path.
C1794	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	built	A [consumer contract's predicate](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md), one assertion the factory can decide against a single observed exchange, has a third outcome for the same reason a [criterion](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) does: where the candidate's run produced no exchange to decide it against, it is **undecided** rather than passing.
C1795	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	unbuilt M5	Undecided is read at the Merge to master gate the way a failure is, which is the treatment [_What the candidate environment decides_](../05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md) already sets out.
C1796	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md	stated	Replaying each consumer's recorded actual usage is the layer that is not built, and not for its size.
C1797	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	**A consumer contract is a predicate, not prose.**
C1798	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	It has to be decidable by the factory against one observed exchange, and it is drawn from the list of allowed predicate kinds the factory owns.
C1799	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Over what the consumer receives: the field is read at all, it arrives populated, it has this unit, its values stay inside this domain or this range.
C1800	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Over what the consumer sends: the operation is called at all, this request element is sent or left out, and the values sent stay inside this domain or this range.
C1801	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	The send side is derived from the same build, because compatibility runs the other way for an input.
C1802	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	The list grows with the rest of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md) (8); a consumer picks from it and cannot invent a kind of assertion when one is derived.
C1803	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	unbuilt M2	An assumption that will not fit a predicate is not in a consumer contract at all — it stays the consumer's own acceptance criteria, and an item against the producer if it is ever violated.
C1804	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	It owns security predicates, decided per toolchain against the build, that an owner may only extend: shipped as content of the product, versioned by the [install event](../../deferred.md).
C1805	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	**It is derived from the consumer's build, not entered by hand.**
C1806	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	A **consumer contract** is a record and an artifact of the consumer's item, written through the [artifact store](../01-one-pipeline.md) at the [implementation stage](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) from that item's build, by whoever authors the stage, an agent or a human backstopping it, and derived either way rather than typed.
C1807	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Which producer each predicate is over is derived with it, at [_Which producer a consumer reaches_](11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md).
C1808	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	It has no field naming the release it entered force at: no release exists when it is written, and the merge queue writing one later would be a second writer.
C1809	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	So a consumer contract derived for a candidate that never merges is filtered out, because no release names its item.
C1810	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	unbuilt M2	The consumer is read and what it assumes derived again at every release, so a consumer contract cannot outlive the code that motivated it.
C1811	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Which consumer contracts bind at a moment is a query: for one service, those derived from every release from its [_last known-good release_](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) — what a rollback could return to at all — up to its newest release.
C1812	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	A service none of whose windows has closed [passed](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) or timed out has no last known-good release, and the range is then every release it has, which is the direction a first release's missing [rollback target](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) already takes.
C1813	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	A consumer that stops reading a field stops declaring it: the list [_Deprecation_](08-deprecation.md) waits on shortens itself, and its emptying raises the brownout alone — the removal follows only on the brownout's own evidence, the way [_Deprecation_](08-deprecation.md) sets out — with nobody having to remember either.
C1814	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	unbuilt M2	**Its authority comes from the gate, not from the derivation being right.**
C1815	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	A producer's own diff runs against the contract version its current release publishes, because the promise is to what is running.
C1816	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	A consumer contract is checked against the contract version its producer's newest release publishes, because that is what the consumer will meet.
C1817	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Neither is written down and neither produces a record: both are computed at the moment the gate fires, and again inside the merge queue at re-verification, which rejects on the same terms with itself as the actor.
C1818	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	**Where derivation cannot see, an owner supplies the predicate with a safeguard.**
C1819	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	stated	A safeguard is authored and maintained by an owner, so the blind case does not go unnoticed.
C1820	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	**A derivation that could not run records that it could not, and the record puts a human at the gate.**
C1821	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	The derivation is per toolchain, so an extractor is a component of the factory, shipped with it, and which toolchains have one is published as a fact of the factory's version — readable before a service is adopted rather than discovered at its first removal.
C1822	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Where no extractor covers a consumer's build, or extraction fails outright, the outcome is **could not derive**: a record, not an empty list, because "no consumer reads this" and "no consumer's read was visible" call for opposite responses and a record that cannot tell them apart licenses the wrong one silently.
C1823	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md	built	Nothing bounds what an unreadable consumer consumes, so that reaches every candidate touching a published contract in the install, and a marked element's [deprecation list](08-deprecation.md) can no longer be known empty, so no removal ships mechanically while the record stands.
C1824	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/07-who-owns-a-contract.md	unbuilt M2	A contract belongs to the service that publishes it, and changes only inside that service's items.
C1825	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/07-who-owns-a-contract.md	unbuilt M2	It gets no timeline of its own: a promise is only real when something serves it, so the authority to accept a change to one belongs where there is a build to check it against.
C1826	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/07-who-owns-a-contract.md	unbuilt M2	What a consumer gets is the promise its producer's kind of contract makes, and what it owns is the assumptions its own build declares — not a decision at every gate the producer passes.
C1827	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/07-who-owns-a-contract.md	unbuilt M2	A consumer contract checked mechanically is not a veto — what answers it is the diff, not a person.
C1828	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/07-who-owns-a-contract.md	unbuilt M2	An objection raised after the fact is an item against the producer, joined to the original by intent, like any bug, and an owner who wants a human on a particular contract gets one with a safeguard (9).
C1829	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	Deprecation is an obligation of the contract, not a note someone leaves.
C1830	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	When the producer adds the new form, the old one is **marked** — a field of the contract version, per element, derived from the build like the rest of the form, because no diff shows which new form supersedes which old one and the producer's own item is the only thing that knows.
C1831	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	Where a toolchain has no place to state it, a store schema being the ordinary case, the build states the mark beside the migration in the same file the derivation already reads.
C1832	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	A mark is itself a change to the form, so it mints a version and it is a minor: a consumer sees a bump for an annotation nothing breaks on, which is the cost of the version tracking the form rather than the promise.
C1833	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	The list is then a query and nothing writes it — for one marked element, the consumers whose contracts in force still name it, the consumers whose contracts in force over that producer are [partial](12-what-the-derivation-records.md), plus any safeguard's predicate naming it.
C1834	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	A detector of its own, the pass over the deprecation list, raises both, and has a row in [_Components and what they call_](../../components.md).
C1835	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	It raises the brownout when the derived consumer contracts naming the element are gone, and the removal when the brownout's window reached its cap with no crossing and with volume received.
C1836	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	It writes a [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) of its own each pass, so a stopped pass is a named row rather than a brownout that never arrives.
C1837	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	It raises neither again for an element whose brownout failed, the failed window standing in the record for the detector to read, both raises being a human's from then on.
C1838	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	Each raise is keyed by the contract and the element, the [evidence key intake states](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) for these raises, and the key is what stops a second brownout arriving beside one already raised.
C1839	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	A safeguard stops neither item existing, only passing: the candidate is rejected at the Merge to master gate on the safeguard, counts an attempt, and appears in [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) as an escalation (12) naming the safeguard and its author, which is the blocked item asking the consumer to confirm.
C1840	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	The brownout is an item of the producer whose release disables the marked element in behaviour while leaving the form unchanged (the field unpopulated, the operation erroring), deliberately the change [_What a diff cannot see_](05-what-a-diff-cannot-see.md) names, made this once on purpose.
C1841	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	It ships, deploys, and rolls back like any release, under whatever [rollout strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) the score and an owner's safeguards pick.
C1842	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	Its [window](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) is the one window that reads more than the producer's own numbers, the exception stated beside the rule it excepts in [_The analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md).
C1843	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	It runs to the [cap](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) rather than stopping where the [boundary](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) would allow, the way the [held-out sample](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md)'s does, its evidence being the point.
C1844	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	A service breaking anywhere in the install while it is open fails it, and the failed exit is the ordinary one: the [rollback](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) restores the element unattended, the [revert](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) and the [hold](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) follow as after any failed window.
C1845	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	The failed brownout stands in the record as a consumer read the derivation missed, so raising that element's brownout or removal again is from then on a human's act, never the detector's.
C1846	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	The detector raises the removal only on a brownout window that reached the cap uncrossed having received volume (one whose comparison received none says nothing about the element, which is the reading [_The analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) gives an empty comparison).
C1847	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	That removal reaches [Implementation](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) with a human deciding it there: the drop's diff destroys stored data, so [reversibility is resolved rather than valued](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) there.
C1848	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	The brownout is the one release whose intended effect is a caller's failure — the factory degrading production on purpose, which nothing else may do — and the exception is stated the way [the search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md)'s is, named and bounded: it ships as an item through every gate, one window bounds it, the ordinary rollback reverses it, and the removal it precedes would fail the same consumer permanently, with nothing watching and nothing left to restore.
C1849	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	Brownouts hold windows open, so the [window limit](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) already bounds how many elements a producer clears at once — and at the supplied limit of 1 a brownout is the producer's whole window budget for a full cap, its other releases holding at the production deploy gate meanwhile, the price the [held-out sample](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) already pays for the same run-to-cap watch.
C1850	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	unbuilt M5	A store's old form takes no brownout: its consumer is the service's own past release, which is not running to err, and the release range [_What a consumer declares_](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) queries is already what covers it.
C1851	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md	built	The list contains the consumers the factory can derive, so a consumer outside the factory is on it only where an owner's safeguard supplies a predicate replacing that consumer's contract — the blind case [_What a consumer declares_](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) sets out, and blocking the removal item is what the safeguard does here.
C1852	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	A service's store is a contract too, and its consumer is the service's own past — the release that was running a minute ago, which a rollback can restore.
C1853	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	It is also a consumer that writes: a rollback makes the restored build the store's writer again, so its consumer contract is derived from writes as well as reads — the elements the code writes, and whether each is written populated and inside its domain, from the build like everything else.
C1854	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The [declarations in force](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) are the same range query, from the last known-good release up to the newest, which is exactly the set of releases a rollback can put back in front of the store.
C1855	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The rule is the same there: **no single item may break the store.**
C1856	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	unbuilt M5	A breaking schema change is five items, and while it remains, the old form has the same deprecation obligation an old interface has.
C1857	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	unbuilt M5	The store gains the new form beside the old; the code writes both forms and still reads the old; a **backfill** copies into the new form every row the old form already holds; the code reads the new form; the old form is dropped.
C1858	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	unbuilt M5	The code writes both forms until the drop, because until the drop a rollback can restore a release that reads the old one.
C1859	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	Reads move only after the backfill, and the item that moves them waits on its record the way the drop does.
C1860	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The schema change itself is a step of the deploy that ships it, applied by the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) and named on the [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md), and a rollback undoes none: the schema moves only forward, which is what makes this promise the one a rollback rests on.
C1861	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	Which changes a store already carries is read from the store and never from the factory's records.
C1862	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The deployer keeps a **schema history** in each service's store: one row per change applied, naming the release that shipped it, the change's identity, and a checksum of its text, written in the same transaction as the change where the engine allows one.
C1863	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	Every deploy, [the search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md)'s included, applies exactly the changes its build declares that the history does not hold.
C1864	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The deploy record reports what the deployer did and is not what it decided from.
C1865	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	An engine that cannot put a change and its row in one transaction can leave a change applied with no row, so every change is authored to be applied twice without effect, and [the candidate environment](../05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md) checks that by applying it twice, a second application that changes anything being a rejection at Merge to master.
C1866	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	It exercises [the snapshot](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md) the same way, taking and verifying one before the destructive change and rejecting at Merge to master where it cannot.
C1867	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The history is not a record of the graph: it lives where the schema does, reached through the same credential, and [the record inventory](../../records.md) lists no row for it.
C1868	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The deploy of [the adoption item](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md)'s release applies none of them.
C1869	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	On every environment it writes into the history one row per change the build declares, naming that release and marked as found applied rather than applied by the deployer, and applies nothing; the mark is a field of every row, so a later reader tells a change the factory applied from one it took on the adoption's word.
C1870	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The next release's deploy then applies exactly what its build declares that the history does not hold, as any deploy does.
C1871	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	A backfill item is a release whose change is data and not form.
C1872	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	It declares no schema diff and opens no contract version, only the element it fills and the element it fills from, in the form the store's extractor states, as [_What the derivation records_](12-what-the-derivation-records.md) sets for every derivation.
C1873	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	unbuilt M5	Its change is written to be rerun from where it stopped, a row already copied being skipped, so a deploy rerun after a stop finishes the copy.
C1874	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The candidate environment checks that by running the change twice over the seeded store, and a second run that changes anything is a rejection at Merge to master.
C1875	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	unbuilt M5	The deployer marks its deploy record complete only when every row the old form holds is present in the new, and that fact gates what follows.
C1876	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The item that moves reads and the removal after it are each rejected at Merge to master while no deploy record marks the backfill for that element complete, the rejection a diff already meets without the migration shipped ahead of it.
C1877	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The constraint rule below reads the same fact: a not-null constraint or a domain check on the new form is addable once the backfill is complete and every declaration in force writes the form populated and inside the constraint's domain.
C1878	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	Enforcement requires nothing new: the factory diffs the schema a candidate declares against the one in production, and a diff the declarations in force forbid without the migration already shipped ahead of it is a rejection at the Merge to master gate.
C1879	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	What the declarations forbid is read over writes as well as reads.
C1880	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	Dropping an element a declaration in force still names is destructive, and so is adding a constraint (a not-null constraint, a domain check, a uniqueness rule) that a write declared inside the range would violate.
C1881	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	What decides them is [the candidate environment](../05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/README.md), where the run's writes land in the environment's own store and every store declaration in force, read and write, is decided against the state it holds.
C1882	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md	built	The past release's predicate over a column the candidate stopped writing is exercised there or it is undecided, and undecided is read at the gate the way a failure is.
C1883	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/10-work-that-spans-services.md	unbuilt M5	Work that spans services needs no new record type.
C1884	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/10-work-that-spans-services.md	unbuilt M5	One request can produce four items in four services, and the intent is what joins them — every item already links back to the intent that caused it, so "everything that came from this request" is a query that works today.
C1885	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/10-work-that-spans-services.md	unbuilt M5	The four items of a contract migration are the same a few hops longer: two items decomposed from the intent, the brownout raised on the mark's evidence, and the removal on the brownout's window — each link naming the record before it, back to the contract version the intent's own item minted — so the query returns all four, walking the evidence links where it walked the intent link.
C1886	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	Which producer each predicate of a [consumer contract](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) is over is derived with it, and that field is the edge every cross-service check reads.
C1887	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	So each such address is an entry of that file naming the service and the interface it reaches, authored beside the code that reads it and diffed like the rest of the file.
C1888	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	The extractor pairs each call site with the entry it reads its address from, and the consumer contract names, per predicate, the producer contract the pairing resolves to.
C1889	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	unbuilt M2	That field is the only place the edge between two services is held.
C1890	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	unbuilt M2	The [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) carries no dependency set, because the set is derived from the build at every release and a copy on the service record would be the same fact twice, one of them stale.
C1891	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	An entry may declare its address outside the factory, and a call through it is covered by nothing, which [_Two versioned things_](01-two-versioned-things.md) already says of such a call.
C1892	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	unbuilt M2	Three readers read the field, and none reads an absence as no edge.
C1893	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	[Enforcement](04-enforcement.md) reads a producer's consumers as the consumer contracts in force naming its contract, and the [context factor](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) reads the same query.
C1894	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	The [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) composes a [candidate's environment](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) from the producers the candidate build's consumer contract names, and theirs through their current releases' consumer contracts.
C1895	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	unbuilt M2	It writes each entry's address for that environment at composition, which is what lets one file serve every environment.
C1896	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	A call site whose address the extractor cannot trace to an entry, a literal in the code or a value read from the store, is [could not derive](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) for that consumer contract, and so is an entry naming no service for an address inside the factory.
C1897	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	So an edge the analysis did not find is told from an edge that does not exist, and a consumer with a could-not-derive contract is on every producer's [deprecation list](08-deprecation.md) and in no candidate environment's composition, the human at the gate being what decides it.
C1898	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md	built	A service whose file names no producer for an address it reaches is could not derive until its entries are authored, which is the state an [adopted](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) service arrives in.
C1899	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	**The record names the extractor and what it could not follow.**
C1900	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	A consumer contract names the extractor that derived it, the toolchain and the factory version that shipped that extractor, and lists the constructs the extractor met and could not follow: a read through reflection, a string-keyed access, a generated accessor, a mapping read from configuration.
C1901	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	A predicate in it is a read or a write the extractor showed, and the record's silence on an element never shows that the consumer does not touch it.
C1902	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	A record whose list is empty is complete, and one whose list is not is partial.
C1903	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	unbuilt M2	The [deprecation list](08-deprecation.md) reads a partial record the way [_Which producer a consumer reaches_](11-which-producer-a-consumer-reaches.md) says it reads a could-not-derive record: the consumer stays on the list of every marked element of every producer contract the record names.
C1904	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	unbuilt M2	What lifts it is the case _What a consumer declares_ leaves to the owner, a safeguard (9) on the consumer supplying predicates for the constructs the record lists; the derived and the supplied predicates together are then read as complete.
