C1905	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	unbuilt M2	Nothing else reads a partial record differently from a complete one.
C1906	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	**A could-not-derive record names its cause.**
C1907	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	No extractor for the toolchain, or an extraction that failed, and for the second what the extractor reported.
C1908	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	**An upgrade that changes an extractor derives again.**
C1909	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	unbuilt M2	At an upgrade's first start where the shipped extractor for a toolchain changed or was added, a fact of the factory's version, the [install's first-start step](../10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) derives a consumer contract again for every release in force on that toolchain.
C1910	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	It derives from the build the release names, held in the [artifact store](../01-one-pipeline.md), and writes through the artifact store beside the earlier record and never over it, the new record naming the new extractor.
C1911	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	A release's contract in force is its derivation by the newest extractor.
C1912	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	**What a derivation expects of the source is the extractor's to state.**
C1913	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	unbuilt M2	Where a declaration sits and how a mark, a backfill, a schema change, a screen's [transition function](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/01-the-transition-check.md), or a [mutation tool](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/03-what-the-encoding-rests-on.md) is stated is the extractor's, per toolchain, published with it as the same fact of the factory's version as which toolchains have one.
C1914	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	built	A build that does not follow the convention reads could not derive, the outcome [_What a consumer declares_](06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) gives a derivation that could not run, and puts the same human at the gate.
C1915	how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/12-what-the-derivation-records.md	unbuilt M2	What tells an agent the convention is the implementation role's [role prompt](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md), which ships with the product carrying each toolchain's convention beside its extractor, so what the factory checks and what its agents are told to write are one thing versioned once.
C1916	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The **health monitor** compares that release against a **control** — a set of instances running the build already in production, started alongside the release and taking comparable traffic.
C1917	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	A control belongs to the deploy the rollout is part of and is not a deploy of the release it runs.
C1918	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	It mints no [release number](../06-releases/04-the-release-number.md), and the production deploy record of the release it was started beside names it: the build the control runs and the instances running it.
C1919	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	stated	There is no rollout component: the rollout is the deployer executing the [strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) through [seam 4](../../deferred.md), starting the control, widening the release's share as it reads the comparison, and advancing its own deploy record's status target by target.
C1920	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	There is one control per production [target](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) the release has reached, started on that target when the rollout reaches it, and the deploy record names each.
C1921	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The comparison is evaluated per target, each target's release instances against the control on it, and never pooled across targets.
C1922	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	On every start, a crash and an upgrade of the self-hosted factory being the same stop, it reads its [deploy records](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) not marked failed that no target or only some targets have completed.
C1923	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	It asks each of those targets which build it is running, and either completes the deploy in the target order or returns every target it reached to the release that was current before it.
C1924	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	Keyed on the deploy record and the target, a repeat writes nothing and no target is deployed to twice.
C1925	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The one case the restart cannot decide is a [schema change](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) the record does not mark complete: the deploy is marked failed with the previous release left current, on the [store rule](../07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) rather than on the deployer knowing how far it got.
C1926	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	At passed and at timed out the health monitor calls the deployer to tear the control down and then closes the window, the order [_The analysis window_](02-the-analysis-window.md) states for every exit.
C1927	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	A rollback closes the window of every release it undoes, failed for the one whose analysis failed and skipped for the rest, and no control survives it: every control ends with its window, and so do the instances of every release the rollback undid.
C1928	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	That is the ordering this paragraph exists to state: those instances are torn down when the last window that could return to them closes, and never at an exit of their own.
C1929	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	A build running on a production [target](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) beside the [current release](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is a mismatch only where no open window names it: as the release under watch, as the control that window's deploy record names, or as the release a rollback of it would return to.
C1930	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	It is never exempt on a target the [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) marks complete, that target being meant to run the release under watch, so the old build on it is a mismatch whatever window is open.
C1931	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The exemption covers the targets the rollout has not reached yet and nothing else.
C1932	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The exemption is bounded by the window's own cap: a window open past it excuses nothing, because the record that suppresses the check is written by the component whose failure the check exists to survive, and an exemption that outlives the thing it describes is one a stopped health monitor holds open forever.
C1933	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	stated	The measurement exists in production and nowhere else, and the reason is arithmetic rather than policy: it is computed from organic traffic, and a [candidate environment](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) has none.
C1934	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	A rollout fails when the comparison crosses the [boundary](02-the-analysis-window.md), and the rollback follows automatically — except while a [mismatch](08-drift-detection.md) holds that service's production deploys, where [_Drift detection_](08-drift-detection.md) says what happens instead.
C1935	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The health monitor runs after every production deploy, and the strategy decides only how the factory acts on it.
C1936	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	So the real comparison is available only with the strategy that keeps one, and a deploy without a control falls back to reading the release against the recent history of the release [a rollback of it would return to](03-overlapping-windows.md) — the newest release below it whose window closed passed or timed out, descending past skipped as that query does, a release a rollback undid having no instances left to read a history off.
C1937	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The baseline is only as good as the build it runs, which is the argument for a safeguard: an owner can add a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) setting explicit thresholds for a service, the way they add one at a [gate](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) or on a strategy, and a service whose normal behaviour is already bad is where that is worth doing.
C1938	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	An explicit threshold is absolute where the comparison is relative, and it applies in addition to the comparison rather than instead of it — the release passes both or neither, so a safeguard here can only add a check, like every other.
C1939	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	It is authored on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) beside the [window limit](03-overlapping-windows.md) and the window's parameters, and it is not one of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md)'s eleven rows: those are one closed set that one section gathers, and this is authored without being among them, the way [retention](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) is.
C1940	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	An explicit threshold names no [intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) and no [criterion](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md), and that is decided rather than missing.
C1941	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	Between rollouts there is no second arm, so the health monitor also reads each service against its own recent history — the same quantities, measured against what that service was doing before the deploy rather than against instances running beside it.
C1942	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The rule it is read under is [_The analysis window_](02-the-analysis-window.md)'s and not a new one: a crossing inside an open window rolls the release back, and outside one it raises an [item](../01-one-pipeline.md).
C1943	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	What it states instead is the **average run length** — the mean volume of traffic a service whose behaviour has not changed runs before this reading crosses it once, which is what statistical process control asks of a detector over a stream with no horizon.
C1944	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	They are authored on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) beside them, supplied by the score where an owner authors neither, and a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) may only shorten the run length, which adds a check rather than removing one.
C1945	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	An explicit threshold is read at a run length too, on the same division: the owner set its size when they set the number, and the run length is supplied where they authored none.
C1946	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	More than one reading now runs on one service, the comparison, this one, and an explicit threshold where a safeguard set one, so what an [incident](06-incidents.md) records is which of them crossed, on which quantity, and at what boundary.
C1947	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The quantities are the service's own: request rate, error rate, and latency, emitted because the factory instruments the software it writes, which is what makes a service measurable without an owner authoring anything.
C1948	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	Availability is the error rate read over a period rather than a fourth quantity.
C1949	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	Saturation is read by its effect and never modelled: a pool exhausted or a disk filling appears as errors or latency and nowhere else, because a picture of a service's capacity would put the factory in the business of planning spend on [substrate](../../what-humans-do.md) the owner hosts and the factory did not deploy.
C1950	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The software emits two records per request or job.
C1951	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	One is written when it arrives, naming the service, the [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) that emitted it, the [deploy](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) that placed the instance, the [target](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) it ran on, and the **operation**: the route, procedure, or job kind the request was for, a name the software fixes the way it fixes a failure class.
C1952	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The other is written when it completes, naming the same five, the outcome, and the duration.
C1953	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The instance is told its deploy at placement: the deployer hands it the deploy record's identity in its configuration, beside the token [the way in](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) calls with, so the record carries it and no reader joins it by time.
C1954	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	So the outcome set holds `unfinished` beside the outcomes the software names: a request that arrived in an interval and has no completion counted against that interval once a deadline the instrumentation fixes for the service has passed since the interval ended, computed from the two counts and emitted by nothing.
C1955	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The request rate is counted over arrivals, the error rate over arrivals with `unfinished` among the failures, and the duration distribution over completions alone.
C1956	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	Nothing is sampled: a [window](02-the-analysis-window.md) is a volume condition, and a sampled count is a volume the window would rule things out on that never arrived.
C1957	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The interval a record lands in is assigned when the store accepts it, by the store's clock, and the instance's clock names nothing.
C1958	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	A completion is counted against the interval its request arrived in, which the store computes as the time it accepted the completion less the duration the record carries, a duration being measured on the instance's own clock and needing no other to agree.
C1959	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	So `unfinished` for an interval is that interval's arrivals less the completions counted against it, read only once the deadline has passed since the interval ended; until then the interval is open, and the error rate over it is read as no volume rather than as a low one.
C1960	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	A release arm receiving materially fewer requests than its control over the same interval is a crossing on the request rate, at the boundary the [window](02-the-analysis-window.md) holds for every quantity, however few records the release emitted — and none is the plainest case.
C1961	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The store the outside row of [_What the factory does not build_](../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) names keeps a count of arrivals for each interval per closed set of names (service, build, deploy, target, operation).
C1962	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	It keeps a count and a histogram of durations per the same set with the outcome added, at one resolution the factory fixes for every service and ships with the instrumentation.
C1963	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	The histogram is a count per bucket over a fixed set of bucket boundaries, one set for every service, fixed by the factory and shipped with the instrumentation the way the resolution is.
C1964	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The latency the three quantities name is one quantile of that histogram, which quantile fixed with the boundaries, and it is read at the resolution the buckets give and no finer.
C1965	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The comparison reads the deploy the window was opened over: the release's series under its build against the control's under the control's build, and no series under any other deploy.
C1966	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	So the comparison and the reading against a service's own recent history are each taken per operation as they are per target.
C1967	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The number of operations one release may hold open per interval is capped on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) as the failure record's keys are, the excess landing in a named overflow operation, so a service that names an operation per identifier grows the store by the cap and no further.
C1968	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	stated	How long the store keeps them is a requirement on the host and not a value an owner authors: at least the longest of the window's cap, the period of the service's [objective](05-service-level-objectives.md), and the span the reading against its own recent history is read over, stated as a shape the way [_An environment per candidate_](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) states the platform's.
C1969	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	A fourth kind is kept on the same terms: a **failure record**, a count per interval per service, build, deploy, target, failure class, and code location, the point in the code the failure was raised from.
C1970	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The number of distinct keys one release may hold open per interval is capped on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), the cap the operation already takes, the excess landing in a named overflow bucket rather than growing the store without bound, so the count stays true and where it was truncated stays visible.
C1971	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	A fifth kind is kept on the same terms, for a service with an [area](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) graded `irreversible`: a count per interval per service, build, deploy, target, and [hazardous operation](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md), of the times the software performed the operation the area names.
C1972	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The health monitor reads the count as a fourth quantity for that service and for no other: compared between the arms, read against the service's own recent history, and sized, allocated, and closed on the terms [_The analysis window_](02-the-analysis-window.md) states for the three.
C1973	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The names on a record, the outcome set, the interval resolution, the histogram boundaries and the quantile, the failure record's key set, and the `unfinished` deadline are one shape, and the shape carries an **emission version** that every record names and the store keeps as one more name in the closed set.
C1974	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	A build emits the version the factory that built it ships, so a service moves to a new version on its next build and never by an item raised for the purpose, and until then it emits the version it was built with.
C1975	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The [window](02-the-analysis-window.md) names the emission version each arm was read at, and a comparison whose arms differ in version is read over the quantities both carry, a quantity the newer alone carries being outside that window's set and recorded on it as such.
C1976	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	The health monitor reads every emission version the factory has shipped, on the promise [_One process_](../../one-process.md) makes for the factory's own store: a version adds a name or a quantity beside what the version before carried and removes nothing until the version after, so a series kept at an earlier version is read as it was.
C1977	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	On each pass it writes onto its [last check](08-drift-detection.md) for the service the newest time the store holds a record for, and a read whose newest record is older than the interval that last check carries, or whose period the store does not cover, is read as no volume and never as a low one.
C1978	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	unbuilt M1	[_Ops_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) is where the numbers are read and there is no other place: a screen of numbers with no setting beside them is what that screen refuses.
C1979	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md	built	So an explicit threshold is the only reading left, used alone rather than alongside a comparison for the one release where there is nothing to compare — and where no safeguard sets a threshold, the first release is unmeasured and nothing about it is discovered by watching.
C1980	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The **analysis window** is a record, one per production deploy of a release its service has not watched before, whichever attempt that is, and one per deploy [the search](03-overlapping-windows.md) calls for, over a build nothing has watched.
C1981	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	So a rollback opens none, the release it returns to having been watched already, and neither does a redeploy of one already watched.
C1982	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The health monitor writes it, opened when that deploy record is written and closed once at exactly one of four exits, because the health monitor is what evaluates every exit and the deployer never advances that record again on the deploy's own account.
C1983	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The window names the deploy it was opened over and, through it, the release, the service, and the control; where the deploy is [the search's](03-overlapping-windows.md), whose record names no release, it names the build instead.
C1984	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	It also names whether passed is available to it, the size per quantity, the confidence, power, and cap resolved at the open, and the set of targets the boundary was allocated over at the open.
C1985	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	It names the size and run length in force for the other two readings that could fail this release, the own-history reading and the explicit threshold, beside the [policy version](../../what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md) and [score version](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) in force.
C1986	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	It names the **boundary version**: the construction that turns the size, confidence, power, and run length into a boundary, and allocates it over the set, is the factory's, shipped with it and authored by nobody, and a version of the factory that changes it changes what every exit means.
C1987	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A mechanism that learns from exits reads exits at the boundary version in force and none at another, since `passed` and `failed` under two constructions are not one currency: the [per-author prior](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md), the [window limit](03-overlapping-windows.md), the size and power the score supplies, and the [held-out bands](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md).
C1988	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	So an upgrade that changes the version restarts each where it starts, the prior at an unseen author's width, the window limit at 1, the supplied size and power at what the product ships, the bands empty.
C1989	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A service missing one of [the four fields the deployer populates on its service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) opens a window that records only that it measures nothing for that service, since what the field would have supplied is what a reading needs to exist at all.
C1990	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	It closes `timed out` at the open, there being nothing for the cap to wait on, and it is a rollback's target like any timed-out window: [_Overlapping windows_](03-overlapping-windows.md) asks whether any window failed the release and not whether anything measured it.
C1991	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	So the health monitor writes a last check record per service and the deployer one per target of a persistent [environment](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), the same record the drift detector keeps, carrying the interval its pass runs on and recording on its last pass that no further pass is owed, both as [_Drift detection_](08-drift-detection.md) sets them.
C1992	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The window is also how long the factory may act on the health monitor alone.
C1993	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Inside it, a **crossing** of the boundary — what sequential analysis calls a statistic reaching the stopping boundary set for it — rolls the release back with no human involved; outside it, the same crossing raises an [item](../01-one-pipeline.md).
C1994	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A rollback deploy opens no window of its own: the build being restored has been watched already, and a fresh window would compare it against the release just failed and restore that one.
C1995	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The window is a volume condition, not a clock — and not a volume computed in advance either.
C1996	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The comparison is evaluated as traffic arrives, against a boundary that is valid at every point it is read.
C1997	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The unit the boundary's variance is estimated over is the interval and not the request.
C1998	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	A routing key on the record is refused for three reasons the store already states: it is content of the request, a series per key grows with users, and the store is one the host provisions for a year or more.
C1999	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	No one grouping is right for both readings, so _closing without failing_ is reserved as a phrase and every rule over exits names the exits it admits, one at a time.
C2000	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	An exit is more often several records than one, so the window's close is the last durable step of it and never the first, on the rule [_Tight integration_](../../what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) states for every event that writes more than one record.
C2001	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The [deployer](01-the-health-monitor.md) writes the [rollback](../06-releases/06-rollback.md)'s deploy record, the deploy record of every release the rollback undid advances to rolled back, those releases' windows close skipped, [intake](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) writes the revert intent, and this window closes failed last.
C2002	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Each step is keyed the way [the merge queue](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md)'s record write is: the deploy record on the release and the target, the intent on the failed release its evidence names, a window's close on the window.
C2003	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Where the failed exit finds no release to return to, the order is the same: the revert intent and the [page](07-pages.md) precede the close.
C2004	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	That rate is the **power** — the probability that a regression of the size the window is actually watching for crosses the boundary rather than reaching passed — authored per service beside the size and the confidence, and resolved at the open like them.
C2005	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Passed is available only while the control is readable.
C2006	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The health monitor already reads every service against its own recent history, and it reads the control the same way: a window whose control is crossing that reading cannot exit passed, and stays open until the control is readable again or the cap closes it timed out.
C2007	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	What an owner authors is that size, the confidence required, and the power required at that size, with the rest of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md) (8), safeguarded per service the way explicit thresholds are (9); where they author nothing the score supplies all three.
C2008	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The size is one value per quantity, three for a service and four where an area of it names a [hazardous operation](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/03-hazard-severity.md): the smallest change in the request rate, in the error rate, in the latency quantile, and in the count of that operation that the comparison has to detect.
C2009	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	A safeguard on the size is a minimum level of protection, the score may ask to catch something smaller and never something coarser, and on the power it is a floor, the direction a floor under the confidence already sets.
C2010	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The duration is measured and never set, and that is where the throughput comes from: a release that is plainly fine shows it early and its window closes.
C2011	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The confidence is over the set of quantities and not per quantity: what it bounds is how often a release that is fine is rolled back, because a crossing on any one of the three quantities [the health monitor reads](01-the-health-monitor.md) starts the same rollback, the same [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) on the service's deploys, and the same [incident](06-incidents.md).
C2012	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	So the health monitor reads each quantity at the stricter boundary that holds the authored confidence over the set, which is its arithmetic to do once rather than every owner's to do by hand.
C2013	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The power takes no such adjustment and is held per quantity, because the two exits are opposite logical shapes: failed is a disjunction — a crossing on any one quantity fails the release — where passed is a conjunction, every quantity having ruled its own regression out, so a regression on one quantity reaches passed only if that quantity's own boundary missed it and reading three cannot raise the rate.
C2014	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	So the threshold is read the way [the reading against a service's own recent history](01-the-health-monitor.md) is — against a boundary valid at every point, with an average run length in force — and not as a count compared against a number.
C2015	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The comparison is [evaluated per target and per operation](01-the-health-monitor.md), so the set is the three quantities on every operation on every production [target](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) the rollout is planned to reach, the [service's targets](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) at the open and the operations the release's build names, and a crossing on any one starts the same rollback.
C2016	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The boundary is allocated over that whole set at the open, by the arithmetic above, and does not move as targets are reached.
C2017	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The window names the target set the allocation was made over, so an exit stays readable against the boundary it was read against after the environment record changes.
C2018	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A target the rollout never reaches, the release failing or the cap closing the window first, keeps its allocation unspent.
C2019	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The power stays per quantity and per target, for the reason it stays per quantity: passed is a conjunction, every quantity on every target having ruled its own regression out.
C2020	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	So the operations a window reads alone are those whose volume can support the power in force at the size in force inside the cap.
C2021	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The rest are pooled into one series per quantity per target and read as one, so a regression among them still crosses, diluted by their combined share and no more.
C2022	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Which operations were read alone and which pooled is named on the window at the open, since a later reader cannot tell a pooled crossing from one operation's otherwise.
C2023	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The size, the explicit threshold, and the objective stay authored per service and are read per operation, one value against each series, so no owner authors a value per operation.
C2024	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	What the other two contribute over a window is that window's volume divided by each reading's run length in force, which is arithmetic on values already resolved at the open rather than an unknown, so [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports the three together per service and not the confidence alone.
C2025	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A cap limits it in elapsed time, authored the same way.
C2026	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A release arm silent beside a control that is serving is [a crossing on the request rate](01-the-health-monitor.md) and exits failed, never timed out.
C2027	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	So the window records the volume each arm received, and where both are nothing the exit says nothing about the release.
C2028	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Such a window stays admissible for one thing only, [a rollback's target](03-overlapping-windows.md), which asks whether any window ever failed that release and not whether anything measured it.
C2029	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	A service's first release ends there too and for a different reason: it has no comparison to rule anything out, so passed is not an exit available to it — an explicit threshold a safeguard set can still fail it, but nothing can clear it early.
C2030	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The [sample the score holds out](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) runs to the cap rather than stopping where the boundary would allow — [auto-passing](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) a change the score wanted gated is where the factory is most openly guessing, so it takes the longest watch available, which on a quiet service is not much.
C2031	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The window reads the [producer](../07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md)'s own numbers and nothing else, one window excepted below.
C2032	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	Any service crossing that reading while a brownout's window is open fails it.
C2033	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The parameters are learned.
C2034	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	Only a rollback or a human's undo traceable to the health monitor counts as that evidence, and not a rollback a human [marked as not caused by the release](03-overlapping-windows.md).
C2035	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Which of the two one such event sets is the exit its release's window took, which [_How it learns_](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) states once for both: a window that closed passed cleared what it should have caught, so the event is about the power, and one that timed out ruled nothing out at all, so it is about the size.
C2036	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	The size in force is the coarser of the two, per quantity — what the evidence asks for, and the finest size this service's own traffic can rule anything out at inside the cap, which is arithmetic over the volume its windows have actually received and over the confidence in force.
C2037	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	On latency that finest size has a second floor: the width of the histogram bucket [the health monitor](01-the-health-monitor.md) reads the quantile from, since a change smaller than a bucket is a change the kept series cannot show.
C2038	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	built	Where the traffic cannot support the power in force at that size inside the cap, passed is not an exit available to that window, which runs to the cap the way a first release's does and a window over an unreadable control already does.
C2039	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md	unbuilt M1	So [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports the size in force per service and per quantity with what the traffic reaches beside each, the confidence and power that reading was taken at, and whether passed is reachable at all — the one place a human sees a service being watched for less than the evidence asked for.
C2040	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	An open window blocks nothing.
C2041	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	The next item builds, verifies on its own environment, merges, mints its number, and deploys while the window before it is still open — up to the **window limit**, which is how many windows one service may hold open at once.
C2042	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	The window limit is authored with the rest of gate policy (8), and where an owner authors nothing the score supplies it, the same division the window's own parameters use.
C2043	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	A safeguard on the window limit is a maximum on how many releases one rollback undoes — the score may ask for fewer open windows, never more — so a safeguard can only add safety here too.
C2044	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	What the score supplies starts at 1 and rises only with evidence, because that evidence arrives one-sided — a service that has never rolled back has seen the throughput the window limit gives and none of the rollback size it causes.
C2045	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	What moves it is stated exit by exit, the way the [per-author prior](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md)'s evidence is and for the same reason: a window closing `passed` raises it, a rollback that undid more than its target lowers it — unless a human marked it as not caused by the release, which is set out below — and `timed out` and `skipped` raise nothing.
C2046	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	A service's **last known-good release** is the standing value — the release watched by the newest closed window whose exit is passed or timed out.
C2047	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	A **rollback's target** is computed for one rollback: the newest release *below the release being rolled back* whose window closed passed or timed out, descending past failed, past skipped, and past any window still open.
C2048	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	Nothing writes either: the release record is written once at the [fast-forward](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) and never again, so an outcome settled by a window closing long afterwards cannot be a field of it, and the fact is already implied by records that exist.
C2049	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	A release whose deploy [stopped before its build took traffic](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) (a schema change that failed to apply) does not count for either query, however its window closed.
C2050	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	Both queries read that from the deploy record, marked failed with no target complete, and descend past such a release the way the target descends past skipped.
C2051	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	A release the factory skipped over is not one it can return to.
C2052	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	Rollback is then a traffic shift onto the instances of the rollback's target, which are still running — long-lived, warm, and sized for all of production.
C2053	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	They are, because a release's instances are kept at the capacity they had while any open window's rollback could return to that release, and torn down only when the last such window closes.
C2054	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	Where the rollout was without a control there is nothing to shift traffic onto and the rollback is the slow one — the build of the rollback's target redeployed and waited for.
C2055	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	What an owner may author is the fraction of its fleet the build being replaced keeps while it is the rollback's target, on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) beside the window limit and the explicit threshold, and not one of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md)'s eleven rows.
C2056	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	It is never supplied by the score: the factory [does not model a service's capacity](01-the-health-monitor.md), so it cannot know what fraction is enough, and the one value it can set without knowing is all of it.
C2057	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	Below that the rollback scales the target's instances back to full before it shifts, and a scale-out that fails inside the window [pages](07-pages.md) with production still on the failed release — the owner bought the saving with that exposure, and the page names it.
C2058	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	So an owner may author a **proof test** on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) beside the fraction: how often the deployer, inside an open window, shifts a share of traffic onto the instances of the rollback's target and back again.
C2059	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	A test that finds fewer instances than [the deploy record names](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is already a [mismatch](08-drift-detection.md) by the comparison that reads that count, holding the service's production deploys; a test that finds the fleet there and cannot shift onto it stops nothing, and [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reporting it is the whole of what happens.
C2060	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	A safeguard may not suspend any of this.
C2061	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	Nor may an operator pause it, on a service or anywhere.
C2062	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	Master is linear, so the release above a bad one includes it: a rollback inside overlapping windows undoes every release above its target, up to the window limit of them.
C2063	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	A rollback still sets a [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), where an open window no longer does.
C2064	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	The hold stays until the revert item ships, and it does not hold the revert — a dependency hold that blocked its own dependency would never lift.
C2065	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	The revert also deploys ahead of every release the hold is holding, which is the one place the release number does not order deploys: those releases cannot deploy until the revert ships, so making the revert wait behind them would be the same deadlock one step further out.
C2066	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	What bounds the batch is the **backlog cap** — how many releases may wait behind a rollback hold, the ones the rollback [skipped](02-the-analysis-window.md) and the ones merged while it stands alike, every one the revert's deploy would deliver, before [_The merge queue_](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) stops fast-forwarding that service's candidates.
C2067	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	It is a field on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) beside the window limit, and where an owner authors nothing it is the window limit, so both halves of [_the exception_](../06-releases/01-one-item-per-release.md) are bounded by one number until an owner separates them; a safeguard (9) may lower it and never raise it.
C2068	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	What recovers attribution is a **search**, the health monitor's own authority bounded by evidence the way the rollback is.
C2069	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	The search calls [the build runner](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) to make one build at a time and the deployer to deploy that build with a [control](01-the-health-monitor.md); each such deploy is measured by a window of its own and ends with that window, whatever the exit.
C2070	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	The first applies the revert onto the rollback's target and answers whether the revert itself is at fault; the rest bisect the batch by release number.
C2071	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	A search deploy's record names the build and no release, so the service's [current release](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) stays the rollback's target throughout, and the drift detector reads the target against the build the record names.
C2072	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	The search runs only where each of its windows can close on evidence: where the service's traffic cannot rule out a regression of the size in force inside the window's cap, or cannot support [the power in force](02-the-analysis-window.md) at that size, no search runs, since a bisection over a signal that cannot conclude terminates anyway and names an innocent release.
C2073	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	And a **search budget**, on the service record beside the cap: a maximum count of builds and a maximum total time production spends on them, each one putting a build that passed no gate in front of real traffic.
C2074	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	Where an owner authors nothing the budget is what bisecting a batch of the cap's size needs, each build watched for one window, and a safeguard (9) may lower it.
C2075	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	So this hold is the one [_What a gate may change_](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) gives a routing and a stated reason of its own.
C2076	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	So a named human at [Ops](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) may mark a rollback as not caused by the release, with the reason they state, the way a rollback on their own judgment names its [source](../06-releases/06-rollback.md).
C2077	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	The mark is a record of its own, written once, pointing at the rollback's deploy record; it is read by everything that learns from outcomes and by nothing that acts.
C2078	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	unbuilt M4	Before the revert ships, the mark ends the revert item — [dropped](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md), written by [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) with Ops as the caller — and lifts the hold the rollback set, there being no defect on master for the hold to keep off production: the next release from master carries the change, opens a window of its own, and is measured again, and until one comes the service stays on the release the rollback returned to, because a redeploy of a release already watched [opens no window](02-the-analysis-window.md).
C2079	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md	built	After the revert has shipped the change is gone from master and the mark corrects the evidence only; restoring it is a new intent the owner raises.
C2080	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/04-after-the-analysis-window.md	built	The health monitor keeps running after the window closes, on the reading against the service's own recent history — the control was torn down with the window, so no comparison is left to run.
C2081	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/04-after-the-analysis-window.md	built	It is an [unrefined](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) intent in [_Work_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), the same shape as the intent an end user's [_reports_](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) are grouped into (4, 5), taking the same [stages](../01-one-pipeline.md) and the same gates.
C2082	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	A **service level objective** is a target an owner authors on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) — the proportion of a quantity that must be good over a stated period, 99.9% of requests served without error over thirty days being the ordinary shape.
C2083	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	The factory computes what is left of it from the emission the health monitor already reads, and that remainder is the **error budget**.
C2084	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	While the budget is exhausted, that service's production deploys are held, and the hold lifts itself when the period rolls forward far enough to restore it; nothing is decided and no [_page_](07-pages.md) fires, which is the shape the hold a [declared dependency that is not current](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) sets already has.
C2085	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	It raises an intent on the service when the budget is exhausted, or when the **burn rate**, the share of the budget spent per hour, exhausts it before the period ends at either reading, the last hour or the period so far.
C2086	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	The evidence that keys the raise is the service and the objective's period, so one intent stands per period.
C2087	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	The raise reaches [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) as an unrefined intent and never as a page: an escalation on it pages, the way [_Pages_](07-pages.md) treats one on an item raised from an incident.
C2088	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	A period the store does not cover, read as [_The health monitor_](01-the-health-monitor.md) states, leaves the budget uncomputed, and an uncomputed budget holds the way an exhausted one does, lifting itself once the store covers the period: a budget taken as intact over records that are not there is an absent input read as evidence.
C2089	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	It is authored and never supplied, which is the opposite of the division the window's size and the [window limit](03-overlapping-windows.md) use, and the reason is that the score learns from the factory's own outcomes: a target inferred that way is set to whatever the service already achieves and never binds.
C2090	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	Where an owner authors none there is no budget and nothing is held — that reading and the window are the whole of what protects that service.
C2091	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	built	Beside the service level objective, the service record takes two further owner-authored rates: currency per environment-hour and currency per instance-hour, pricing the hosting a candidate and a kept fleet consume outside the factory.
C2092	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	unbuilt M1	Converted at the write, they turn the recorded [environment-hours](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) and [instance-hours](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md) figures into money the same way [a rate authored per model version](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) turns spend into cost per feature, and where none is authored those figures stay units only.
C2093	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	unbuilt M1	So two items pass the hold: a revert, which passes the hold [a rollback leaves](03-overlapping-windows.md) for the same reason, and an item whose intent a detector raised on that service, the health monitor's at a crossing or the objective's own.
C2094	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md	unbuilt M1	A request an owner raises (1) on that service does not pass; the route is the objective's intent, which exists whenever the budget is exhausted.
C2095	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	An incident is a record on a production environment, written by the health monitor at the crossing against the release running there, whether or not that release's window is still open.
C2096	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	It names which of the three readings crossed — the comparison against the [control](01-the-health-monitor.md), the reading against the service's own recent history, or a threshold an owner's [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) set.
C2097	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	It also names the quantity the reading crossed on, and the boundary it was crossed against: that quantity's size, and the confidence or the [average run length](01-the-health-monitor.md) in force, beside the [policy version](../../what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md) and [score version](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) at the reading.
C2098	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	At the same crossing, the health monitor copies the [failure records](01-the-health-monitor.md) for that service, release, and target onto the incident, a field of it rather than a link to the store.
C2099	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	unbuilt M4	It is kept for the incident's life and is removable the way any kept record's text is, by [a redaction](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), which destroys words and never the link.
C2100	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	Its status advances to resolved when the crossing has stopped against what is running *and* what was raised from it has finished: a rollback stops the crossing and does not resolve the incident, production being still worse, which is what the hold and the page both say.
C2101	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	It is also what the health monitor keys its own deduplication on, before it calls [intake](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md): an open incident on this service and this release makes a further crossing an observation on it and never a second intent.
C2102	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	built	An incident whose crossing has not stopped, with no open window, [pages](07-pages.md) whoever holds (2) while the item it raised is being worked — raising that item does not answer the page.
C2103	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	unbuilt M4	The failure records the incident carries are material for that work, reached the way any other material is: named on the [input manifest](../01-one-pipeline.md) before the stage's agent starts.
C2104	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md	unbuilt M4	Exceeding that limit turns it into an escalation (12), the same Work row a stuck feature produces: a bug the factory cannot fix is not a different kind of stuck.
C2105	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	One **notifier** delivers everything waiting on a human out of the product, on three channels: mail, chat, and the page.
C2106	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	It routes all three the same way, on the [_People_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) declaration, by the [duty](../../what-humans-do.md) the row belongs to, or by the named human where the row belongs to none, so the routing is implemented once rather than beside each thing that waits.
C2107	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	A **page** is the narrow channel: what qualifies for one is a wait where the deployed software is worse until a human ends it, and nothing else fires one.
C2108	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	It is the only channel that widens and the only one that writes into [the log](../../deferred.md), a record per delivery there being a log sized by how often the factory notifies.
C2109	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	What a delivery on any of the three writes instead is a **delivery record**, one per row the notifier delivers and the notifier's alone to write: the row, the channel, the recipient it resolved from People, whether the transport accepted the send, and when.
C2110	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	It is overwritten at each attempt the way a [last check](08-drift-detection.md) record is, so what exists is one record per waiting row rather than one per delivery.
C2111	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	So the notifier writes a last check record too, beside the health monitor's and the deployer's, and nothing pages when the notifier is what stopped: the channel that would carry that page is the thing that stopped.
C2112	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The notifier in turn pages whoever installed the detector when the detector's own last check is stale, so each of the two processes watches the other.
C2113	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	A **page event** is one append to the log naming the row, the human reached, and which of four it is, reached, acknowledged, widened, or answered, each with an [actor](../../deferred.md).
C2114	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The first delivery reaches every human holding the duty at once; unacknowledged it widens exactly once, to the owner; there is no narrower first recipient and no second widening.
C2115	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Acknowledged is written when a human says they have the row, which is an act in the product and nothing else — it ends no wait, decides nothing, and stops only the widening.
C2116	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	unbuilt M4	The same act on a row that is a [decision](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) appends an acknowledgement to the decision as well, one act writing both, and a gate row that pages nobody still takes the acknowledgement, which _Where a gate is, and what decides it_ sets out.
C2117	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Answered is written when the row stops waiting, and the component that ends the wait calls the notifier at the same write it ends it with.
C2118	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	So that page's answered event is written by the notifier the next time it reads that store and finds the mismatch cleared, and its time is the time of that read rather than of the clearing.
C2119	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The fourth is a window past its cap that nothing has evaluated, which is what a stopped health monitor looks like from outside.
C2120	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The fifth is a rollback the health monitor called for whose [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) is still not complete on every target at the deployer's next [last check](08-drift-detection.md) for that environment: production serves a release the factory has already failed, and the mechanism that would remove it did not finish.
C2121	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The sixth is an open incident whose crossing has not stopped, with no open window — the deployed software is worse until a human ends it, whatever the factory has since raised from it.
C2122	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	It pages whoever holds (2), and the factory having raised an intent on that incident does not make this page one that only informs.
C2123	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	unbuilt M4	The software is no worse for the hour, and the customer is — a breach notified late is a breach notified late whatever production is doing — so it pages whoever holds (2) for that constraint when the deadline is written, at any hour, and is answered when the intent ends or the human marks the obligation met.
C2124	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	unbuilt M4	Passing it while the intent is still open pages whoever holds (2) for that advisory, at any hour, answered the same way a constraint's deadline is.
C2125	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The intent it raises pages nobody through grouping, which still takes no gate, but the mark does: whoever holds (2) receives it at any hour, the owner reached if nobody does.
C2126	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	unbuilt M4	It is one page per intent and never per report: a later report attaching to an intent already paging raises nothing further.
C2127	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The cap is how many intents a service's marked reports may page per interval, a field of the [factory-wide settings record](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) with a shipped default, which an owner may lower and a safeguard (9) may lower and never raise.
C2128	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Past the cap a marked intent waits at Work as every marked one does, a human still resolved to [Spec](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) on it, and one page per interval goes out instead, naming the service and how many marked intents arrived past the cap.
C2129	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Whether this page fires at all is a field of the [factory-wide settings record](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), on by default, so an owner who will not be woken by a stranger turns it off.
C2130	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	A rollback the factory performed on its own is reported, not requested, and reporting is not paging — **the factory does not page to inform.**
C2131	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	A page has no duty of its own — inventing a thirteenth would be a mechanism where routing already exists.
C2132	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Installing the drift detector is [substrate](../../what-humans-do.md) outside the twelve, so the page reaches whoever [_People_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) records as having installed it, and widens to the owner where nobody is recorded.
C2133	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	A duty may be held by more than one human, and a page reaches all of them at once.
C2134	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	There is no rotation naming which one it reaches first: a rotation would be a People declaration enforced by nothing, and what a stale one does — widen to every holder — is what happens here without it.
C2135	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Unacknowledged, a page widens once more, to the owner.
C2136	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	Where production serves a release the health monitor has called for a rollback on and the rollback has not run, the software is worse now and worse for every hour of the wait, and the page fires whenever the condition arises, on every service.
C2137	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	For the second kind an owner authors on the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), beside the explicit threshold and the objective, the hours within which that service pages.
C2138	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md	built	The hours carry the time zone they are read in, which [_The screens as software_](../11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md) requires of every authored calendar value.

C2144	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md	unbuilt M2	The **drift detector** is one process outside the pipeline that reads what is actually running on each production target and compares it against what that service's production [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) marks for that target — the release it names where the target is marked complete, the previous one where it is not, and nothing where the complete record is a [removal](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/04-retirement.md)'s.

C2153	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md	built	A record is owed a pass for exactly as long as the thing it names exists, and the component that writes it records on its last pass that no further pass is owed — an overwrite like every other pass's, the record kept the way everything in [the graph](../../what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) is kept rather than deleted.

C2157	how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md	built	Installing it includes writing one address into its store, mail or chat, the owner's and nobody's duty, and when the third comparison finds the notifier's last check stale or every factory last check stale at once the detector delivers to that address itself, naming what it found, and records the delivery in its own store.
