C2317	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md	built	The truncation appends a chained row of its own, naming who authored the retention value, the value it enforced, the boundary it cut to, and the policy version and score version in force at the cut.
C2318	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md	built	The checkpoint is not a field: it is the oldest record the log still holds, and storing it beside the log would be one fact in two places able to disagree.
C2319	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md	unbuilt M4	Everything else in [the graph](../../../what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) is kept, because deleting any of it breaks a link a record already points at — which is what one graph exchanges for the customer's own disk.
C2320	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md	unbuilt M4	Kept means the records: what is removable inside a kept record is its text, by [a redaction](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), which destroys words and never a link.
C2321	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	built	A **legal hold** suspends [_Retention_](02-retention.md)'s removals.
C2322	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	built	It is a record with one writer, [Factory](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), the same writer a [safeguard](../02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) and a [halt](../04-stopping-the-factory.md) already have, naming a subject and a reason.
C2323	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	built	While it stands, truncation and [report expiry](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) are refused wherever it reaches, and so is deleting the [_People_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) mapping of any actor key a record within its reach names: what a hold over an approval preserves is who approved, and the mapping is the only thing that says so.
C2324	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	unbuilt M7	People refuses the deletion and records the refusal, so no erasure-list row is written and no restore is needed to undo it.
C2325	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	built	It ends only at a gate row of its own, held by a human always and routed away from the human who wrote it, the treatment [_A safeguard's withdrawal_](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md) already gets, for the same reason: ending a hold removes a protection and is decided rather than merely written.
C2326	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	unbuilt M7	A [redaction](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) inside a hold's reach is refused while the hold stands, and the refusal is recorded.
C2327	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md	stated	That conflict, a hold standing against an erasure the owner owes, is counsel's to resolve and not this document's.
C2328	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md	built	An owner authors what the number is compared against, never how it is computed — the formula is published so a human can disagree with it, and the [vector](../../04-risk-score/README.md) behind it is learned from outcomes.
C2329	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md	unbuilt M6	A gate is where policy applies; what is between the gates is the factory's own — including [what an agent is told](../../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) there, which the factory writes, versions, and puts through a gate of its own, and which an owner reaches only by adding a safeguard at that gate (9).
C2330	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md	unbuilt M6	The [six patterns](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) are a closed set and the factory owns the form, which is what lets it end the interview — an owner confirms criteria (6) and does not author the shape one may have.
C2331	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	unbuilt M7	Everything else an owner authors is per subject and adds protection.
C2332	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	A [safeguard](02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) puts a human at a gate, a [risk threshold](01-what-is-in-it.md) is a field of one environment record, and a parameter narrowed is narrowed for one scope.
C2333	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	unbuilt M7	None of them is a stop: a human at a gate still has Approve in front of them, and the [emergency action](../01-one-pipeline.md) is approve now rather than skip.
C2334	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	A **halt** is the one authored record whose subject is the factory.
C2335	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	Factory writes it, the way it writes a safeguard and under the same rules: it is never edited, it is withdrawn by a second record naming it, and that withdrawal is not in force until [_A halt's withdrawal_](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md) approves it.
C2336	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	Setting it and withdrawing it each append a [policy version](02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), so the interval the factory stood halted is a fact of the trail with an [actor](../../deferred.md) at each end, rather than something a later reader reconstructs from what stopped arriving.
C2337	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	While one stands, every firing of a [deploy to production](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) row on every service holds, and [the merge queue](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) stops fast-forwarding every service's candidates, written into [the log](../../deferred.md) the way [the backlog cap](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md)'s stop already is.
C2338	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	Nothing is decided, no [attempt](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) is counted, and the [score](../04-risk-score/README.md) learns nothing, which is the treatment [a hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) already gets and the reason a halt is one rather than a reject.
C2339	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	It is the one hold no approve passes.
C2340	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	Every other hold the factory sets offers a human Approve at the row, and approving through this one would be withdrawing the halt in the place the design least wants that decided: at a deploy gate, by whoever is there, during whatever made the owner set it.
C2341	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	What passes it instead are the two exceptions the [exhausted error budget](../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) hold already takes, a revert and an item the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) raised on that service.
C2342	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	unbuilt M7	A revert is known by the link its intent carries to [the release it undoes](../06-releases/06-rollback.md), whichever source raised it, and never by the source.
C2343	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	The queue stop does not catch a revert's own candidate, for the reason the backlog cap's does not.
C2344	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	It suspends nothing that recovers.
C2345	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	unbuilt M7	The [rollback](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) inside an open window runs, [the search](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) runs, and the revert ships, for the reason [_Overlapping windows_](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) gives against a pause: a stop that removed a recovery would remove it in exactly the period a human is least available.
C2346	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	A halt stops the factory acting forward and never stops it undoing what it did.
C2347	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	Setting one is the owner's and is not a thirteenth [duty](../../what-humans-do.md): the withdrawal row belongs to no duty and widens to the owner, which is the routing every unheld row already takes.
C2348	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	unbuilt M7	The record shows on [Factory](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) and on [the home view](../11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) for as long as it stands, so a halted factory is never a quiet one, and ending it is a decision at a row rather than a field somebody flips.
C2349	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	A halt is unbounded and reaches everything.
C2350	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	A **change freeze** is the other shape: a period, on one service, authored ahead of what it is for.
C2351	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	It is a field of the [service record](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) beside the [hours within which the service pages](../08-operations/07-pages.md), and it names periods within which that service's production deploys are held.
C2352	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	It is authored outright with nothing supplied, for the reason those hours are: nothing the factory observes says when a customer's peak trading period, contractual change calendar, or notified maintenance window falls.
C2353	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	A safeguard (9) may add a period or lengthen one, and may never shorten one.
C2354	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	It holds the way an exhausted error budget holds, and that is the whole of the mechanism: nothing is decided, no [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md) fires, and the hold lifts itself when the period passes.
C2355	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	unbuilt M7	It takes the same two exceptions, so a freeze never holds the fix for what the freeze made worse.
C2356	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md	built	It binds deploys alone and the rollback inside a window is untouched, so a freeze stops what ships and never what recovers.
C2357	how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/README.md	unbuilt M2	Gathered, it is eleven rows.
C2358	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	An agent reaches its model through a **provider account**, and the factory stores a reference to that account's credential rather than the credential itself — seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../deferred.md), which the fleet is the first thing to use.
C2359	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	unbuilt M7	The [resolver](../../deferred.md) is a file on disk today.
C2360	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	A fleet entry has nine fields: a model, an effort, a role, a scope, the name of a credential, the **processing location** the credential resolves to, the classes of material the entry may be handed, how much the model reads at once, and how many dispatches pass between [evaluation-set runs](02-a-model-under-a-name.md).
C2361	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The processing location is the provider and the region, written by the owner beside the credential, so the provider is now a named party wherever an entry names one.
C2362	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The model names its version, because the [_per-author prior_](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) is kept per version and a version that behaved differently under one name would average two authors into one prior.
C2363	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The operations an agent may perform belong to the [role](../01-one-pipeline.md), not the entry: an owner may narrow them on the entry and never widen them.
C2364	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	**Effort** is how long the model works before it answers.
C2365	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The factory does not check that the credential's provider offers what the entry asks for, the way it does not check a quota — it discovers both by reaching for them, and an entry asking for an effort nobody offers fails at the same place an exhausted account does.
C2366	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The account itself is not a record.
C2367	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The credential name is the whole of the handle.
C2368	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	An owner writes the entry, at [Factory](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), and no component in [the pipeline](../01-one-pipeline.md) writes back to it: the factory reads the [substrate](../../what-humans-do.md) and never writes it.
C2369	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	unbuilt M7	Six of the nine fields decide that: a model somebody is willing to run, the effort they will have it run at, a credential somebody lends, the processing location it resolves to, how much the model reads at once, and how often to spend on an evaluation-set run; the other three are chosen among values the factory already defines.
C2370	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	How much the model reads at once is the owner's to write for the reason the processing location is: it is a fact at the provider, which the factory reads nothing from, and [context assembly](../01-one-pipeline.md) needs it before the run, the [input manifest](../01-one-pipeline.md) being written first.
C2371	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	unbuilt M7	A factory writing its own entries would choose whose lent account pays for the next stage, the authority [_A spend ceiling_](08-a-spend-ceiling.md) keeps with the owner; [_A fleet proposal_](07-a-fleet-proposal.md) is how it argues instead, naming a fleet and no credential.
C2372	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	unbuilt M7	The roles are the factory's own [stages](../01-one-pipeline.md), plus the two put on an [intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) before any item exists, [the grouper](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) before any intent does, and the role that argues a fleet proposal.
C2373	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	None is a deploy: the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) performs every deploy and no entry is ever put on one.
C2374	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The scope is drawn on the lines a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) is; the entry for the role that argues a fleet proposal carries none, since it argues over the whole fleet.
C2375	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The classes of material are narrowed from what [_One pipeline_](../01-one-pipeline.md) hands an agent: the intent's statement, reports, an owner's answer, the repository, run output, constraints, and an incident's copied [failure records](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md).
C2376	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	[Context assembly](../01-one-pipeline.md) reads the classes of material at every dispatch: a class the entry does not name is withheld before the selection rule selects anything, and the [input manifest](../01-one-pipeline.md) records each withheld source as excluded with the entry as the reason, beside what the rule excluded.
C2377	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	[Dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) honours both halves of a scope, the item's [area chain](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) and its service's [project](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md); an agent checking its own scope would be the agent deciding where it may go.
C2378	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	What the entry costs is that the fleet does not grow itself: an item whose stage and [area](../../what-humans-do.md) match no entry is stuck at dispatch.
C2379	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	It does not wait for a proposal to say so: [dispatch writes the hold](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), naming the stage and the scope no entry covered, so the item shows in [Work](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) stopped on that cause rather than as a [timeline](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) that does not advance, and Factory counts the items stopped that way.
C2380	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	Every run of an agent writes an **agent run record**, the counterpart to the [input manifest](../01-one-pipeline.md): where the manifest names what was withheld before the agent started, the run record names what was sent, where, by what, on whose account, and for what.
C2381	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	One is written per run of any agent, by the component that performed it, once; nothing rewrites it.
C2382	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	It carries four groups of fields.
C2383	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	What ran: the model version and effort the entry named, and the [role prompt version in force](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) and the skill versions the run matched.
C2384	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	What it ran on: the credential name, the processing location it resolved to, the per-person key [_People_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) maps to whoever lent it, and whether the account is a person's own or an organisation's, the last two read off the People declaration at the run.
C2385	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	unbuilt M7	What it served: the item and its stage, the intent and its [interview](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) round, the intent a [decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) cut, [the grouper](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) run, or the [evaluation-set run](02-a-model-under-a-name.md), one of the five and never none.
C2386	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	What it spent: the units the provider returned, per kind it counts apart, input, output, cached input, or any other; the time it returned them; the sources handed over; and the converted amount.
C2387	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	That amount sums each kind's units at the rate the owner authored for that kind, model version, and effort on the People declaration, and is absent where any kind returned has no rate.
C2388	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	What ran and what it ran on are on the record rather than resolved through the entry or the declaration, because the owner may change both without changing what any past record says, and for a run that fires no gate this record is the only trace.
C2389	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The lender is a per-person key and never a name, so erasing a person deletes the mapping and leaves the record.
C2390	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	The account may be an organisation's or a person's own, and the factory takes either, because a reference resolves the same way whichever it is.
C2391	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md	built	So a personal account attaches through no second mechanism: same field, same resolver, same record.
C2392	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	The [_per-author prior_](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) is kept per version so that two authors are never averaged into one, and a version that drifts under its name is that averaging arriving anyway, with the wrong sign.
C2393	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	A new model enters with a wide prior and is put in front of a human at more [gates](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md), which is right, while a drifted one keeps the narrow prior it earned before the drift and is gated less at the moment it became worse.
C2394	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	So the factory holds one instrument that is not an outcome: an **evaluation set** per [role](../01-one-pipeline.md), a fixed set of tasks with known answers, shipped with the product as [what an agent is told](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) is shipped — content and not a record, moved only at an upgrade, authored by nobody at the install.
C2395	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	A run is performed exactly as a stage's work is: [dispatched](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) onto the entry, working from the [role prompt version in force](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) for the role and the skill versions whose subject a task matches, at the entry's effort, spending through the entry's credential.
C2396	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	Three things run it.
C2397	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	The entry being written, or its model or effort changed.
C2398	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	Every so many dispatches onto the entry, that count being a field of the entry the owner writes with the rest of it — the entry is the owner's record and the run spends the owner's credential, so how often to spend it is theirs and no default is supplied beyond the run at the write.
C2399	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	And a role prompt version taking force, which runs the set once on every entry in that role.
C2400	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	Its result is a record keyed by model version, effort, role prompt version, the skill versions the run matched, and the set's version, written by dispatch, which performed it.
C2401	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	What the [score](../04-risk-score/README.md) reads from it is the departure of a result from earlier results under the same key, and never across two keys; the reading is the one [_How it learns_](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) already makes for a factor whose distribution moved.
C2402	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	The words are in the key so that departure says the model moved and not that the factory revised what it is told: a role prompt version taking force starts a new baseline for each entry in its role, and the results before it are compared with nothing after it.
C2403	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	A version found departed takes the treatment a drifted factor takes: the prior is resolved rather than weighed, a human deciding whatever the formula returns, with the [factor vector](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) on each decision naming why — until the evidence since the departure narrows it again.
C2404	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	Two role prompt versions on one model version and one set are two results one key apart, which is all there is to read about a version: the set measures the set, and whether a version made the work better is a human's judgment at the version's gate, which [_Reach is what decides one_](03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md) settles.
C2405	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	Spend on work that ships nothing, at a rate the owner sets on the entry and the factory sets at every role prompt version, counted against the credential's [spend ceiling](08-a-spend-ceiling.md) the way a stage's spend is, so a factory revising a role prompt often reaches the ceiling sooner.
C2406	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	stated	An owner moves every entry naming it to its successor, and [_Paid for is not authored by_](04-paid-for-is-not-authored-by.md) says what a prior starting over on every entry at once does: the number rises and humans land at gates across the factory.
C2407	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	The product ships a starting prior per model version, beside the evaluation set and the first role prompts, measured by [the builder](../../what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md) on the builder's own work, and an entry naming a version the product shipped a prior for starts there rather than wide.
C2408	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	The install's own evidence moves it from there as it would from wide, and a shipped prior never replaces evidence the install already holds, an upgrade seeding only the versions the install has none on.

C2410	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md	unbuilt M8	The install exception stays for the role prompt, the skill, and the evaluation set, which a fresh install has no version of yet, since a factory with nothing decided in it has to run; each such entry names the same identity, so an ungated entry says which bundle supplied it.

C2416	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	the widest impact anything in the factory has, so the score gates it whatever the likelihood

C2419	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	So the factory improves its skills on its own, and on any [threshold](../../09-gate-policy/README.md) an owner is likely to author it does not improve a role prompt without a human at the gate.
C2420	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	Nothing measures what a version does to the work, and that is not an omission.
C2421	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	The [evaluation set](../02-a-model-under-a-name.md) reads a version on fixed tasks, two versions on one model version being two results one key apart, which says whether the words moved the model's answers and never whether the work improved.
C2422	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	There is no [analysis window](../../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) over a version and no [control](../../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) to compare one against: a control is instances taking organic traffic, and a role prompt serves none.
C2423	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	What a version did shows in [_Factory_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md)'s gate rejection rate and rework rate, sliced by the version in force, over as many items as a rate takes to move — which on a small install is longer than anybody will wait.
C2424	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	So a role prompt improves on a human's judgment at the version's gate, and that is decided rather than open.
C2425	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	stated	A randomised selection of which items are authored under a new version, the way [the held-out sample](../../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) selects, was refused on arithmetic: the volume a comparison needs scales as the inverse square of what it must detect, and a factory producing a few items a day, split two ways, rules out only enormous regressions.
C2426	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	And the [_per-author prior_](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) cannot see one at all: it is per model, so a model whose role prompt changed reads as one author across both, and the score's opinion of that author is an average over work done under two sets of words.
C2427	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md	unbuilt M8	The role prompt in force is also what the authoring agent works from, because there is nothing else to give it.
C2428	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	An agent works from words as well as from a model, and the words are the factory's.
C2429	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	A **role prompt** is what an agent in a role works from.
C2430	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	There is one per role, so the set is closed the way the set of roles is: a role names the work of one [stage](../../01-one-pipeline.md), of the two put on an [intent](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), of [the grouper](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), or of the role that argues [a fleet proposal](../07-a-fleet-proposal.md), and all of them are the factory's own.
C2431	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	A **skill** is a procedure an agent is given where the [item](../../01-one-pipeline.md) it was dispatched onto matches the skill's subject — an [area](../../../what-humans-do.md), a [service](../../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), or a [project](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), which are the same lines a scope is drawn on.
C2432	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	The set is open, and it is what the factory adds to when it learns something worth keeping about one part of the software rather than about one stage of the work.
C2433	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	A **selection rule** is what [context assembly](../../01-one-pipeline.md) selects by where the material a stage hands an agent exceeds what the entry's model reads at once: which kinds of material go first, how each is cut, and what is left out.
C2434	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	There is one, for the whole factory, because what it reaches is every dispatch.
C2435	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	Where no version is in force, assembly gives what fits in arrival order and records the rest excluded on the [input manifest](../../01-one-pipeline.md), the way the [score](../../04-risk-score/README.md) supplies a value nobody authored, so no item waits on it.
C2436	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	None of the three is a file.
C2437	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	Their writer is the [artifact store](../../01-one-pipeline.md), which already implements the version chain, the versions of one thing in the order they arrived, the [actor](../../../deferred.md), and the authorship attribute once rather than once per author, and which gains another caller in the agent that authors a version.
C2438	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	A version is never edited.
C2439	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	Correcting one is a withdrawal and a second version, the way a [constraint](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) already works and for its reason: what an agent worked from when it authored a stage is then reconstructible from arrival and withdrawal alone.
C2440	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	Which version is in force is not a field either — it is the newest version of the chain that its [gate](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) approved and nobody has withdrawn, a version not approved being not in force with the one below it in force still — nothing at all where an upgrade started the chain — and the entries install wrote standing without an approval, a factory with nothing decided in it having to run — and a field saying so would be one fact in two places able to disagree.
C2441	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	Withdrawal is also the undo, and it is the cheapest one the factory has.
C2442	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	A role prompt that made things worse is withdrawn and the version below it is in force at the next [dispatch](../../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md): no [rollback](../../06-releases/06-rollback.md), no revert item, no [last known-good release](../../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), and no queue held behind it.
C2443	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	The first version of each ships with the product rather than being written by anybody.
C2444	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	That is the arrangement [_Constraints and the design system_](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) already names for content the factory supplies, and the factory's own first authored version supersedes it in the chain.
C2445	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	An upgrade that changed the shipped words moves nothing by itself: at the factory's first start on the new version, what shipped enters the chain on the same authoring-nothing call that entered the first version at install — the artifact store writing it, the author field empty as the exception [_One pipeline_](../../01-one-pipeline.md) states — and it enters as a version awaiting [the gate every version fires](../../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md): a version not approved is not in force, so where it extends a chain the words the install ran on stand until the gate decides, and where it starts one — the role or the skill an upgrade adds — nothing is in force until then, only the work that would have used it waiting: a new role's items undispatched in [_Work_](../../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), [dispatch](../../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) matching nothing whose prompt has no version in force, and an item matching a new skill's subject running without it.
C2446	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	The install's entries alone enter in force ungated — a factory with nothing decided in it has to run, the acceptance [_Gate policy_](../../09-gate-policy/README.md) already makes for the values the score supplies.
C2447	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	What decides it is the gate every version fires, and an empty author is a factor the score cannot compute, resolved the way [an unavailable factor](../../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) always is — a human deciding whatever the formula returns — so the changed words of a shipped role prompt or skill reach force through a human whichever way its reach alone would have gone.
C2448	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	unbuilt M8	The same event makes [_Constraints and the design system_](../../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) write a withdrawal-and-arrival pair; a role prompt has the version chain and a gate a constraint does not, so here the gate is the answer.
C2449	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	What the upgrade does not cost is the diagnosis: [dispatch writes a hold per stopped item](../../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) naming which of the two records is missing, so the stall reaches Work on each item and Factory as a count under that cause, on the same screen the version and the entry are written.
C2450	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md	built	Each of the install's entries names the [shipped-bundle identity](../../../deferred.md#the-products-release-channel) it entered under, so the ungated entry says what release wrote it.

C2457	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	A personal account has a quota an organisation's agreement usually does not, so the fleet has a failure nothing else in the factory has: an agent stops part-way through a stage because the account it runs on is exhausted.
C2458	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	That is not the model failing, and it is a **[hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md)**.
C2459	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	Nothing wrote it at a [gate](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md), no gate having fired, so it is written into the [decision log](../../deferred.md) by the log, with whoever could not reach as the caller and the actor — an agent for its own model credential, the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) for an environment's.

C2461	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	An owner may delete or re-credential an entry without changing what any past record says, where an environment record is kept for as long as the [deploy records](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) naming it.

C2464	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	It reads outstanding holds, though, so it does not dispatch onto a credential already known unreachable, [writing one of its own per item it declines](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), and dispatches again when a retry resolves.
C2465	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	Dispatching again writes the second row of this hold and of each hold dispatch itself opened, so the hold ends where the work resumes; it does not wait to be closed by an agent that has stopped.
C2466	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	The stage waits and resumes when there is a credential to reach, counting nothing against [_the attempt limit_](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) and teaching the prior nothing.
C2467	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	unbuilt M8	The score learns from a reject and not from a hold, which [_What a gate may change_](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) settles for gates and [_The analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) keeps when it counts only a rollback traceable to the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) as evidence.
C2468	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	built	No [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) fires for a model credential.

C2471	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md	unbuilt M8	It reaches whoever People records as having lent that credential.

C2474	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/06-a-credential-taken-back.md	built	The factory finds out the way it finds out about an exhausted account, by an agent failing to reach its model, and it does the same thing.

C2488	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	At first start, and at an upgrade's first start where a stage was added, the **install's first-start step** writes a proposal covering every role: an entry for each, drawn from the shipped starting priors and the shipped [evaluation sets](02-a-model-under-a-name.md) rather than argued.
C2489	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	The owner opens it at [Factory](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) like any other proposal, and nothing pages.
C2490	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Every other proposal is argued, which is what the rest of this section describes.
C2491	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	A proposal argues over the fleet and not over one entry.
C2492	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	It may say that five entries should be retired and six written in their place, because a fleet shaped wrong is not repaired by adding to it, and a mechanism that could only add would recommend growth for as long as anything was wrong.
C2493	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	No record written for traceability points at a fleet entry — [_An account that runs out is a hold_](05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md) records a credential name and never a pointer, for this reason among others — so an entry deleted changes nothing any past record says.
C2494	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	The prior survives too, being per model: [_A credential taken back_](06-a-credential-taken-back.md) already says a fleet rebuilt keeps every bit of its history, and a fleet redesigned keeps it on the same grounds.
C2495	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Its writer is not a [detector](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md).
C2496	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	A detector is one of three callers of [intake](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), and intake writes every intent; a component that also wrote a record of another kind would be a second writer with no [seam](../../deferred.md) between the two things it wrote.
C2497	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	So a proposal has a writer of its own, and the record has two writers with a field as the seam: the factory writes the proposal and never its **disposition**, an owner writes the disposition and never a proposal.
C2498	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	A disposition is open, accepted, or dismissed.
C2499	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	That rule is to raise for a condition rather than for an observation, attaching to what has not finished instead of raising a duplicate, so a shortage that lasted a month would produce a month of proposals.
C2500	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	The **install's first-start step** writes on the proposal half of that seam without becoming a second writer of the same kind.
C2501	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	It writes the proposal only at a version's first start, at an install's first start or at an upgrade's first start that added a stage, and every other write of the proposal side is [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md)'s, performing the run of the role below.
C2502	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	The argued proposal takes [the grouper](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md)'s treatment, for that section's reason: composing one is model work, and model work outside [the fleet](README.md) would run on words no gate ever decided.
C2503	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Arguing a proposal is a role of its own, put on neither an intent nor an item, running under a fleet entry an owner writes like any agent, at that entry's model and effort and through its credential.
C2504	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Its [role prompt](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) is versioned and decided at [the gate every version fires](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md), where [reach](03-what-an-agent-is-told/02-reach-is-what-decides-one.md) puts a human at every role prompt version, so the words that compose what an owner reads are decided the way every agent's are.
C2505	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	[Dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) performs the run and writes the proposal, the way it writes an [evaluation-set result](02-a-model-under-a-name.md).
C2506	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	An owner writes a fleet entry for the role before the factory can argue at all: a fleet with none has the first-start proposal, [the readiness reading](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), and dispatch's holds, and no argued proposal.
C2507	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Dispatch performs it on a pass of its own, reading the evidence already on the record: its own holds, [the readiness reading](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), and [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md)'s rates.
C2508	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	A proposal attaches to the condition it argues over, so a shortage that lasts a month is one open proposal and not a month of them.
C2509	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	The pass overwrites a [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) of its own, carrying its interval, so a pass that stopped is a named row on [the home view](../11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) rather than a fleet nobody argues for.
C2510	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	There is no gate.
C2511	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Nothing ships from a proposal and nothing is decided by it: the owner's write is the review, and what that write produces is an entry [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) matches against, reviewed at a gate the way every other authorship is.
C2512	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	What a proposal may argue is wider than what an owner may do with it.
C2513	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	stated	A proposal for a new role is a proposal for a new [stage](../01-one-pipeline.md), and stage definition is outside everything an owner authors, where [_What is not in it_](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md) puts it, so nobody at this install can enact one, and it reaches the owner as something to take to whoever builds the factory.
C2514	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	Composing the fleet is substrate rather than one of [the twelve](../../what-humans-do.md), so a proposal waits at Factory until an owner opens it, and no [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) fires, because nothing deployed is worse for a fleet that is too small.
C2515	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md	unbuilt M8	An unmatched item is not that same gap: it adds no new state and needs no new count, since [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) already writes the hold naming the cause and [_Factory_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) already counts it there, and a per-role [readiness reading](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) reports the same shortage before any item has reached it.
C2516	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	A **spend ceiling** is a limit an owner authors on what the factory spends through one credential, in the currency the owner's rates are authored in, over a period the owner authors as a length and a start date, and reaching it before the period ends is a [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md).
C2517	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	One period follows another from the start date, so an owner sets the length and the start date to the provider's billing cycle and the ceiling bounds the invoice the scope below names; it is not the sliding window the [error budget](../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) reads over, because an invoice is not one.
C2518	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	It is authored on the [_People_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) declaration that records who lent the credential, which is already per credential name and owner-written, and it has no default: where none is authored the credential is unbounded, Factory names it so, and the provider's quota is the only stop, which is what every install had before.
C2519	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The credential is the scope because it is where the money is: one credential is one account at one provider and one invoice, where an entry, a stage, or an area each draw on accounts whose invoices are separate.
C2520	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	It reaches a model credential only, since what is recorded is what a run spent.
C2521	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	Authored on the credential an environment reaches its targets through, it compares nothing, because a deploy records no spend.
C2522	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The length is in days or in calendar months and nothing finer or other, months because a billing cycle is authored in them and a month is not a count of days.
C2523	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The currency is a field of the credential's row on the People declaration, one per credential, and a rate in a second currency on that credential is refused at the write, so the sum the ceiling compares is in one currency by construction; what that costs is a credential billed in two currencies needing two credentials.
C2524	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	What it is compared against is the [agent run record](01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) every run of an agent already writes, which carries the credential name, the units the provider returned per kind, the converted amount summed over kinds at the rates authored, and the time they were returned.
C2525	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The sum the ceiling compares is a query over the converted amounts of the run records naming that credential whose time falls in the period, and it reads nothing at the provider.
C2526	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	A run record whose converted amount is absent, because a kind it returned has no rate for that model version and effort, gives the ceiling nothing to sum, and a credential under a ceiling fails closed on it.
C2527	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	That is the same hold as the ceiling reached, routed to the owner and naming the kind, the model version, and the effort that want a rate, and it is cleared by authoring the rate rather than by authorising an overage.
C2528	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	stated	A ceiling in the provider's units was refused because the units a provider counts apart are priced several-fold apart and the mix swings by role, so a bound on their sum bounded volume and never the invoice, even on a credential running one model.
C2529	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	What a stage spent and what [the interview](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) spent are each the same query narrowed by the field that names what a run served, so an item's total and an intent's derive from the records naming them, rather than being written a second time.
C2530	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	An [evaluation set](02-a-model-under-a-name.md) run's record carries the same credential name and enters the same sum with the rest; a run the factory triggers at a role prompt version is spend the owner did not schedule, and the ceiling is what bounds it.
C2531	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	There is no reservation: dispatch does not hold units aside before a stage starts.
C2532	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The agent reports units as it works, and the ceiling is compared at each report; where a report would put the sum past it, the hold is written mid-stage by whoever could not proceed — the row [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) keeps for a ceiling met there, the same shape it keeps for a credential exhausted part-way through a stage.
C2533	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	Cost per feature is the same record read the other way: the intent's own run records plus the run records of the items [decomposed](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) from it, reading no fleet entry and no account, the credential name resolving nothing, so the substrate a human wrote is never read.
C2534	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	It is reported per model version, in units per kind, and converted at the write wherever the owner authored a rate per kind on the [_People_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) declaration: a rate is the owner's agreed price and not a fact read from the provider, the same kind of owner-written fact as the ceiling beside it.
C2535	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	Where a rate covers every kind of every model version and effort a feature's stages ran on, cost per feature sums to one total across them.
C2536	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	Where one is missing, the reading stays per model version with no total, because a feature's stages run in more than one role and a role may run on a different model.
C2537	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The ceiling is in that currency and compares the converted amounts, so where it stands the rates are complete for every kind the credential's runs returned, and cost per feature over those runs sums to one total.
C2538	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	Reaching the ceiling before its period ends is the hold [_An account that runs out is a hold_](05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md) describes, written the same way and routed to the owner rather than to whoever lent the credential, since raising, clearing, or lengthening the period is the owner's and a row reaching the lender would reach somebody who cannot act on it.
C2539	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	It is written into the [decision log](../../deferred.md) by the log, naming the credential, with no [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md), no attempt counted, and the prior taught nothing.
C2540	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	[Dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) puts nothing further onto that name until it clears, writing a hold of its own per item it declines, so that twelve items waiting on one ceiling are twelve rows in Work and a count at Factory rather than one row naming a credential and no item.
C2541	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The owner clears it at [_Work_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), and a clear authorises an overage for that period only: it does not reset the sum, and the same units stop the next period at the count authored for it.
C2542	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The ceiling is units over the period in force rather than a total since the last clear.
C2543	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	Which period a run falls in is on no record: it is derived at the read from the run record's time against the start date and length in force, so a period lengthened or re-anchored later re-buckets every run already written rather than stranding it in a period that no longer exists.
C2544	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	The start date carries the time zone it was authored in, as [_The screens as software_](../11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md) requires of every authored calendar value, and a period ends at that zone's midnight.
C2545	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	Factory notifies at a fixed fraction of an authored ceiling and of nothing else, so the hold is not the first anyone hears of it.
C2546	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	It is not one of [gate policy](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md)'s eleven.
C2547	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	Every row there has a value the score supplies and a safeguard narrows.
C2548	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	The score learns from rollbacks and the changes humans undo, and a ceiling's evidence is cost, which the score does not read.
C2549	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	unbuilt M8	So there is nothing to supply and nothing to safeguard, and it is authored outright, beside the [service level objective](../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) and the [fraction of its instances a release keeps](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), which [_What is not in it_](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md) lists.
C2550	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	It bounds model spend and nothing on the host — the [environment per candidate](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) and the fleet a release keeps are hosting the owner pays for outside the factory, so the ceiling bounds the provider's invoice and never the hosting one.
C2551	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md	built	It is compared at each report rather than only at a stage's end, so overshoot is bounded to one report's worth of units; a stage that stops without ever reporting still spends unseen until its account is next reached.
C2552	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	stated	**It is not a [duty](../../what-humans-do.md).**
C2553	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	stated	Supplying a credential is not work done with the factory — it is the substrate the factory runs on, like the host the owner already provides, and it stays outside the twelve for the same reason installing [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) does.
C2554	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	unbuilt M1	Routing needs a named human rather than a duty number: the row reaches whoever [_People_](../11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) records as having lent the credential, and the owner where that person is the one who took it back or where nobody has lent one yet, which is where a [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) already widens to.
C2555	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	stated	**It is not what an agent is told.**
C2556	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	unbuilt M1	A [role prompt](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) and a [skill](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) are the factory's, authored by an agent — or entered unauthored from what shipped, the exception [_What an agent is told_](03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) states, install's entries alone ungated — written by the artifact store, and decided at a gate; the entry is the owner's, written by hand and decided by nobody.
C2557	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	unbuilt M1	The division is the record: what an agent runs on is substrate, and the factory reads substrate and never writes it, where what an agent is told is a record the factory has to be the only writer of.
C2558	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	stated	**It is not gate policy.**
C2559	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	unbuilt M1	A credential reference is a field on the fleet entry the way a [strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) default is a field on production's environment record, and [_Gate policy_](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/README.md) stays at its eleven rows.
C2560	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	unbuilt M1	Who lent which credential is a People declaration, enforced by nothing, like the duties a [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) routes on; the [spend ceiling](08-a-spend-ceiling.md) authored beside it is the one field on that declaration the factory enforces.
C2561	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	stated	**It is not what limits spend.**
C2562	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/09-what-the-fleet-is-not.md	unbuilt M1	Two limits reach what an agent spends — [_the attempt limit_](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md), which counts attempts and is gate policy, and [_A spend ceiling_](08-a-spend-ceiling.md), which counts units and is not — and the entry holds neither: the ceiling is authored on the People declaration beside who lent the credential, and the entry names the credential and says nothing about what may be spent through it.
C2563	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md	unbuilt M1	The **fleet** is the set of agents the factory runs.
C2564	how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md	unbuilt M1	[_One pipeline_](../01-one-pipeline.md) says what one is — a model in a role, with a scope — and that the per-author prior is kept per model rather than per role.
C2565	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	**Work** — one [item](../01-one-pipeline.md) is one timeline.
C2566	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	[Intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), [spec](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md), [plan](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/03-implementation-plan.md), [tasks](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/04-tasks.md), implementation, [rollout](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), and the [numbered release](../06-releases/04-the-release-number.md) it ends in, in the order they happened, with each [gate](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) shown inline at the point where it fires.
C2567	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	At the [Implementation](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) gate the implementation is shown as the [build](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md)'s diff against master, the one place the product renders code, for the reason [_What the factory does not build_](../../what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md) gives.
C2568	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	At every gate the [vector](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) is shown while a human decides and the number beside the verdict once it is written, and the [close event](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) records when the row was opened, both for the reason [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) gives.
C2569	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A holder may say they have a row, which appends an [acknowledgement](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) to the decision, and every other holder of the duty then sees the row as acknowledged and by whom; it decides nothing and the row stays in front of all of them.
C2570	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	[Decomposition](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md) is the one gate above a timeline rather than on it: an intent that became four items shows one decision with four timelines under it, and shows as [partly delivered](../02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md) where those timelines did not all reach a release.
C2571	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Features and bugs are the same kind of item.
C2572	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A project is a grouping of work, not a separate place: it is a record an owner writes at Factory, and that screen says what one holds.
C2573	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Board and list views answer "where is it stuck", which includes [the merge queue](../05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) and the [windows](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) a service has open: who holds each, what is under watch, and what waits.
C2574	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	A thing waits where the windows are all open, an [error budget](../08-operations/05-service-level-objectives.md) is exhausted, a [rollback](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) is holding, a [_fleet entry has no credential to reach_](../10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md), a credential is at its [_spend ceiling_](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md), no [fleet entry](../10-fleet/README.md) covers the stage the item reached, that stage's role has no [role prompt version in force](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md), or a human is holding.
C2575	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Each stop the factory itself causes is a row and not an absence: [dispatch writes one per item and stage](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), naming its cause and the values it was met on.
C2576	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	So the screen shows an item the factory stopped as stopped on a named cause, with a link to where at Factory the record that lifts it is written.
C2577	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Filtered to what is waiting on a human, it is the home view and has the badge count, because answering the factory is the daily job.
C2578	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	What waits on a human is pending gates, [UAT](../../what-humans-do.md) assignments (7), the factory's [interview](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) questions (3), and [escalations](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) where the factory admits it is stuck (11, 12).
C2579	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The factory's own [holds](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) are in it too, where the named cause is a record only a human writes: a fleet entry that does not exist, a role prompt with no version in force, a credential nothing can reach, a ceiling only an owner raises or clears.
C2580	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	So are dispatch's two [constraint](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md)-caused stops, what lifts each being an owner's write too.
C2581	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	A credential nothing can reach routes by the rule [_An account that runs out is a hold_](../10-fleet/05-an-account-that-runs-out-is-a-hold.md) sets rather than to the owner by default, to whoever People records as having lent the name the row carries, which is the same shape as a safeguard naming who answers its rows.
C2582	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	A [_ceiling_](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) reached routes to the owner, raising or clearing one being the owner's, so no row of either kind reaches a human who cannot act on it.
C2583	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Those holds reach the badge and fire no [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md): a badge is what an owner is already reading, and what pages is decided where each wait is defined.
C2584	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A pending gate is a [_decision_](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) whose open event has neither a close event nor an abandonment, which is what the [notifier](../08-operations/07-pages.md) delivers and what People answers "who gates what" from.
C2585	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A row an edit superseded, a supersession ended, or an escalation stopped carries an abandonment and is not pending, so nothing here reads the item's stage to tell a live wait from a dead row.
C2586	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Which human a row waits on is the [duty](../../what-humans-do.md) the design names where one is named: confirming [criteria](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) (6) at Spec, UAT (7) at the [Merge to master](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) gate.
C2587	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It is the owner everywhere else, which is where a [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md) already widens when nobody is recorded.
C2588	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) adds the human and [names who its rows route to](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), so the check reaches the person who authored it rather than the owner by default — a compliance officer who safeguards a regulated area answers their own rows.
C2589	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Where the safeguard names nobody the rows go where every unheld row goes, to the owner, and no thirteenth duty is needed for either: routing needs a reachable human and not an obligation the twelve would have to score.
C2590	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	That filter is not a fifth screen: everything it would contain is attached to an item, and where a thing is stuck is the question Work already answers.
C2591	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	What [_Three properties every screen needs_](02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) puts on the view besides the filter is a row per [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) record past the interval it names with a further pass owed, and at zero the digest.
C2592	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	That is attached to no item and is not in the filter or in the badge count: the badge counts what waits on a human, and a component whose pass ran late waits on nobody.
C2593	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside those rows sits a **readiness reading**, also attached to no item: per role, whether a [fleet entry](../10-fleet/README.md) matches it and a [role prompt version in force](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) exists, and the age of the oldest item [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) holds unmatched.
C2594	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Unlike a last check row, a role with no matching entry reaches the badge, because that is a wait on a human and not a pass that merely ran late.
C2595	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A first install shows a row waiting there before anything is wrong, and every install carries the reading until [a fleet proposal](../10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) leaves every role covered.
C2596	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	**Ops** — deployed software per [environment](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md): which release of each service is running, what [contracts](../07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md) it publishes, health, [incidents](../08-operations/06-incidents.md), the rollouts in progress and the [control](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) each is measured against, where the [strategy](../03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) keeps one, and every [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) record with the age of each, all of them and not only the ones past their interval.
C2597	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside each service it reports what that service is actually being watched for: the [size in force](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) per quantity with the finest size its traffic reaches beside each, the confidence and power that reading was taken at, and whether [passed](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) is reachable at all.
C2598	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Two readings never close: the one against the service's own recent history, and an explicit threshold where a safeguard set one.
C2599	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	For each, it also reports the size and [average run length](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) in force, with the [crossings](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) that run length admits on a service where nothing changed.
C2600	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A service missing one of [the four fields the deployer populates](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) shows unmeasured beside its releases, the same record [_the analysis window_](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) already carries.
C2601	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside the span the store holds for each service it reports the [emission version](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) that service's newest record carries, which is how an owner sees a service still emitting the shape an earlier factory shipped.
C2602	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	This is the one place a human sees a service watched for less than the evidence asked for, or watched with no way to reach passed at all, so that every one of its windows runs the whole length of [its cap](../08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md).
C2603	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Neither of those is a stop, and neither reaches Work or fires a [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md): the release is under watch and nothing waits on a human, which is exactly why weak protection would otherwise be invisible.
C2604	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Which release is running is the [deploy record](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md)'s answer, per target where its completion per [target](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) differs, so that a rollout stalled on one target is visible before anything pages, and where [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) disagrees with it that is what the screen shows, over the record and not beside it.
C2605	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A [mitigation](../../deferred.md) standing on a target is shown here for as long as it stands.
C2606	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	An acting screen, not watch-only: it can [undo a change after it shipped](../../what-humans-do.md) (10), rolling back while the build it would return to is still running or raising the [revert](../06-releases/06-rollback.md) after; instruct a mitigation and end one already standing; mark a rollback as [not caused by the release](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md); and fire a [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) on a human's own judgment.
C2607	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It holds [gate and risk policy](../09-gate-policy/README.md), thresholds, strategy safeguards, environments, the [_agent fleet_](../10-fleet/README.md) and the credential and [effort](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) each entry runs on, the [_role prompts and skills_](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) with the version of each in force, and the [_fleet proposals_](../10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) waiting on a disposition.
C2608	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It holds the [constraints](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) in force with the three widest reaches, listed by reach.
C2609	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Those are the permanent things the factory works inside, read here rather than found through the requests they arrived with, supplied here where one arrives on its own, a design system's upload among them, and withdrawn here where a withdrawal is written.
C2610	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Beside them it lists the registries watched for [advisories](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md), and beside that, the registries a service resolves from that no feed is named for.
C2611	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A constraint that arrived with a single intent is read and withdrawn at Work, on its intent.
C2612	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A **project** is a record, and this is where an owner writes one.
C2613	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It is the root every [area](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) chain ends at, and the widest reach short of the factory that a [constraint](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) (2), a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9), or a [fleet entry](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md)'s scope may name.
C2614	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It has one writer, an owner here, and the write that creates it writes [production's environment](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) in the same event, production existing everywhere and an owner not choosing it.
C2615	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The record holds its identity and nothing an owner authors: what is authored per project is a field of production's environment record, of a constraint, of a safeguard, or of a fleet entry, each naming the project.
C2616	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Which project a [service](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) is in is a field of the service record, part of the identity [decomposition](../02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) writes when it creates the service, taken from [the project intake wrote on the intent](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md).
C2617	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Every mechanism drawn on a project reaches an item through that field of its service: dispatch's scope match, a safeguard's reach, and a constraint's binding alike.
C2618	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A project is never deleted, so an area, a constraint, a safeguard, or a scope naming one never points at nothing.
C2619	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	No write moves a service between projects: the field is identity, and the seam on the service record gives an owner parameters and never identity.
