C2620	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The same screen reports the numbers: throughput, rework rate, gate rejection rate, and cost per feature.
C2621	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It reports how many [_reports_](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) are waiting to be grouped, and how many the way in refused for exceeding a rate, per service and over the whole channel — the way in being the thin report channel the factory ships inside every deployed service.
C2622	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Beside those it reports how many submissions the report store could not read, per service, and lists each service whose current release carries a way in built under an earlier release of the product than the running one, and each service whose project has no [notice](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) in force for the way in to show.
C2623	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It shows whether the [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md) a report marking harm can cause is turned off.
C2624	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It reports how many items are stopped at [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) now and under which of its six causes.
C2625	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It reports the [mutation score](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/03-what-the-encoding-rests-on.md) per service beside the existing counts.
C2626	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It reports criteria withdrawn per service and per author beside [the count in force](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md), and beside them the count [unreliable](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md) per service.
C2627	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It reports the hours a mitigation has stood, per target.
C2628	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It reports, per service, the [product licence](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) an owner authored and the licences its current release resolved.
C2629	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It reports how many gates a [resolved factor](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) put a human at, a factor decided for a human whatever the number is rather than weighed into it, grouped by which factor the vector names.
C2630	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It reports what each agent is doing and how well, and the human's own load: how many rows wait on a human now, how long a row a human decided waited, and how long it was open in front of them before the verdict, per duty and per named human.
C2631	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside the load it reports what each human approved and how often it was later undone (10) or rolled back, the same pair [the screen reports for the factory](04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md), per human.
C2632	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside that pair it reports how many of a human's approvals were of their own edit, the self-approval count [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) writes wherever no second holder of the row's duty exists.
C2633	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	What limits cost per feature is the [_spend ceiling_](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) where an owner authored one on the credential, a limit on what the factory spends through it that a [hold](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) marks when reached, and the quota on the [provider account](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) behind it otherwise, shown as unbounded where none is authored.
C2634	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside it Factory reports the burn rate, units spent so far against the period in force, and when spending at that rate projects to exhaust the ceiling.
C2635	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The numbers are queries computed at read time over one span the factory owns and nobody authors, the same span [the digest](02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) reports over.
C2636	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Throughput is releases per service, gate rejection rate is rejects over firings per gate row, and rework rate is items with more than one [attempt](../03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) at any [stage](../01-one-pipeline.md) over items reaching a release.
C2637	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Cost per feature is what the intent and its items spent on models, and nothing of the substrate.
C2638	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Where the owner has authored a rate for every kind of unit of every model a feature ran on, it is one converted total across models and time; where one is missing, the figure is labelled units per intent, per model version, and the screen says the factory does not measure cost.
C2639	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Beside cost per feature Factory shows each intent's [outcome](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md): the [acceptance round](../02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md)'s verdict on the intended effect the requester confirmed, the before-and-after report rate, or nothing where the factory raised the intent, absence being the answer and not a gap.
C2640	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	It counts neither the [environment per candidate](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) nor the [full fleet a release keeps](../08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) while a window could return to it, which is the largest cost a rollout has and lands on hosting the owner pays for outside the factory.
C2641	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Environment-hours per item and instance-hours per release are reported beside it, per service and per item — the recorded facts [_An environment per candidate_](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) and [_What stands for a rollback_](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/02-what-stands-for-a-rollback.md) keep of that hosting, carrying the converted amount where the service record's hosting rate is in force and units only where none is authored.
C2642	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The [refused report count](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) is one exception and is a counter kept by the report store, the store the way in writes into, because the record a query would count is the write the rate exists to refuse.
C2643	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	[The auto-pass rate a threshold was authored at](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) is the other, read from the [policy version](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) that authored it rather than computed over the span, because a value recomputed over a moving span is not a fixed reference.
C2644	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The human's load is two reads of rows the [decision log](../../deferred.md) already holds.
C2645	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The rows waiting are [open events](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) with neither a close event nor an abandonment that name a duty or a human, which is the reading the decision's own section gives and needs no join against the item's stage.
C2646	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	How long a row waited is its close event's time against its open event's, over rows a human closed, grouped by the duty or human the open event names.
C2647	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The count of items stopped at dispatch is a third such read, over rows the same log holds: a hold whose second row has not been written, grouped by the cause the first names.
C2648	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The count of gates a resolved factor put a human at is a fourth, and it reads open events rather than holds: those whose [vector](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) records a factor resolved rather than valued, grouped by which factor and computed over the same span as the rest, so the number is a rate the owner can compare against the span before it.
C2649	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Two of them are the exception and have to be, because nothing else measures a [role prompt](../10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md): gate rejection rate and rework rate are also readable grouped by the role prompt version in force, which the [factor vector](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) on each decision names, so a version that made things worse is visible as two rates side by side.
C2650	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Not stage definition: the stages are the factory's own, and a role prompt and a skill are read here rather than authored, [a gate](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md) being where a version is decided and a [safeguard](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) (9) all an owner writes.
C2651	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	That gate's row is decided here as well.
C2652	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	It belongs to no item, as the withdrawals and endings [_Actions at each gate_](../03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md) lists beside it do not: its row names a version and no item at all, so Work has nowhere to put it, one item being one timeline, and this screen already shows the version in force beside it.
C2653	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The three actions are the row's own, Approve, Reject with feedback and [Edit in place](../03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md), and reading a version here while deciding it somewhere else is what would leave the gate that reaches a human for every role prompt version, on any threshold an owner would author, with nowhere to decide it.
C2654	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	**People** — humans, the duties each holds, who gates what, who does user acceptance testing (7), who lent a credential [_the fleet_](../10-fleet/README.md) runs on, and who installed [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md), those last two being what routing needs where no duty owns the row.
C2655	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The duties are also what a [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) routes on, there being no rotation to declare beside them.
C2656	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Declared, not enforced: the model routes work today, and is the [seam](../../deferred.md) authentication attaches to later.
C2657	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The one field here the factory does enforce is the [_spend ceiling_](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) an owner may author beside a lent credential, a limit on what is spent through it that holds work when reached.
C2658	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Beside the credential the declaration also holds whether the account is a person's own or an organisation's, and a rate an owner may author per kind of unit the provider returns, per model version and effort, which Factory converts into cost per feature and reports there rather than here.
C2659	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Both are read off the declaration onto each [agent run record](../10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) at the run, with the lender's per-person key, so a declaration edited later changes no past record.
C2660	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A People row may also hold no duty and lend no credential: a row added only so a human can read the four screens without gating, approving, or otherwise acting anywhere.
C2661	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Every acting call at Work, Ops, Factory, and People refuses that row rather than each screen checking it on its own, and the row reads all four.
C2662	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Until [seam 5](../../deferred.md#security-comes-last) is enforced its name is claimed the way every other row's is, which the [actor](../../deferred.md#security-comes-last) field already records.
C2663	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Nothing enforces the declaration, and every write to it is still on the chain.
C2664	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A write here, other than to the mapping below, appends a [policy version](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) to the [log](../../deferred.md) with People as caller, as a write at Factory does.
C2665	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The version names, per key, the duties that key holds, the credential it lent, and the ceiling and rates on it, by key and never by name.
C2666	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	So the mapping stays outside [the chain](../../deferred.md#security-comes-last) and an erasure still deletes it alone.
C2667	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	The declaration stays what every mechanism reads, the version being the trail's copy, and the write takes the order [_One shape across all of them_](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) gives an owner's write: the version first, the declaration second, and the factory's start re-deriving the declaration from the newest version.
C2668	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A [decision](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md)'s open event names the version in force at the firing.
C2669	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	For a close event, an acknowledgement, a [page event](../08-operations/07-pages.md), or a [withdrawal](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md)'s row, the roster in force is the newest version before that row in the chain.
C2670	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	So whether the actor held the row's duty at the close, whether a second holder existed when the self-approval refusal was evaluated, and whether a withdrawal was routed away from its author are each answered from the chain, as [_Traceability_](../../what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md) requires.
C2671	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Removing a duty's second holder, closing one's own row, and restoring the holder leaves three chained rows where it left none.
C2672	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	A row's name is what an actor or a [page event](../08-operations/07-pages.md) resolves to and never what either stores: from the first record each holds an opaque per-person key instead, and this declaration is the one place that key maps to a name, kept outside [the chain](../../deferred.md#security-comes-last) so it can be changed independently of it.
C2673	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	built	Erasing a person deletes the mapping and nothing else: the key stands on every record it was ever written to, so the chain, its links, and its counts are undisturbed, and what those records name is gone.
C2674	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	The deletion is refused, and the refusal recorded, while a [legal hold](../09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md) reaches any record that key is written on, since the mapping is what a hold over a decision preserves.
C2675	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md	unbuilt M2	Every screen resolves a name through this mapping rather than holding one, and the mapping is therefore the one record [the erasure list](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) must reach for an identified human, beside a redaction's own targets.
C2676	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	Everything on [the four screens](01-work-ops-factory-people.md) serves whoever holds a duty from [the owner's list](../../what-humans-do.md), however many people that is.
C2677	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	stated	Duties 4 and 5 — report a bug, complain — belong to end users, who never open this product.
C2678	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	The way in is thin and embedded in the deployed software; what they send is a [_report_](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), and it appears in Work under the intent it was grouped into rather than as a timeline of its own.
C2679	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	When the factory is working, there is nothing to do and the screens are empty.
C2680	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	Empty must not be mistaken for broken: the home view at zero shows a digest of what the factory shipped, decided, and auto-approved while nobody was looking.
C2681	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	So the home view also reads every [last check](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) record, the record each of the components below overwrites every pass, carrying the interval that pass runs on.
C2682	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	It shows a row for each one past the interval it names with a further pass owed, whatever wrote it.
C2683	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	A [readiness reading](01-work-ops-factory-people.md) is a third case that reads the same at zero: nothing has stopped, so a fleet not yet complete shows a row there before anything is wrong, and every install carries it until the fleet is.
C2684	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	Every one is read, because they are not one per component: the [health monitor](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) writes one per service; the [deployer](../08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) one per target of a persistent [environment](../05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), which is production and the ones a customer defines and never a candidate's, and one for [the platform](../05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md); and [_Drift detection_](../08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) one per production target.
C2685	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	Five are single: the [notifier](../08-operations/07-pages.md)'s, one each for the pass over [the constraints in force](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md), the pass over [the advisory feed](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md), and the pass over [the deprecation list](../07-contracts/08-deprecation.md), and [dispatch](../02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md)'s for the pass that argues [a fleet proposal](../10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md).
C2686	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	Every one and not the newest of a class, because that aggregation is the one that hides a partial stop.
C2687	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	So each record is read against its own interval and nothing is aggregated over a class at all, for the reason [_current release_](../06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) already refuses to let one target report for the rest: one member of a class may not stand for the class.
C2688	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	The row names what wrote it and what it checks, as in "the health monitor has not checked *payments* since 04:10", rather than showing a duration, so nothing on the page asks the reader to know how to read a number.
C2689	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	Each record is read against the client's clock and re-read while the view is open, over the subscription [_The screens as software_](03-the-screens-as-software.md) gives the client, so a record nothing overwrites crosses its interval on an open screen without a reload.
C2690	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	Where that subscription drops, the rows give way to the disconnected state and no age is shown: a client that went on rendering ages from a store it could no longer reach would read healthier the longer it stood, which is the reading the rows exist to prevent.
C2691	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	The rows are on the view whether or not it is at zero, and the digest is the part that appears only at zero: a stopped [notifier](../08-operations/07-pages.md) is exactly what leaves gates opening and rows arriving, so a signal shown only on an empty page would be withheld in the case it was written for.
C2692	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	Gates and escalations leave the product by mail or chat.
C2693	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	built	The same rule holds inside the product: a screen already open learns that a record changed from the subscription [_The screens as software_](03-the-screens-as-software.md) gives the client, never from a reload, so a board is true as long as it is connected and says so when it is not.
C2694	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md	unbuilt M8	A [_page_](../08-operations/07-pages.md) is the third channel and the narrow one, for a wait where production is worse until a human ends it.
C2695	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	The four screens are one client, built and versioned with the factory and upgraded with it.
C2696	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	unbuilt M8	Versioning the client with the factory does not version the tab a human already has open: an upgrade is applied by whoever hosts the install, and a screen loaded before it goes on calling and rendering after it.
C2697	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	So every call the client makes carries the factory version it was built from, beside the principal below, and the factory refuses a call whose version is not its own.
C2698	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	The client renders that refusal as a required reload and never as a failed action, so a screen open across an upgrade is stopped by it rather than carried through it, and no record is rendered by a client built for a shape it was not written in.
C2699	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	unbuilt M8	An [Edit in place](../03-gates/03-actions-at-each-gate.md) the factory accepted before the upgrade is already a version in the chain and is not lost.
C2700	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	Every place a human can be on them is an address, which is an item, a decision, a service on an environment, or a constraint.
C2701	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	So a row the notifier delivers by mail or chat opens at the row and not at the home view, and going back returns to where the human was.
C2702	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	Reopening after a refresh shows what the store holds now rather than what the screen held, the screen holding nothing a record does not.
C2703	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	What the store holds now is also what an open screen holds: the client keeps one subscription per address the human is on, carrying every change to the records that address renders, so nothing on a screen is true only as of its load.
C2704	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	That is what makes [_push, not poll_](02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) hold inside the product as well as out of it: a gate row a sibling holder closed, a rollout whose rollback fired, and a hold dispatch lifted change on the screen before a human acts on them.
C2705	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	A subscription that drops puts the screen in a disconnected state: what it held stays readable and is marked stale, every action on it is refused until the subscription is re-established, and re-establishing it re-reads the address whole rather than resuming from what the screen held.
C2706	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	A verdict is written only against the open event the row was rendered from: on submit the client re-reads that open event, and where a close event or an abandonment has reached it since the row was drawn, the screen shows that state and sends nothing.
C2707	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	So the refusal [the log holds for a second close](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) is met at the screen, before a human has written into a row already decided or already ended.
C2708	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	Who a human is on them is what they declare at [_People_](01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C2709	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	The declaration is carried on every call as the principal that seam 5 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../../deferred.md) puts there, and nothing enforces it until that seam is built.
C2710	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	unbuilt M8	The client is where authentication attaches then, once, and no screen holds a session the store does not.
C2711	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	The screens are held to what the factory holds a product's screens to.
C2712	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	The [design system](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) the product ships with is the one the screens are built from, and each of the four declares a [state machine](../03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/04-the-screen-state-machine.md) the way every screen the factory builds does, held in the client's own build rather than in a spec version the factory never writes for itself.
C2713	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	Empty, loading and failed are among its states, as _Spec_ requires of every screen, and disconnected is a fourth the subscription above adds; each of the four screens declares all of them, the client being exempt from nothing it holds a product to.
C2714	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	The predicates [a run on a candidate environment](../05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md) can decide are decided over each of those declared states: a contrast floor, a name on every control, a focus order, a target size.
C2715	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	Every time shown is a record's own, written in UTC where the record was written, and rendered in the viewer's locale and zone by the client and by nothing else; the text is in one language until an owner supplies another, translation being content the product ships and not a record.
C2716	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	built	A calendar value a human authors, an hour of day or a date, is stored with the time zone it was authored in, named as the IANA time zone database names one and sent by the client beside the value, and every reader of the value computes in that zone and in no other.
C2717	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	unbuilt M8	Five fields are such values: the [hours a service pages within](../08-operations/07-pages.md), the date a [constraint](../02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) binds from, its review date, the zone a constraint's deadline is counted in, and the date a [spend ceiling](../10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md)'s period starts from.
C2718	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/03-the-screens-as-software.md	unbuilt M8	What is computed from one is an instant, written in UTC like every other time and rendered in the viewer's zone like every other time, so two holders in two zones read one window and one deadline.
C2719	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	built	The Factory screen reports two rates side by side: how much the factory auto-approved, and how often a human later undid it (10) or the factory rolled it back.
C2720	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	unbuilt M8	How much autonomy to allow is decided from the pair, and every outcome the second of them counts is one the [risk score](../04-risk-score/README.md) learns from.
C2721	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	unbuilt M8	The [risk threshold](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) is authored against the pair and this number together — the score [refuses to loosen it on cost](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) because the owner authors what human review is worth, and a wait per duty is the number they author it with.
C2722	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	built	Two more numbers sit beside the pair, because the pair says how much the factory auto-approved and never whether the number it auto-approved on ranks anything.
C2723	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	built	That number is the one the [risk score](../04-risk-score/README.md)'s formula returns and the one a threshold is compared against.
C2724	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	built	The two are the realized auto-pass rate at each threshold in force against the rate [recorded on the policy version that set it](../09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), and the share of [held-out](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) releases whose windows failed within each band of that number, each band with the count of resolved held-out windows behind it.
C2725	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	built	Both are shown per [factor set](../04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md), the scale the threshold is read on being fitted per set.
C2726	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	unbuilt M8	The same pair per human, beside how long each row was open in front of them, is what says whether the review is worth anything, and it is reported split by what put the human at the row.
C2727	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	unbuilt M8	A [review-sampled](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) row is a change the factory itself would have auto-passed, where a threshold sent a human what the score put above the line and the auto-pass rate is over what fell below it.
C2728	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	unbuilt M8	How many such rows a duty gets is the [review sample rate](../09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) an owner authors (8), so a rate authored at zero buys the human time back and leaves this screen nothing to say about that duty.
C2729	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md	unbuilt M8	Beside the pair sit the rows each human [referred](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) and the rows each rejected, the rejections split by what put the human at the row and by how each resolved, on the terms [_How it learns_](../04-risk-score/02-how-it-learns.md) reads them: the re-authored version approved having changed what the rejection named, approved unchanged, rejected again, or the item reaching the attempt limit.
C2730	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	Factory reports the [page](../08-operations/07-pages.md) channel beside the human's load, from the page events [the log](../../deferred.md) already holds and over the same factory-owned span the rest of its numbers use.
C2731	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	Four numbers: pages per service and per named human, the share unacknowledged when they widened, the interval from acknowledged to answered, and how many pages a human fired at [Ops](01-work-ops-factory-people.md) on their own judgment.
C2732	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	These are read the way the [auto-approve and undo pair](04-what-the-factory-auto-approved-and-what-was-undone.md) is read: numbers nothing decides on, that an owner argues with.
C2733	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	Nothing here fires a page, narrows [the condition](../08-operations/07-pages.md), or changes [the hours a service pages within](../08-operations/07-pages.md), each of which is authored or decided where it is defined.
C2734	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	Beside them the screen reports what reached whom.
C2735	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	unbuilt M8	The human's load says how long the factory stood waiting on a human, which is a [close event](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md)'s time against its open event's, and that interval contains a delivery nobody has ever looked at.
C2736	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	So it is reported split at the first delivery the transport accepted, which the [delivery record](../08-operations/07-pages.md) names: the part before the split is the channel's and the part after it is the human's.
C2737	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	unbuilt M8	A row no delivery was ever accepted for is reported as a fault of the channel and against nobody.
C2738	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	built	The part after the split is split once more, at the first [acknowledgement](../03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) where the row is a decision one holder acknowledged: before it the wait was a shared duty's, with the row in front of every holder and taken by none, and after it one human's.
C2739	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/05-the-page-channel-and-what-reached-a-human.md	unbuilt M8	The counts are computed over the one factory-owned span like the rest, so an owner who suspects the channel changed reads one number over both halves of the change.
C2740	how-the-factory-works/11-screens/README.md	stated	One product, four screens.
C2741	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	**The factory is one process, and [the drift detector](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) is the second.**
C2742	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	Every component the inventory lists runs inside that process, with the two exceptions already stated: the way in ships inside each deployed service, and the drift detector is installed beside the factory so that it is not in it.
C2743	one-process.md	built	Exactly one instance of the factory runs, and what enforces it is the factory's own store, through a **lease** the store holds: one row naming the instance holding it, an expiry the holder renews on a pass of its own, and a number that rises by one at each acquisition.
C2744	one-process.md	built	A starting process acquires the lease where it is unheld or expired and takes the next number; while another holds it, the start fails rather than running beside the holder.
C2745	one-process.md	built	The merge queue reads master before it mints and the health monitor is the only thing that closes a [window](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), so two instances would mint one number twice and close one window twice, and no record afterwards would tell either from one instance doing it once.
C2746	one-process.md	built	Every write any component makes to the factory's own store carries the token of the instance making it, and the store refuses, in the same transaction as the write, one whose token is not the lease's current number.
C2747	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	A resumed instance finds its first write refused and stops, before a second release number or a second close of a window and not after.
C2748	one-process.md	built	The log's append carries one condition more: the row is written only where the head is still the one its chain field hashes over, in the same transaction, so two appenders reading one head produce one row and one refusal.
C2749	one-process.md	built	That holds from the first row for the reason [seam 2](deferred.md) gives for the chain field, since a fork could not afterwards be told from an edit.
C2750	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	What the token cannot reach is the far side of [seam 4](deferred.md): a deploy target checks no token, and a call begun under a live lease can finish after it lapsed.
C2751	one-process.md	built	The deployer writes the [deploy record](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md)'s row for a target before it calls that target and marks the target complete after, both writes carrying the token.
C2752	one-process.md	built	A stalled deployer's claim is refused, so it makes no call, and one that lapsed mid-call completes nothing.
C2753	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	A target running what no completed record names is the first comparison [_Drift detection_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) makes, a mismatch cleared by a human, so every effect a stale instance can have on a target is refused before it happens or read as a mismatch after.
C2754	one-process.md	built	Neither the lease nor the schema history below is a record of the graph, and [_Records and their writers_](records.md) lists no row for either: each is the store's account of itself.
C2755	one-process.md	built	A restart after a crash waits out the interval before it can acquire, and a deploy a lapsed instance finished is a page and a human's clearing rather than a refusal.
C2756	one-process.md	built	A component here is a pass or a loop of its own, and one can stop or fail on one service's work while the rest go on.
C2757	one-process.md	built	That is why [the health monitor](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) writes a [last check](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) per service rather than one for itself, and why [the home view](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/02-three-properties-every-screen-needs.md) reads each of those records against the interval that record carries rather than the newest of a class.
C2758	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	What one process does settle is the other end: the process stopping makes every last check stale at once, which is one state and not four, and nothing inside the factory is left to read it.
C2759	one-process.md	built	**Every component's restart is a read of its own records.**
C2760	one-process.md	built	The merge queue reads master at every start and writes the release record its own unfinished merge left owing.
C2761	one-process.md	built	[The deployer](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) completes or returns the [deploy records](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) no target has finished.
C2762	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	The health monitor's restart is the set of windows those same records left open: a window opens when a deploy record is written and closes at exactly one of four exits, so a start finds the open ones by reading records and never by keeping a list.
C2763	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	It evaluates each of those windows again.
C2764	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	An [exit](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) that a stop interrupted partway is finished by that second evaluation, because the close is the exit's last step under [_Tight integration_](what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md)'s rule for an event that writes more than one record.
C2765	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	The notifier's restart is the [delivery record](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md) it overwrites per waiting row, so a row still waiting is delivered again and one that stopped waiting is not.
C2766	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	Factory's restart reads the newest [policy version](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) per scope and rewrites any owner-authored field not already holding what that version names.
C2767	one-process.md	built	Every other component holds nothing between calls and resumes by being called again.
C2768	one-process.md	built	**The factory's own store keeps a schema history too, the same shape [_The store is a contract too_](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/09-the-store-is-a-contract-too.md) defines for a service's store.**
C2769	one-process.md	built	It is one row per change, naming the version that shipped it, the change's identity, a checksum of its text, and whether the change widened the store or removed something from it.
C2770	one-process.md	built	The store rule holds here, and its consumer is the version before, which a bad upgrade has to be able to put back.
C2771	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	So a breaking change to this store is the same sequence, spread over versions rather than items.
C2772	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	The version that ships it adds the new form beside the old, writes both, and copies every row the old form holds into the new, at every start until the drop, a row already copied being skipped.
C2773	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	The removal ships no earlier than the version after.
C2774	one-process.md	built	The promise carries a number and the number is one, in both directions: a version reads what the version before it wrote, and the version before reads and writes what this version wrote, until the version after that removes the old form.
C2775	one-process.md	built	A version's first start reads the history, and starts against one that holds, beyond the changes it declares, only rows from the version after it that widened the store.
C2776	one-process.md	built	It refuses to start where the history holds a removal it does not declare, a row from a version further ahead, or a change it declares that the history cannot honour, and a skipped version is not a supported upgrade.
C2777	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	So a bad upgrade is undone by starting the version before, with nothing restored: what the bad version wrote is in a form the version before reads, and the rows the version before writes meanwhile are reached by the copy when the newer version starts again.
C2778	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	The upgrade is the [install event](deferred.md) the log records, and the row names the changes the first-start step applied.
C2779	one-process.md	built	Where a change cannot take that sequence, the version declares it as a one-way step, a removal with no widening before it, and a snapshot stands for the version before.
C2780	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	Before the first-start step applies a removal, the drop or a one-way step, it takes a snapshot of the factory's own store, verifies it, and names it on the install-event row beside the change, the treatment [_A schema change_](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md) gives a service's store before a destructive change.
C2781	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	A snapshot it cannot take and verify is an upgrade not performed: no change is applied and the version refuses to start, the refusal a history mismatch already takes.
C2782	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	The snapshot is kept on the store's own hosting, so the install event says where what a removal destroyed can still be read.
C2783	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	It covers the factory's own store and not the whole [recovery unit](what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md), since an upgrade touches nothing else in the unit.
C2784	one-process.md	unbuilt M7	That is why the sequence above is the ordinary case, and a version declares a one-way step only where no widening can carry the change.
C2785	records.md	unbuilt M14	[_Tight integration_](what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md) states the rule this file inventories: every record in the graph has one writer, the single component that writes it, and where two writers reach one record the split between them is a seam declared in the section that owns the record.
C2786	records.md	unbuilt M14	Every seam row below says what the split is on: a field of the record, an event that writes it, or the kind the record was created as.
C2787	records.md	unbuilt M14	The writers are [intake](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md), [the report store](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), [decomposition](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), [dispatch](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), [the artifact store](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), [the log](deferred.md), [the merge queue](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md), [the builder](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), [the deployer](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), [the health monitor](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md), [the drift detector](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md), [the notifier](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md), and the screens an owner writes from, which are [Work, Ops, Factory and People](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C2788	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [intent](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) | intake | three sources call it — an owner's request, [the grouper](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), and a [detector](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md). The factory calls it at each round of [the interview](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) and at the [acceptance round](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) that follows production, [decomposition](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) calls it to record how many times that intent has been decomposed again, Work calls it to end the intent, and a named human at Ops calls it to ask for a [rollback](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md)'s revert |
C2789	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [question](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) | intake | the factory calls it to ask, Work calls it to write the answer onto the question already asked |
C2790	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [requirement](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) | intake | the factory calls it at the [interview](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md)'s confirming round, one record per statement of what the requester confirmed, and where the factory raised the intent itself, once per statement it enumerates from the evidence instead; at a reopened interview's confirming round the same call also writes the supersession of every requirement of the earlier reading that the new reading does not restate unchanged; decomposition calls it to mark one it judged unanswerable, with the reason, and to write, per item answering a share of one, the requirement derived for that share, pointing at the one it derives from |
C2791	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [constraint](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) | intake | an owner at Factory or at Work, and the factory itself at its first start after an upgrade that changed the [design system](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) it ships |
C2792	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [report](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) | the report store | called by [the way in](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), which the factory ships inside every deployed service; the store is also the only thing that may remove a report |
C2793	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [redaction](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) | Factory | one writer, the same as a [safeguard](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), a [halt](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) and a [legal hold](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md) have; an owner performs one erasure as one action there. The bytes are destroyed by each target's own writer — the report store, intake, and the artifact store — each reading the redactions naming records it writes, on a pass of its own, and writing no redaction |
C2794	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [erasure list](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) | the report store | outside the graph and the [recovery unit](what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md); Factory calls it at a redaction and People at a mapping deletion, the row landing before the redaction or the deletion; the report store, intake, the artifact store and the People mapping each replay the rows naming their own records after a restore; the store retires a row past the [backup retention](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/02-retention.md) |
C2795	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [item](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) | decomposition and dispatch | seam: the event. Every other write is dispatch's: each [stage](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) calls it as it reports a transition, Work calls it to set the priority and to end an item, and Ops calls it to end a [revert item](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) that [a mark](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) has made unnecessary |
C2796	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [service record](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) | decomposition, an owner at Factory, and the deployer | seam: the field.
