C2824	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [analysis window](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) | the health monitor | opened when the deploy record is written, closed once at exactly one of four exits |
C2825	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [incident](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md) | the health monitor | written at the [crossing](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), against the release running on that production environment |
C2826	records.md	built	| [last check](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) | eight components, each writing its own | the health monitor writes one per [service](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), the deployer one per target of a [persistent environment](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) and one for [the platform](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md), the drift detector one per production target, and the notifier a single one for itself, and the pass over [the constraints in force](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md), the pass over [the advisory feed](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/03-advisories.md), and the pass over [the deprecation list](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md) a single one each, and [dispatch](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) a single one for the pass that argues [a fleet proposal](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) |
C2827	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [the mark that a rollback was not caused by the release](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) | Ops | written once, by the named human who marks it |
C2828	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [mismatch](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md), and [the head of the log's chain](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) that the detector records on each pass | the drift detector | the detector keeps both in a store of its own, which no factory component may write; a mismatch is cleared at the detector and never from Ops |
C2829	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [safeguard](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md), and the [withdrawal](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md) that ends one | Factory | the safeguard has [five readers](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) and this one writer. A withdrawal is a second record naming the first, and it is not in force until the [gate that decides it](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md) approves it |
C2830	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [halt](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md), and the [withdrawal](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md) that ends one | Factory | its subject is the factory, it is read by the gate component and by the merge queue, and it is never edited. A withdrawal is a second record naming the first, and it is not in force until the [gate that decides it](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/11-a-halts-withdrawal.md) approves it |
C2831	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [legal hold](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/03-what-is-not-in-it/03-a-legal-hold.md), and the withdrawal that ends one | Factory | one writer, the same as a [safeguard](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) and a [halt](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/04-stopping-the-factory.md) have. A withdrawal is a second record naming the first, and it is not in force until a gate row routed away from its actor approves it, which is the treatment [_A safeguard's withdrawal_](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/10-a-safeguards-withdrawal.md) already gets |
C2832	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [factory-wide settings record](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) | Factory | it exists before any [project](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) does, and a factory runs whether or not an owner ever opens it; intake also calls it, on the arrival of a records-retention [constraint](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md), to set the [retention floor](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) |
C2833	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [fleet entry](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) | an owner at Factory | no component in [the pipeline](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) writes back to it |
C2834	records.md	built	| [agent run record](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) | the component that performed the run | one per run of any agent — a stage, an interview round, [the grouper](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), an [evaluation-set run](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md), or a [decomposition](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md); the counterpart to the [input manifest](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), naming what was sent, where, by what model and words, on whose account, and for what; written once and never rewritten |
C2835	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [fleet proposal](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) | the install's first-start step, dispatch, and an owner | seam: the event and the field. The proposal a version's first start writes is [the install's first-start step](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md)'s alone, at an install's first start or at an upgrade's first start that added a stage; every other proposal is written by [dispatch](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), performing the run of [the role that argues one](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md). Either way the writer of the proposal never writes its **disposition** — open, accepted, or dismissed — and an owner writes the disposition and never a proposal |
C2836	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [evaluation-set result](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md) | dispatch | written by the component that performed the run, keyed by the [model version and effort](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) it ran at, the [role prompt version and skill versions](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) it ran under, and the version of the [evaluation set](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md) it ran; what the run spent is on its [agent run record](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) instead |
C2837	records.md	unbuilt M14	| [People declaration](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) | an owner at People | nothing enforces what it declares, except the [spend ceiling](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/08-a-spend-ceiling.md) an owner may author beside a credential somebody has lent the factory; every write to it other than the key-to-name mapping appends a [policy version](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) with People as caller, and the mapping alone stays outside the chain |
C2838	records.md	unbuilt M14	**Some things the document names are not records, and each says so where it is defined.**
C2839	records.md	unbuilt M14	A [candidate](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md) is an item plus the build in force for it, and [a rollback](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) is a deploy event whose every field is on the deploy record.
C2840	records.md	unbuilt M14	[The merge queue](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) is a component, its membership and its order read out of records that already hold them.
C2841	records.md	unbuilt M14	A [deploy target](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) is a field of the environment, and which of them a service runs on is a field of the service record.
C2842	records.md	unbuilt M14	The commit, the set of packages a build resolved, the [design-system record named by a build in a project with screens](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), [what a run on a candidate environment produced](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), and [the notice file produced from the resolved set](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) are fields of the build.
C2843	records.md	unbuilt M14	So are [the shipped-bundle identity of the release that made it](how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) and what the build's own process decided before any environment existed.
C2844	records.md	unbuilt M14	A [contract element](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/01-two-versioned-things.md) is part of the form the contract version carries, and the [list of consumers a marked element waits on](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/08-deprecation.md) is a query.
C2845	records.md	unbuilt M14	A [screen](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/04-the-screen-state-machine.md) is the chain of state machines superseding one another, and [the encoding that decides a criterion](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) is code in the build.
C2846	records.md	unbuilt M14	A [design system](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) is a constraint whose reach is one project, and [what it contains](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) is a field of that constraint record, derived when the record is written; an [evaluation set](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/02-a-model-under-a-name.md) is content the product ships.
C2847	records.md	unbuilt M14	The [provider account](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) is not one either, the credential's name being the whole of what the factory holds.
C2848	records.md	unbuilt M14	[Current release](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md), [last known-good release and a rollback's target](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md), [the consumer contracts in force](how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md), [the criteria in force for a build](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/02-in-force-and-withdrawal.md), [a release's authorship rollup](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md), and [an intent partly delivered](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/04-when-an-intents-items-do-not-all-ship.md) are queries over records that exist, and nothing writes any of them.
C2849	what-humans-do.md	stated	The factory does everything else.
C2850	what-humans-do.md	built	It is a list of duties, not of people: an owner may hold all twelve or delegate any of them, and [_People_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) is where each attaches to a named human — a designer holding (2), (6), and (11), a compliance officer holding (2) and a safeguard (9) over a regulated area.
C2851	what-humans-do.md	built	Whoever holds a duty gets its gate rows and its user acceptance testing assignments (7) in their own view of [_Work_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C2852	what-humans-do.md	built	Writing the [People declaration](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) that attaches each duty to a human is the owner's own write and not a thirteenth duty: it distributes the twelve, and a duty over it would be one of the twelve deciding who holds the twelve.
C2853	what-humans-do.md	built	Nothing enforces it, and nothing has to — a [_page_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md) or a gate row with no holder recorded widens to the owner, the person who would have written the row.
C2854	what-humans-do.md	built	That write is the residue segregation leaves: the rule in [_Where a gate is, and what decides it_](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md) stops a human approving a version they authored, and no rule reads the write that decided who holds a row's duty.
C2855	what-humans-do.md	built	What bounds that is [the log](deferred.md)'s chain: each such write appends a [policy version](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) naming who holds what, so the roster in force at any row is read from the log rather than from today's declaration.
C2856	what-humans-do.md	built	A holder removed so that they may approve one row, then restored, leaves one version before that row's close and another after it.
C2857	what-humans-do.md	built	What falls outside is substrate rather than work, and the design names three: hosting the factory, installing [_Drift detection_](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) beside it, and composing [_The fleet_](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md) — the entries the factory dispatches against and the credentials they run on.
C2858	what-humans-do.md	built	The third covers composing an entry as well as lending a credential, because writing a model, an [effort](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md), a [role](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), and a [scope](how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) onto an entry is a write the factory never makes and no more one of the twelve than supplying the credential is.
C2859	what-humans-do.md	built	It also covers reading a [_fleet proposal_](how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/07-a-fleet-proposal.md) and giving it a disposition — the same obligation from the other side: the factory argues for a fleet, the owner decides, and deciding is substrate because writing is.
C2860	what-humans-do.md	built	Each is still an obligation, and nothing here scores one — least of all a proposal, which waits at [_Factory_](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) with no [page](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/07-pages.md) and no number saying how long it has waited.
C2861	what-humans-do.md	built	Upgrading the factory is part of hosting it, not a fourth: a self-hosted product is brought up to date by whoever runs it — the same person, the same kind of obligation.
C2862	what-humans-do.md	built	Request features.
C2863	what-humans-do.md	built	Supply constraints.
C2864	what-humans-do.md	built	A constraint names what it binds — the factory, a [project](how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md), an area, or one intent — and binds every item inside that from then on.
C2865	what-humans-do.md	built	A constraint with one of the three widest reaches is permanent like the rules (8): the factory works inside it and never gets out.
C2866	what-humans-do.md	stated	Laws and regulations are permanent constraints; so is a **design system** — the reusable decisions every screen is built from, its tokens, its components and the states each may be in, and the rules for using them, neither artwork nor the tool the artwork is drawn in — and an owner supplies one per project or picks from the ones the factory ships with.
C2867	what-humans-do.md	built	The narrowest reach is one intent: the constraint arrives with a single request and binds every item [decomposed](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) from it, a screen design among them — every item, because one request yielding four binds all four.
C2868	what-humans-do.md	built	Nothing removes a constraint on its own: it binds until withdrawn, so an owner who never withdraws one has the factory building against it years later.
C2869	what-humans-do.md	unbuilt M1	What a constraint may carry beyond its reach — a date it binds from, a date it is to be reviewed by, and a period the factory's own conduct is bound to once a trigger occurs, the shape a law takes — is set out in [_Constraints and the design system_](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md).
C2870	what-humans-do.md	built	Answer the factory's [interview](how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md), for as many rounds as it asks, until the intent is refined — and one round more once every item has shipped, saying whether what shipped is what was asked for.
C2871	what-humans-do.md	stated	Report bugs.
C2872	what-humans-do.md	stated	Complain ("this button is too slow").
C2873	what-humans-do.md	built	Confirm the [acceptance criteria](how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) are the right ones.
C2874	what-humans-do.md	built	Perform user acceptance testing (UAT).
C2875	what-humans-do.md	built	Author gate policy and risk thresholds.
C2876	what-humans-do.md	unbuilt M1	The duty is one line here and eleven parameters in [_Gate policy_](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/README.md), which is the set.
C2877	what-humans-do.md	unbuilt M1	None has to be authored for the factory to run: what an owner leaves alone, the score supplies.
C2878	what-humans-do.md	built	Add a safeguard — a gate held always-on, or a parameter narrowed — on any subject [_A safeguard is a record_](how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) names — a stage, a service, a project, an area, a contract element, gate policy's own list, or the drift detector's last check.
C2879	what-humans-do.md	unbuilt M1	The subject follows the mechanism the safeguard binds, which is why a service is among them: the [window limit](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) and the [analysis window](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md)'s parameters are per service, and so is an explicit [health threshold](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md).
C2880	what-humans-do.md	built	An **area** is an owner-declared grouping of the software — coarser than a file, free to cut across services — and names "the payments path" wherever a safeguard, a score factor, or a gate an owner added back has to be narrower than a project.
C2881	what-humans-do.md	built	An area lies inside one area or one project, so declared areas form a chain rooted at a project, and a safeguard drawn anywhere on that chain reaches the work below it.
C2882	what-humans-do.md	built	Undo a change after it shipped — one the factory auto-approved: a [rollback](how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) while the [build it would return to](how-the-factory-works/08-operations/03-overlapping-windows.md) is still running, a revert after.
C2883	what-humans-do.md	built	Help author a spec or an implementation plan the factory cannot produce properly on its own — up to writing it together with the factory.
C2884	what-humans-do.md	built	Take over issues the factory cannot fix on its own.
C2885	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	stated	**Tight integration is the first of the two properties the product is built around.**
C2886	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	Intent, item, [spec](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md), [build](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), [gate decision](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/01-where-a-gate-is-and-what-decides-it.md), deploy, [incident](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/06-incidents.md), and score are records in one graph, linked to each other.
C2887	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	What one graph does is act on it, with no human copying a fact from one system to another: what a consumer assumes is [derived from its build](../how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) rather than entered by hand, [the health monitor](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) finding a problem [after its window closed](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/04-after-the-analysis-window.md) writes an intent at the start of [the pipeline](../how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), and the score learns from the same log that serves as [the audit trail](../deferred.md).
C2888	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	**One record has one writer, and a boundary between two is declared.**
C2889	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	Every record in the graph has one **writer**, the single component that writes it, and no second component writes it.
C2890	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	Where two genuinely have to reach one record, whether an owner and a component or two components, the writing is split between them, and that split is a [seam](../deferred.md) stated in the section that owns the record.
C2891	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	A split is on a field of the record, on the event that writes it, or on the kind the record was created as.
C2892	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	[_Components and what they call_](../components.md) is the same list from the other side: a row per component, what it may call, and the rule that an edge not in a row does not exist.
C2893	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	built	**Every record in the graph carries a format version, not only the log's.**
C2894	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	A record that a later version updates moves its version only when the whole record is rewritten to the new shape, and that rewrite is part of the migration that performs it.
C2895	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	**An event that writes more than one record has a durable step order, and every step after the first is keyed so that writing it again writes nothing.**
C2896	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	[_The merge queue_](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) states the construction for the fast-forward and the release record, and it is the construction every such event takes.
C2897	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	The order is chosen so that a stop leaves the event visibly owing rather than visibly done, which makes half-done and finished different states rather than the same one.
C2898	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	Three orders follow from it: [the analysis window](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md)'s close is the last step of every exit, an item's [stage](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/02-what-an-item-names.md) is the last step of the transition that moves it, and an [authored parameter](../how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md)'s field is written after the policy version that records it.
C2899	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	What says the event is unfinished is the last step's absence, or its disagreement with a step taken before it.
C2900	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	[_One process_](../one-process.md) holds the other half, every component's restart being a read of its own records, and that last-written step is what such a read looks for.
C2901	what-the-factory-does/01-tight-integration.md	unbuilt M13	The one exception is [_Drift detection_](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md), which compares two of those records against what the factory cannot rewrite: the production [deploy record](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) against what is actually running, and [the log](../deferred.md)'s chain against the head the detector recorded on its last pass, each record's chain field hashing its own payload together with the same field on the record before it.
C2902	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	**Traceability is the second**, the testable form of the first: every artifact links back to the intent that caused it and forward to what it produced, under the [policy](../how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/README.md) and score in force at the time.
C2903	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	Testable is what keeps it from being a discipline anyone has to maintain: it holds where a question is answered by following links, and fails where an answer has to be reconstructed.
C2904	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	Two records supply those links — the [intent](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) every item points back to, and the [release record](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md) linking the item, the build, the gate decisions, the [contract versions](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/03-what-a-build-is-called-and-when.md), and every deploy.
C2905	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	A third link runs the other way and does: the intent carries the reading the requester confirmed as a set of [requirements](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md), one statement each, and every [acceptance criterion](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) names the requirement it answers.
C2906	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	Which criteria answer a statement is then a query, and so is which statements no criterion answers, which is what an intent's completeness is read from.
C2907	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	[_Decomposition_](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) and [_Spec_](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) are where that second query is a rejection rather than a reading.
C2908	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	built	A decision read back against today's values is not the decision that was made, so each decision record names the [policy version](../how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) and the [score version](../how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) it was decided under, next to [the actor](../deferred.md) and for the same reason — no such field can be added to a history written without it.
C2909	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	built	Both versions are rows of the [log](../deferred.md) the decision is a row of, chained like it, so a version rewritten after the fact breaks the chain rather than silently moving what every decision naming it is read against.
C2910	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	built	What traceability rules out is any action that skips a record.
C2911	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	A human who wants different code [authors it upstream](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/04-what-a-gate-may-change.md) rather than patching one at a gate, an emergency is [approve now rather than skip](../how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), and undoing something already shipped is a [rollback](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) while its [control](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) is still running or a [revert item](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) after.
C2912	what-the-factory-does/02-traceability.md	unbuilt M13	Every shortcut is an item — that is what keeps the links unbroken.
C2913	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	A grouper — the component that reads the [report store](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) and decides which reports are one problem — finds four more saying the same thing and calls [intake](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/README.md) once for the group; intake writes one intent whose statement summarizes them and points at all five.
C2914	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The intent is unrefined, so [_the interview_](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/02-the-interview.md) asks the owner what "slow" is worth fixing to, each question a record and each round counted against [the attempt limit](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/05-the-attempt-limit.md) (8).
C2915	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The answer arrives, [decomposition](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) proposes two items — one per service — and the [Decomposition gate](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/01-decomposition.md) fires over the set, the score reading the [factors](../how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) that gate has — exposure being inapplicable above a build rather than unavailable, which is why the absence of one does not by itself put a human there — and putting a human at it or not.
C2916	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	[Dispatch](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md) matches the [stage](../how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) the item is at against a [fleet entry](../how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md) and puts a model in a [role](../how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) on it.
C2917	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The spec stage authors [acceptance criteria](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) in the six patterns, and the [Spec gate](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/README.md) is where a human confirms them (6).
C2918	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	Here that is certain rather than conditional: this intent was grouped from reports, and that source is [resolved and not weighed](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md) — a human whatever the formula returns, the [risk threshold](../how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/01-what-is-in-it.md) an owner authored reaching it not at all.
C2919	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	On an intent an owner raised, the same gate is the threshold's to decide.
C2920	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	[Plan](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/03-implementation-plan.md) and [tasks](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/04-tasks.md) follow, each an artifact, each a gate.
C2921	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	Implementation authors the code, the encodings of those criteria (an encoding being the code that decides one criterion against an observed run), and the [consumer contracts](../how-the-factory-works/07-contracts/06-what-a-consumer-declares.md) the factory derives from the build; the [Implementation gate](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md) decides over the item's first build, and the score computes its change factors from that build's diff against [master](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md).
C2922	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The candidate deploys to [an environment of its own](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md), composed from the [current release](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) of every service it depends on.
C2923	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The encodings run there and decide each criterion; a criterion that comes back both ways is [undecided](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/04-what-the-candidate-environment-decides/01-the-third-outcome.md) and rejects the way a failure does, where the disagreement appeared.
C2924	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	[The merge queue](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) re-verifies the candidate against the master it will actually merge into, and the [Merge to master gate](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/07-merge-to-master.md) reads that run — the criteria, the consumer contracts of every service consuming what this one publishes, and the [UAT](../what-humans-do.md) assignment (7) where a human holds one.
C2925	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The queue fast-forwards, writes the [release record](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md), and mints [the number](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md) with it.
C2926	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	[Deploy to production](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/08-deploy-to-production.md) is the last gate and has no Reject: the number already exists, so the actions are approve and hold.
C2927	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The [deployer](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) performs the deploy on the [strategy](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) the score picked, starts a [control](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) if that strategy keeps one, and writes the deploy record.
C2928	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	The [health monitor](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/01-the-health-monitor.md) opens an [analysis window](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md) over the deploy and compares the release against the control until the window closes at one of its four [exits](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md).
C2929	what-the-factory-does/03-one-change-end-to-end.md	unbuilt M13	Say it closes passed, the exit meaning the comparison ruled out a regression of the size worth catching: the control is torn down, the [per-author prior](../how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) of the model that wrote the change narrows, and the window's own parameters move on what it took to reach that exit.
C2930	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	Where a thing writes or keeps a record the factory has to be the only writer of, it is the factory's and this document describes it.
C2931	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	Where a thing stores bytes, runs a process, moves traffic, or counts requests, it comes from outside and the factory reaches it.
C2932	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	So nothing here reimplements a repository, a container runtime, a scheduler, or a store of numbers, and nothing outside decides anything the factory records.
C2933	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Names the repository on the [service record](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md), commits a candidate branch into it under one credential, and fast-forwards [master](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) at [the merge queue](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/03-the-merge-queue.md) under another, both resolved through seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../deferred.md)
C2934	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Calls [the builder](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) to build, in a process that holds nothing of the factory's and reaches only the sources the build resolves from, and decides a [criterion](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/02-spec/03-the-six-patterns.md) by running its encoding
C2935	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Reaches it only through the named operations of seam 4 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../deferred.md), which is where policy attaches later, and reads back each pass how many [candidate environments](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/02-an-environment-per-candidate/README.md) it holds against how many the records say
C2936	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Picks [a rollout strategy](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) the platform can perform, read from what the [environment record](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) declares per target, every deploy going [without a control](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) where none can, and names on the [deploy record](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/README.md) the strategy it performed
C2937	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Instruments the software it writes so that it emits them, computes the comparison over them, and records on each pass the newest time the store holds
C2938	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Names the artifact by digest on the [build](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md), and requires it reachable at least while its release could be a rollback's target — the floor [_Rollback_](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/06-rollback.md) states as a shape
C2939	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Reads it on a pass of its own and matches each entry against what every service's current release resolved, raising an intent per advisory that matches
C2940	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Reads a [fleet](../how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md) entry an owner wrote, and writes none back — where [what the agent is told](../how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) is a record on this side of the division, written by the factory alone
C2941	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	Everything on the factory's side of that division is what the rest of this document describes: the graph and the [append-only decision log](../deferred.md), [dispatch](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/05-dispatch.md), the [gate component](../how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md), the queue's ordering and [the release number](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/04-the-release-number.md), the score, [the analysis window](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/02-the-analysis-window.md), the drift detector, and the [four screens](../how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md).
C2942	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	A host requirement crosses that division: the graph, the [report store](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/02-reports.md), and git make one **recovery unit**, backed up and restored together to a single **recovery point**.
C2943	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	That the members landed at one point is checked rather than assumed: [_What the queue reads before it mints_](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md) reads master against the release records at every start, and a service whose members landed apart is held there until master is whole.
C2944	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	The store the health monitor reads stays outside the unit: it is read by time range and names nothing the graph has to hold first, so the ordering question never arises for it.
C2945	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Restoring it would also discard the release numbers production emitted while the factory was down, and the first mint after a restore reads those to skip the numbers the restore lost.
C2946	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	The drift detector's own store stays outside too, the owner's to keep separately or not at all, because a store no factory component may write is not the factory's to restore either.
C2947	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	A restore that leaves its recorded head ahead of the graph is the mismatch [_Drift detection_](../how-the-factory-works/08-operations/08-drift-detection.md) already names, cleared there by a human.
C2948	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	The start after a restore is the [install event](../deferred.md) the log already records, naming the actor who ran it.
C2949	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	The snapshot [_One process_](../one-process.md) has an upgrade's first start take before it removes anything from the factory's own store is not a recovery point of the unit: an upgrade touches that store alone, and what a restore to that snapshot costs is stated there.
C2950	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	A forge — one product that is repository host, review tool, pipeline, and registry at once — has parts on both sides of that division, which is why the factory neither adopts one nor rewrites one.
C2951	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Its other parts are records here — a review is a gate decision with an actor, a policy version, and a score version on it; what may reach master is the queue's exclusivity rather than a rule configured per repository; and what a [stage](../how-the-factory-works/01-one-pipeline.md) does is the pipeline.
C2952	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	[_Work_](../how-the-factory-works/11-screens/01-work-ops-factory-people.md) shows an item's artifacts on a timeline, so a human confirming criteria (6) or performing [UAT](../what-humans-do.md) (7) reads those and not code.
C2953	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	At the one gate whose artifact is the code, [_Implementation_](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/05-implementation/README.md), Work renders the build's diff against master, read from the repository and written nowhere: a diff is bytes the repository already holds, outside the division above, and rendering it is a read like the clone an agent makes.
C2954	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	Nothing administers a repository either — creating one and holding write access to it is part of hosting the factory, [substrate](../what-humans-do.md) rather than one of [the twelve duties](../what-humans-do.md).
C2955	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	An owner marks the service [provisioned](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md) once the repository exists with the factory holding write access and the service's store exists on every persistent environment, and until then an item on that service waits at dispatch and at both deploy gates rather than failing a stage against a repository that is not there.
C2956	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	Master's only inbound path being the queue rests on how the repository is administered, not on a record.
C2957	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	The factory holds two credentials per repository where the host can tell master from a branch, the shape seam 3 of [_Deferred, but not designed out_](../deferred.md) gives them: one that pushes a candidate branch, resolved to an agent's run and to the build runner's clone, and one that fast-forwards master, resolved to the queue alone.
C2958	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	So an implementation agent cannot write master with what it was handed, and what refuses it is the host's rule over master, which the owner records with [provisioned](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/README.md).
C2959	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	On a host that cannot restrict by branch the two are one credential, the field says so, and what refuses another push is who holds it; the factory refuses none itself.
C2960	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	What it reads is the evidence: [_What the queue reads before it mints_](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/05-what-the-queue-reads-before-it-mints.md) compares master against the release records at every start and before every mint, and a commit there that no build of an approved candidate names is one the queue did not make, told from its own unfinished merge by the records.
C2961	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	Such a commit holds the service's merges until a human returns master or accepts the commit, and an accepted one ships as a release naming no item, so the claim that what was verified is what ships is read off each release rather than off who holds write access.
C2962	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	On a repository where humans can also push, the exclusivity holds by convention, and what the factory can tell is whether it held.
C2963	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	[_The rollout strategy_](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md) states one half of that already — which strategies a service can take is bounded by where it runs — and the health monitor is the other half: numbers reaching no store the factory can read leave a pipeline that ships changes and measures none of them.
C2964	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	stated	The factory is for services of one shape: instances on a deploy target the deployer reaches, taking [organic traffic](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/02-the-rollout-strategy.md), emitting one record per request served, replaceable one at a time, returnable by shifting traffic.
C2965	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	A mobile or desktop application, a firmware image, a library, a CLI, and a scheduled pipeline are refused at [adoption](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/03-decomposition/01-a-service-that-already-exists.md) and at decomposition, the refusal stated with its reason: the operations half is the product, and a deliverable it cannot measure would be reported as shipped and measured.
C2966	what-the-factory-does/04-what-the-factory-does-not-build.md	unbuilt M13	So is a service whose store is not one per environment, for the reason [_A schema change_](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/05-the-deploy-record/01-a-schema-change.md) gives.
C2967	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	stated	**The licence** is one file, `LICENSE` at the root of the repository the product is built in, saying under what terms a customer may run, change, and redistribute it.
C2968	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	stated	The design states none of those terms — a licence is decided in that file and nowhere else — and does not restate what the licence allocates: warranty and liability between the customer and whoever builds the product — **the builder** from here on — are the licence's terms too.
C2969	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	Every such value was either authored by the customer (8) or supplied, and the [policy version](../how-the-factory-works/09-gate-policy/02-one-shape-across-all-of-them.md) and [score version](../how-the-factory-works/04-risk-score/01-factors-at-least.md) every decision names say which.
C2970	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	The authored one carries who wrote it, the supplied one is the score's, and both are chained rows of the [log](../deferred.md), so neither can be rewritten after the fact to move a value from one side to the other.
C2971	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	The model that wrote a change, the [effort](../how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/01-what-an-agent-runs-on.md) it ran at, and the credential it ran through are the customer's, written onto a [fleet entry](../how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/README.md) by the customer and never by the factory.
C2972	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	[What the agent was told](../how-the-factory-works/10-fleet/03-what-an-agent-is-told/README.md) is the builder's in its first version and the factory's in every version after, each decided at [a gate of its own](../how-the-factory-works/03-gates/07-what-particular-gates-decide/09-a-role-prompt-or-a-skill.md).
C2973	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	That claim rests on the shipped-bundle identity [_The product's release channel_](../deferred.md#the-products-release-channel) names: which version shipped an agent's words is on the record, not asserted after the fact.
C2974	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	The decision that shipped the change names its [actor](../deferred.md), a human or the score, and beside it whether that name was claimed or verified.
C2975	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	So each fact a liability question asks for is on a record with one writer, read from the log rather than argued: who chose the author, who wrote its instructions, who approved the change and under whose value, and whether that approval was of the approver's own work.
C2976	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	A fact resting on a claimed name reads as one.
C2977	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	stated	What the design does not decide is what a jurisdiction makes of that division — whether the builder of a product that ships software is the producer of the software it ships is a question of law, answered against the licence and per jurisdiction, by counsel rather than by a design document.
C2978	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	stated	The produced software's licence is the customer's own decision, authored as the [product licence](../how-the-factory-works/02-intent-into-items/01-intake/01-constraints-and-the-design-system.md) on each service record, and what the design owes a rights question over it is the same kind of fact the builder's liability gets.
C2979	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	unbuilt M13	Which licence each dependency shipped under is on the [notice file](../how-the-factory-works/05-environments/01-records-and-one-long-lived-branch.md) a release names, and who wrote each line that shipped is on that release's [authorship rollup](../how-the-factory-works/06-releases/02-the-release-record.md).
C2980	what-the-factory-does/05-what-the-customer-runs-it-under.md	stated	Whether that combination may lawfully be distributed is again a question of law, answered against the licence and per jurisdiction, by counsel rather than by this document.

C2982	what-the-factory-does/README.md	stated	A **fully autonomous software factory and operations**: it refines intent, produces the software, deploys it, monitors it, finds issues, and fixes bugs, on its own.
